# Getting Started | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#)

On this page

Getting Started
===============

Automation Blocks is an infinite collection of premade, one-click tools for any task in Adobe Premiere Pro and After Effects. With Automation Blocks, you can fully customize all the tools, and even create your own!

**Premiere Pro**

**After Effects**

Download & Installation[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#download--installation "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Automation Blocks is available at aescripts.com. You can download and install the free trial from the product page or install it using the [manager app](https://aescripts.com/learn/aescripts-aeplugins-manager-app/)
.

[Get Automation Blocks for Pr](https://aescripts.com/automation-blocks-for-premiere-pro/)

[Get Automation Blocks for Ae](https://aescripts.com/automation-blocks-for-after-effects/)

First Steps[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#first-steps "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Premiere Pro**

**After Effects**

Use the Existing Tools[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#use-the-existing-tools "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Automation Blocks comes with many ready to use tools, which are all listed in the library panel. If you hover over any of the tools in the library it shows a short description which will give you a good idea what the tool is actually doing:

![help in the Automation Blocks library](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolsHelpAndHelpTip.png)

Some tools are so easy that this short description is really all you need. But some of the more comprehensive tools also come with more documentation. To see this, just select a tool in the library, then click the "Help" button in the top right corner to open the in-depth documentation. You'll find all of these in-depth documentations in the sections [Pr Tools](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/overviewToolsPr)
 and [Ae Tools](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/overviewToolsAe)
.

Most tools in Automation Blocks come with a user interface. If a tool comes with a UI, this is indicated with a little dialog icon before the name of the tool:

![icon indicating that tool has a user interface](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/libraryUiIconExplanation.png)

Some tools have user interfaces in the Inputs panel of Automation Blocks. Others have dialogs which pop up when you run the tool.

If a tool has no user interface, it usually means that it performs its job without the need for any further input. In some rare cases, a tool expects you to modify the block code before using it (like connecting a particular layer property to a block). However, in these rare cases, this is clearly described in the tool help.

Join the Community[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#join-the-community "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Forum[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#forum "Direct link to heading")

If you need help with customizing an existing Automation Blocks script, want to share a script with the community, or if you have a questions, please post in the [Adobe Premiere Pro support forum](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro/ct-p/ct-premiere-pro)
 or the [Adobe After Effects support forum](https://community.adobe.com/t5/after-effects/ct-p/ct-after-effects?page=1&sort=latest_replies&filter=unresolved&lang=all&tabid=discussions)
. Your Adobe ID is already your user account for this forum, so you can start posting without any tedious registration. Just be sure to **mention _"Automation Blocks"_ in the subject** and add the **topic "_SDK_" in the Pr or "_Scripting_" in the Ae forum** to ensure that we see your post. We'll then do our best to answer your questions.

**Premiere Pro**[![post questions in the Premiere Pro forum](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/postAdobeForumPr.png)](https://community.adobe.com/t5/forums/postpage/board-id/premiere-pro)

**After Effects**[![post questions in the After Effects forum](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/postAdobeForumAe.png)](https://community.adobe.com/t5/forums/postpage/board-id/after-effects)

### Access Community Library[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#access-community-library "Direct link to heading")

We collect all Automation Blocks scripts that are shared in the community in a public folder, which you can access via Dropbox, Google Drive or GitHub.

**Premiere Pro**

*   [Community Library on Dropbox](https://www.dropbox.com/scl/fo/55q5ba1q262zr6j6zuc5t/h?rlkey=u5zukx7hthj0edmkj0t9zxojc&dl=0)
    
*   [Community Library on Google Drive](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/19hr4w7_jhnrrKKM_1ISUydsUjFcaXmx7?usp=sharing)
    
*   [Community Library on GitHub](https://github.com/mamoworld/automation-blocks-pr-community-library)
    

**After Effects**

*   [Community Library on Dropbox](https://www.dropbox.com/sh/1x8juwu30hf1p0o/AAA1nQmbqglqVu22bS5WEJPZa?dl=0)
    
*   [Community Library on Google Drive](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1-2VPXG_fsxyeNqVGBUEKI7eTd3e_n5qy?usp=sharing)
    
*   [Community Library on GitHub](https://github.com/mamoworld/automation-blocks-ae-community-library)
    

Just download the folder right into your Automation Blocks user library, restart Automation Blocks, and you're ready to go.

If you've created a script that you think is useful for the community, please [get in touch with us :-)](mailto:info@mamoworld.com)
. If you are using GitHub, you can also create a pull request.

#### Other Libraries[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#other-libraries "Direct link to heading")

The Community Library is managed and curated by mamoworld. Of course, you can create and publish your own Automation Blocks libraries independently. Get in touch and we are happy to list them here, too.

*   [Automation Blocks for Pr - tools by Better Editor](https://www.bettereditor.be/automation-blocks-for-premiere-pro/)
    
*   [Automation Blocks for Ae - tools by Onderk-Motion](https://github.com/onderk-motion/Automation-Blocks-Tools)
    

### Where is the User Library?[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#where-is-the-user-library "Direct link to heading")

To copy tools from the community library to your user library, you need to know where the latter is located on your system. By default the user library is a subfolder with name _"Automation Blocks Pr Library"_ (or _"Automation Blocks Ae Library"_) inside your user's _Documents_ folder. You can also access the library from Automation Blocks preferences:

![find Automation Blocks Ae Library folder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/findUserLibraryFolder.png)

After you copied new scripts to your user library, make sure to close and reopen the Automation Blocks panel, to force a reload of the library.

Customize & Write Your Own Tools[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#customize--write-your-own-tools "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

In Automation Blocks, you can fully customize all the tools, and even create your own! Instead of programming code, the tools are based on blocks - creating tools feels more like playing with Lego bricks than doing actual programming. It's when you start to customize the example scripts to do what you need, or even write your own scripts from scratch, that the magic really begins!

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/writeYourOwnToolsCreateBinsAndCompExample.gif)

If you want to become a true Automation hero, let's not waste any time: If you want to create tools for Premiere Pro, start with [Write Your First Custom Script with Automation Blocks](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps)
. To get started with writing your own block scripts in After Effects, watch the tutorial series [Write Your Custom Project Setup Script](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript)
 and [Write Your Custom Template Tool](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomTemplateTool)
.

*   [Download & Installation](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#download--installation)
    
*   [First Steps](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#first-steps)
    
*   [Use the Existing Tools](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#use-the-existing-tools)
    
*   [Join the Community](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#join-the-community)
    *   [Forum](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#forum)
        
    *   [Access Community Library](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#access-community-library)

# Ae Color | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeColor/#)

On this page

Ae Color
========

Color values returned by blocks[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeColor/#color-values-returned-by-blocks "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Blocks return label colors as hex strings (like #ff0000 for red), but color values of properties as a [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 of four numbers in the range 0 to 1 (representing, r,g,b,a so that `[1,0,0.5,1]` is 100% red, 0% green, 50% blue)

Specifying Color Values[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeColor/#specifying-color-values "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Blocks support time values in all of the following formats:

*   hex string (like #ff0000 or short form #f00)
*   a [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
     (array) of three numbers in the range 0 to 1 (representing, r,g,b, so `[1,0,0.5]` is 100% red, 0% green, 50% blue)
*   a color name like `red`, `sandstone`. In addition to the default label color names of Ae, css color names like `aliceblue` are also supported.

Convert Color[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeColor/#ae_color_converter "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_color_converter](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeColor/ae_color_converter.png)

With this block you can convert a color that is given in any format (name, hex string or array) into either the hex or the array format.

*   [Color values returned by blocks](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeColor/#color-values-returned-by-blocks)
    
*   [Specifying Color Values](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeColor/#specifying-color-values)
    
*   [Convert Color](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeColor/#ae_color_converter)

# Ae Keyframes | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#)

On this page

Ae Keyframes
============

The blocks in this category allow you to work with keyframes on [properties](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayerProperties)
 in After Effects.

Add Single Keyframe[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_set_value_at_time "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_set_value_at_time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeKeyframes/ae_set_value_at_time.png)

This block creates a single keyframe - you just need to specify the [property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayerProperties)
 on which the keyframe should be created, the [time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime)
 and the [value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayerProperties#ae_property_value_json)
 for the keyframe.

Add Keyframes[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_keyframes_apply "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_keyframes_apply](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeKeyframes/ae_keyframes_apply.png)

This block can create multiple keyframes on any keyframeable property - it is your keyframe copy and paste operation on steriods. If the property already has keyframes, it can even adjust them for you - if, for example, you add keyframes which move the layer 100 pixels downwards, then it can move all keyframes after the newly inserted ones 100 pixels down, too.

To use the block, you need to specify the following parameters

**keyframe list**: to tell your block script which keyframes you want to add, you can either us a [Static Keyframe List](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_keyframe_json_const)
 or a [Dynamic Keyframe List](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_keyframe_json_from_prop)
. By default, the _Add Keyframes_ block comes with a static keyframe list. If you want to use a dynamic one instead, just create a _Dynamic Keyframe List_ block and drag it on top of the static list to replace it.

**property**: choose the [Layer Property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayerProperties#ae_layer_properties_access)
 to which the keyframes should be applied.

**time**: the [time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime)
 where the first new keyframe should be located (the distance of the other keyframes depends on the keyframe data you provide). If you don't provide a value here, the keyframes are inserted at the time in the keyframe data (i.e. at the original time of the keyframes that you copied).

### Automatic Keyframe Adjustment[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#automatic-keyframe-adjustment "Direct link to heading")

The parameters "adjustment before" and "adjustment after" of the block allow you to adjust the existing keyframes to the newly inserted ones - or also vice versa! The first dropdown controls the behavior of the keyframes before the newly inserted ones, and the second dropdown controls the behavior of the keyframes after the newly inserted ones.

#### Adjust Keyframes before newly inserted ones[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#adjust-keyframes-before-newly-inserted-ones "Direct link to heading")

![Merge Options Keys before](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_keyframes_apply_merge_option.png)

Here you have the following options:

*   **none:** keeps all keyframes before the new ones exactly as they are and pastes the new keyframes without modifying them in any way.
    
*   **adjust keys before to new keys:** offsets all the keyframes before the new ones so that the last one ends up exactly at the value where the first newly inserted keyframe is. Example: say you already have two scale keyframes on your layer, which scale it from 20% to 50%, and now after that you want to add two keyframes which scale from 60% to 80%. This option will then change the first two keyframes to 30% and 60%, i.e. add 10 to them to ensure they end at 60%, which is exactly the value that the new inserted keyframes start with.
    
*   **adjust new keys to keys before:** This option adjusts the newly inserted keyframes so they start exactly where the last keyframe before the new ones ends. Example: If you already have two scale keyframes which animate the layer from 20% scale to 50% and you insert new keyframes with an animation from 60% to 80% after that, then those new keyframes will be changed to 50% and 70% to make sure they start exactly at the 50% where the animation of the existing keyframes ended.
    
*   **adjust new keys to keys before or value:** This option is the same as _adjust new keys to keys before_, except that it also adjusts the new keyframes if there are no existing keyframes before the new ones at all. In other words, this option makes sure that the newly inserted keyframes start exactly at the current value, no matter if the property is already keyframed or not.
    

#### Adjust Keyframes after newly inserted ones[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#adjust-keyframes-after-newly-inserted-ones "Direct link to heading")

![Merge Options Keys after](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_keyframes_apply_merge_option_2.png)

Here you have the following options:

*   **none:** keeps all keyframes before the new ones exactly as they are and pastes the new keyframes without modifying them in any way.
    
*   **adjust keys after to new keys:** offsets all the keyframes after the new ones so the first of them starts exactly at the value where the last newly inserted keyframe ends. Example: Say you already have two scale keyframes on your layer, which scale it from 20% to 50% and now you want to insert two keyframes which scale from 60% to 80% BEFORE those existing keys. This option will change the old keyframes to 80% and 110%, i.e. they will still scale up by 30%, but instead of starting from 20% they now start from 80%, where the newly inserted keyframes end.
    
*   **adjust new keys to keys after:** This option adjusts the newly inserted keyframes so they end exactly where the first keyframe after the new ones ends. Example: If you already have two scale keyframes which animate the layer from 60% scale to 80% and you insert new keyframes with an animation from 80% to 100% before that, then those new keyframes will be changed to 40% and 60% to make sure they end exactly at the 60% where the animation of the existing keyframes starts.
    
*   **adjust new keys to keys after or value:** This option is the same as _adjust new keys to keys after_, except that it also adjusts the new keyframes if there are no existing keyframes after the new ones at all. In other words, this option makes sure that the newly inserted keyframes end exactly at the current value, no matter if the property is already keyframed or not.
    

##### conflicting options

Note that the new keys can either be adjusted to fit seamlessly to the existing keys before, or to the existing keys after, but not both. If you choose both options at the same time, the latter one is ignored.

##### Seamless Animation

A very powerful combination for seamless animations is

*   **adjust new keys to keys before or value** combined with
*   **adjust keys after to new keys**

That way, the newly inserted keyframes and the existing keyframes after them are all shifted as needed to create a seamless animation which starts at the current position of the animation.

If the newly inserted keyframes should animate a layer into the scene, you typically want to preseve the position where the animation ends (and not where it starts). To achieve this, you can set _"adjustment before"_ to _"none"_ and _"adjustment after"_ to _"adjust new keys to keys after"_.

### Pasting Keys to Multiple Properties[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#pasting-keys-to-multiple-properties "Direct link to heading")

Instead of providing the block a single property and a single list of keyframes, you can also pass it lists of them.

Here, we paste keyframes on both the position and the scale of a layer:

![pasting keyframes on multiple properties](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_keyframes_apply_example_multiple_props.png)

As the orange arrows indicate, the first keyframe list is applied to the first property and the second keyframe list to the second property.

At first sight, this seems to be the same as if you simply used two copies of the _"add keyframes"_ block (the first to paste the position keyframes and the second to paste the scale keyframes). However, there is one important difference: The behavior of the **at time** parameter. If you give the block an entire list of keyframe lists, it will look at all of them and offset all keyframe times so that the very first one is at the given time. If this sounds strange, it's best explained with an example:

**Example**

Say you read keyframe data for both scale and position from a layer and the first scale keyframe is at 1s, and the first position keyframe at 2s. Now you paste those keys to another layer with the _at time_ parameter set to 5s. If you just paste the position keyframes alone, the first position keyframe will be at 5s. However, if you paste both scale and position at once, the block notices that the scale keyframes start 1s earlier than the position keyframes. So, it pastes the keyframes so that the first scale keyframe is at 5s, but the first position keyframes is at 6s.

Note that this behavior is identical to what After Effects does when you cut and paste keyframes manually in the user interface.

##### set value for keyframed property

If you want to create a single keyframe at the current time, you can also use the block [Set Attribute of Property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayerProperties#ae_property_set_attribute)
 to set the attribute "value". If the property already has keyframes, this will add a new keyframe at the current time (but if the property is not keyframed, this will change the value for all frames and will not insert a keyframe).

Static Keyframe List[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_keyframe_json_const "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_keyframe_json_const](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeKeyframes/ae_keyframe_json_const.png)

Static Keyframe lists save the keyframe data inside the block - in other words, they contain a hardcoded list of keyframes (including all easing information).

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_keyframe_json_const_load_keys.gif)

Using a static keyframe list is very simple: Just select any keyframes (of a single property) in the Ae timeline and click the refresh icon of this block. The text field of the block now contains the keyframe data. To apply those keyframes to a property, use the block [Add Keyframes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_keyframes_apply)
.

##### JSON data

Technically, the result of this block is a text in a json format (which is identical to the data shown in the block's text field) . This allows you to write the data to a text file, for example, or bind it to a [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
.

Dynamic Keyframe List[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_keyframe_json_from_prop "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_keyframe_json_from_prop](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeKeyframes/ae_keyframe_json_from_prop.png)

This block reads keyframe data from a [Layer Property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayerProperties#ae_layer_properties_access)
 and returns it as a json string. In contrast to the [Static Keyframe List](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_keyframe_json_const)
, this block does not store any keyframe data, but just stores the information from which property the keyframe data should be retrieved. In other words, instead of saving a fixed set of keyframes with your script, this block reads keyframe data from the currently open Ae project in real time, each time the block is executed.

The dropdown option of the block allows you to specify which keyframes of the property you want to read and has the following options:

*   **all keyframes**
*   **selected keyframes**
*   **keyframes in workarea**
*   **keyframes in time range** (if you choose this, two more options appear to select a start and end time)

Delete Keyframes[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_keyframes_delete "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_keyframes_delete](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeKeyframes/ae_keyframes_delete.png)

Deletes keyframes on the given property (or if you give it a list of properties, the keyframes of all those properties are deleted). With the additional (and optional) parameters you can specify a min/max range and only delete the keyframes within that range.

Nearest Keyframe Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_nearest_keyframe_time "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_nearest_keyframe_time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeKeyframes/ae_nearest_keyframe_time.png)

Returns the time of the keyframe that is closest to the given time, or [null](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic#logic_null)
 if no such keyframe exists.

Optionally, the block can look only at keyframes before or only at keyframes after the given time.

### Example: Loop over keyframes[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#example-loop-over-keyframes "Direct link to heading")

This block script writes all points in time to the console, where at least one property of the active comp has a keyframe:

![After Effects script: Loop over Keyframes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_nearest_keyframe_time_example.png)

*   [Add Single Keyframe](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_set_value_at_time)
    
*   [Add Keyframes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_keyframes_apply)
    *   [Automatic Keyframe Adjustment](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#automatic-keyframe-adjustment)
        
    *   [Pasting Keys to Multiple Properties](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#pasting-keys-to-multiple-properties)
        
*   [Static Keyframe List](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_keyframe_json_const)
    
*   [Dynamic Keyframe List](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_keyframe_json_from_prop)
    
*   [Delete Keyframes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_keyframes_delete)
    
*   [Nearest Keyframe Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeKeyframes/#ae_nearest_keyframe_time)

# Troubleshooting | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#)

On this page

Troubleshooting
===============

Automation Blocks does not launch[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#automation-blocks-does-not-launch "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If the Automation Blocks window does not open at all or opens with a blank window, please [try this solution](https://aescripts.com/knowledgebase/index/view/faq/zxp-cep-extension-won-t-open/)
.

Script Execution Never Finishes[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#script-execution-never-finishes "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Automation Blocks script still running](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/troubleshooting/troubleshooting_still_running.png)

If the blue `... STILL RUNNING ...` message stays forever, often the script is waiting for an input from you. Maybe you overlooked a popup dialog, because it popped up on your second monitor? In some rare scenarios we've also heard from users that Ae or Pr put popup dialogs behind the main window, so it might be worth minimizing the main window to check what's behind it.

If a script is still running, all other scripts you try to run after it will be blocked, too, until its execution is stopped. When in doubt restart your host application.

If you are developing your own tools, another likely option is that you've created an endless loop in your program. If you keep the block code open, the block which is currently executed is always highlighted. That allows you to see which blocks are causing the trouble. To get a better picture of the program execution, you can also add [write to console blocks](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#block_extendscript_console_log)
 with infos what your script is currently doing and then check in the console window what happens during the script execution.

Foreign Language Versions of Pr[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#foreign-language-versions-of-pr "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you run a non-english version of Premiere Pro, make sure to use Automation Blocks 1.1.001 or newer, since previous versions have issues with non-english versions of Premiere Pro. For details [see here](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters#troubleshooting-clip-param-issues)
.

Issues with Spreadsheets[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#issues-with-spreadsheets "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you run into issues with reading or writing spreadsheets, [see "Troubleshooting Spreadsheet Issues"](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesSpreadsheet#troubleshooting-spreadsheet-issues)
.

Issues with Team Projects[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#issues-with-team-projects "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Automation Blocks has the assumption that the name of a Pr project always ends with `.prproj`. This is always the case for normal Premiere Pro projects, but not necessarily for team projects. Hence, if you want to use Automation Blocks with a team project, make sure to add .prproj at the end of the project name:

![Automation Blocks script works with team project with this workaround](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/troubleshooting/troubleshooting_team_project.png)

### Background[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#background "Direct link to heading")

In Automation Blocks, [project items are described by a path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
. For example, `music/song.mp3` describes the clip with name "song.mp3" in the project bin "music" of the active project. On the other hand `concert.prproj/music/song.mp3` describes the same project item not in the active project, but in the project "concert.prproj". Now if your project only has the name "concert" then Automation Blocks thinks that concert it s name of a bin, i.e. `concert/music/song.mp3` is referring to a clip "song.mp3" inside the subbin "music" of the bin "concert" of the active project. Therefore, Automation Blocks is not able to locate any project item properly, if your project name does not end with `.prproj`.

*   [Automation Blocks does not launch](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#automation-blocks-does-not-launch)
    
*   [Script Execution Never Finishes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#script-execution-never-finishes)
    
*   [Foreign Language Versions of Pr](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#foreign-language-versions-of-pr)
    
*   [Issues with Spreadsheets](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#issues-with-spreadsheets)
    
*   [Issues with Team Projects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#issues-with-team-projects)
    *   [Background](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/troubleshooting/#background)

# Ae Time | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime/#)

On this page

Ae Time
=======

Time values returned by blocks[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime/#time-values-returned-by-blocks "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Time values are always returned as (floating point) numbers representing the number of seconds. In a composition, these times are always measured relative to the start of the composition so that the beginning of the comp is always 0 (even if the [display start time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp#ae_comp_set_attribute_display_start_time)
 of the comp is not 0). For most scenarios, this is a very simple solution. But if you need to work with comps whose display start time is not 0, you need to add the display start time to the value returned by a block, to obtain the time shown in the Ae user interface.

Specifying Time Values[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime/#specify_time_values "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you need to send a time value as an argument to a block, you can specify it in one of the following formats:

*   a simple number like `12.4`is interpreted as a time given in seconds
*   strings containing minutes and seconds like `1:22`, `1m 22s`, `82s`, `82 secs` or `82 seconds`
*   string values containing hours, minutes and seconds like `3h 3m 2s`
*   string values containing timecodes like `00:00:00:00` or `00:00:00;00` (the latter with semicolon will be interpreted as a drop-frame timecode)
*   strings like `frame 10` or `10 frames`

Comp Time vs. Source Clip Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime/#comp_time "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Layer Times: In Point, Out Point and Start Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/layer_times.png)

The [start time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer#ae_layer_set_attribute_start_time)
, [in point](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer#ae_layer_set_attribute_in_point)
 and [out point](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer#ae_layer_set_attribute_out_point)
 of a layer are all specified in composition time, where a value of 0 corresponds to the beginning of the composition.

Convert Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime/#ae_time_converter "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_time_converter](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeTime/ae_time_converter.png)

With this block you can convert a time value given in [any format](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime/#specify_time_values)
 to another format.

### framerate[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime/#framerate "Direct link to heading")

The optional framerate parameter is used to convert between values given in frames and other values. If neither the source nor the target format is in frames, the framerate is ignored. If you want to use the framerate of a composition, footage item or layer, you can simply pass this object as a framerate parameter. If you don't specify a framerate parameter, the framerate of the active composition is used.

*   [Time values returned by blocks](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime/#time-values-returned-by-blocks)
    
*   [Specifying Time Values](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime/#specify_time_values)
    
*   [Comp Time vs. Source Clip Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime/#comp_time)
    
*   [Convert Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime/#ae_time_converter)
    *   [framerate](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime/#framerate)

# Ae Rendering | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#)

On this page

Ae Rendering
============

If you want to render some comps, the typical workflow is to

1.  [Clear the queue](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#ae_renderqueue_clear)
    
2.  [Add Comps to the queue](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#ae_renderqueue_add_comp)
     that you want to render
3.  [start the render queue](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#ae_renderqueue_start)
     or [send it to Adobe Media Encoder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#ae_renderqueue_queue_in_ame)
    

The first step is optional, but it ensures that the queue does not contain any old items that would otherwise be rendered by accident.

Clear Ae Render Queue[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#ae_renderqueue_clear "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_renderqueue_clear](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeRendering/ae_renderqueue_clear.png)

This block removes all entries from After Effects' render queue.

Add to Ae Render Queue[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#ae_renderqueue_add_comp "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_renderqueue_add_comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeRendering/ae_renderqueue_add_comp.png)

This block adds a [Composition](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp#accessComp)
 to the render queue.

**Render Settings & Output Modules**

You can choose the _render settings_ and _output module_ you want to use. You can also add additional output modules using the blue cog wheel icon in the top left corner of the block. This is useful if you want to export the same comp in multiple formats.

![adding more output modules](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_renderqueue_add_comp_multiple_output_modules.png)

**Output Files**

For each output module, you also need to specify an output [file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
. In the file name you can use the placeholders like `[compName]` or `[fileextension]`, which are supported by After Effects. To find all supported placeholders, open the dialog for custom output templates as shown here:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_renderqueue_add_comp_placeholders.gif)

Start Ae Render Queue[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#ae_renderqueue_start "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_renderqueue_start](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeRendering/ae_renderqueue_start.png)

This block starts After Effects' render queue, exactly as if you clicked on the _"render"_ button of the render queue.

![Render Button After Effects Scripting](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_renderqueue_start_screenshot.png)

Send queued items to Adobe Media Encoder[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#ae_renderqueue_queue_in_ame "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_renderqueue_queue_in_ame](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeRendering/ae_renderqueue_queue_in_ame.png)

This block has the same effect as if you clicked the button _"Queue in AME"_ in the render queue: It sends the queued items from After Effects' render queue to Adobe Media Encoder.

![After Effects Scripting: Queue in AME](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_renderqueue_queue_in_ame_screenshot.png)

If you enable the checkbox _"render immediately"_, the block also starts the rendering process in Adobe Media Encoder.

When Adobe Media Encoder receives the queued items, it applies the most recently used encoding preset. If the _"render immediately"_ option is set to true, you will not have an opportunity to change the encoding settings.

##### Compatibility

This block requires Adobe Media Encoder CC 2017 11.0) and After Effects CC 2017 (14.0) or later.

*   [Clear Ae Render Queue](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#ae_renderqueue_clear)
    
*   [Add to Ae Render Queue](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#ae_renderqueue_add_comp)
    
*   [Start Ae Render Queue](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#ae_renderqueue_start)
    
*   [Send queued items to Adobe Media Encoder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeRendering/#ae_renderqueue_queue_in_ame)
