# Ae App & Project | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#)

On this page

Ae App & Project
================

Here you'll find all blocks that do something with the After Effects application or the project, but are not specific to a particular project item or composition. This includes things like creating folders, importing footage or saving and closing projects, as well as executing Ae menu commands and storing values in Ae's settings.

Create Bin[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#ae_project_item_create_bin "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_project_item_create_bin](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/ae_project_item_create_bin.png)

This block creates a new bin in the project panel.

**paths and sub-bins**

Instead of the name of a bin like `footage`, you can also provide an entire path like `footage/audio`. In that case a bin `footage` is created (if it does not yet exist) and inside of it a bin `audio` (again, only if a sub-bin of that name does not yet exist).

**parent bin**

Here, you can enter the name or path of a parent bin, inside of which the bin should be created. Instead of entering a bin manually, you can select the bin in your project and then click the refresh button of the parent bin input.

Note that if you want to create a bin `psd` inside a bin `footage`, for example, you have two options:

1.  enter `footage/psd` as the bin path and leave the parent bin empty
2.  enter `psd`as the bin path and set the parent bin to \`footage\`\`

The only difference is that the first option creates the bin footage, if it does not yet exist, whereas the second option gives an error in that case.

**example**

This video shows some examples of how to use the _Create Bin_ block:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_project_item_create_bin_example.gif)

Sync Folder[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#block_ae_sync_project_bin_with_folder "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_ae_sync_project_bin_with_folder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/block_ae_sync_project_bin_with_folder.png)

This block is your smart file importer. It syncs a bin in the project panel with a folder on your harddrive, i.e. it imports all files from that folder into the bin, but only if they haven't already been imported. This means whenever the contents of the folder changes, you can simply execute the block again to update the contents of the project.

**sync folder** is the [folder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
 on your hard-drive which should be imported.

**with project bin** is the bin in the project panel that the files of the given folder should be imported into. If the project bin does not exist, the block creates it for you.

**subfolders** can be set to

*   `include`: also creates for each subfolder a corresponding sub-bin, and syncs those too. So, the entire folder structure (including subfolders of subfolders) is recreated inside the project panel.
*   `exclude`: ignores all content of subfolders
*   `flatten`: imports files from subfolders directly into the main bin instead of creating sub-bins for them.

To set this value, you can either choose a value in the dropdown list or drag a [text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text)
 value on top of the dropdown. This text then must contain exactly one of the three words `include`, `exclude`, or `flatten`.

**import multi-layer files as** allows you to import those as `footage`, `comp` or `comp (cropped layers)`. These are exactly the same options as those provided by Ae's normal import dialog. To set this value, you can either choose a value in the dropdown list or drag a [text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text)
 value on top of the dropdown. This text then must contain one of the three words `footage`, `comp`, or `comp (cropped layers)`.

**import numbered images as sequence** enables the import of image sequences. If it is disabled, each image is imported as an individual project item. Note that images, which have no number at the end of their file name, are always imported as a single still image and not an image sequence of length 1. So, if you enable the image sequence option, a file _myImage.png_ will still be imported as a still image, but an image _myImage000.png_ or _myImage004.png_ will be treated as part of an image sequence.

**delete other items** enables a check if the synced project bin in the project panel contains other items, which are not included in the folder on your hard-drive. Note that this option deletes all other items (also comps etc), to ensure that the contents of the folder and the synced project bin are 100% identical.

##### no live update

Note that when the contents of the folder on your harddrive changes, the project bin does not update instantly. You need to execute the block again, to apply all changes to the project bin.

Import File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#block_ae_import_file "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_ae_import_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/block_ae_import_file.png)

This block imports a single image, video, audio or Ae project file into the project.

### Project Item Variable[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#project-item-variable "Direct link to heading")

The new project item is saved in the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `project item var`, so you can use this [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 if you want to do something with the imported item in subsequent blocks. In this example, I first import a footage file and then insert it as a [new layer](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer#ae_new_layer)
 into the active composition:

![Import file and insert it in the active composition](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_ae_import_file_example.png)

### Parameters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#parameters "Direct link to heading")

**project bin** is the (name or path of) the bin in the project panel into which the file should be imported.

**import multi-layer files as** allows you to import them as `footage`, `comp` or `comp (cropped layers)`. These are exactly the same options as those provided by Ae's normal import dialog.

**import images as** allows you to import images as `still image` or `image sequence`.

**avoid duplicates** checks if the footage item has already been imported into that project bin before. If this is the case, the block does not import the footage a second time and binds the variable `project item var` to the already existing project item instead.

##### Importing Many Files

If you want to import multiple files, consider using the [Sync Folder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#block_ae_sync_project_bin_with_folder)
 block instead.

Current Project File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#block_ae_project_file "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_ae_project_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/block_ae_project_file.png)

This block returns the [file path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
 of the currently open project file. If the current project has not yet been saved, it gives an error. To check if a project file exists, use the block [Has Project File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#block_ae__has_project_file)
.

Has Project File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#block_ae__has_project_file "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_ae__has_project_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/block_ae__has_project_file.png)

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 which `false`, if the currently open project has no project file yet (i.e. has not been saved at all yet).

Current Project Folder[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#block_ae_project_folder "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_ae_project_folder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/block_ae_project_folder.png)

This block returns the [folder path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
 of the folder which contains the currently open project. If the current project has not yet been saved, it gives an error. To check if a project file (and hence project folder) exists, use the block [Has Project File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#block_ae__has_project_file)
.

Project Name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#block_ae_project_name "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_ae_project_name](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/block_ae_project_name.png)

This block returns the name of the project (i.e. the filename of the aep file) with or without the .aep extension.

If you need the full file path, use the block [Current Project File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#block_ae_project_file)
 instead.

Save Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#ae_project_save "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_project_save](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/ae_project_save.png)

This block saves the current project, exactly like the menu command File->Save.

Save Project As[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#ae_project_save_as "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_project_save_as](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/ae_project_save_as.png)

Saves the Ae project to a new [file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
.

**allow to overwrite if file exists**: if you disable this option, the block gives an error if the file aready exists.

**create folder if not existing**: if this option is enabled, the block creates the folders of the given file path, if they don't yet exist.

Open Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#ae_project_open "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_project_open](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/ae_project_open.png)

Opens the given project [file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
.

**if current project has unsaved changes** offers the choices `don't save`, `save` and `prompt`.

Close Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#ae_project_close "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_project_close](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/ae_project_close.png)

Closes the currently open Ae project.

**if current project has unsaved changes** offers the choices `don't save`, `save` and `prompt`.

New Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#ae_project_new "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_project_new](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/ae_project_new.png)

Creates a new, empty Ae project.

**if current project has unsaved changes** offers the choices `don't save`, `save` and `prompt`.

Execute Menu Command[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#ae_execute_menu_command "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_execute_menu_command](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/ae_execute_menu_command.png)

This block allows you to execute any command from the After Effects main menu. As an argument, you need to provide either the exact label of the menu entry, like `Increment and Save` or `Open Team Project...`. Be sure to write the exact text as it appears in the menu, including spaces, dots and use the correct upper/lower case.

![Menu command using a label](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_execute_menu_command_example.png)

Instead of using the label, you can also enter an After Effects menu command ID. David Torno has created a great [description of what menu command IDs are, as well as a comprehensive list of them](https://www.provideocoalition.com/after-effects-menu-command-ids/)
.

##### Support for other UI Languages

Note that the menu command names area different in each language. `Save as...` only works for English Ae, for example, whereas you need to use in the German version `Speichern Unter...` instead. To be compatible with After Effects installations in different languages, it's better to use the IDs instead of label names.

Get Settings[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#ae_settings_get "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_settings_get](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/ae_settings_get.png)

Retrieves a value that you stored in the settings using [save settings](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#ae_settings_save)
.

Save Settings[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#ae_settings_save "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_settings_save](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProject/ae_settings_save.png)

Save any value (texts, numbers, lists,...) inside After Effects' settings. This allows you to remember values and retrieve them again when the script is executed the next time. You can even use it to share data between different scripts, if one script writes the settings and another reads the settings using the same key.

### Key[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#key "Direct link to heading")

The key is a unique name that you need to retrieve the value again. Make sure to use a descriptive name so it's unlikely it will overlap with other scripts. If you use a key like `width`, it is likely that another script is using the same key too. If you use `width of Mathias' layer distribution tool`, such an overlap is less likely.

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#example "Direct link to heading")

Settings can be very helpful to remember the values that a user has entered into a [dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#modal_input_dialog)
, so we can use the exact same values as default values next time:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_settings_save_example.png)Here, we ask the user to enter a color value. When you run the script for the first time, the dialog shows \`#00000\` as default value. After the dialog is closed, the color entered by the number is saved as the preference \`my script's color\`. When you run the script the next time, this value is used as default value.

### Technical details[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject/#technical-details "Direct link to heading")

After Effects only supports strings as settings data. However, Automation Blocks converts any value to JSON, before storing it in the preferences (and decodes it again while reading the settings). So, you can store any kind of data in the settings, including numbers, logic values and lists.

##### tip

These values aren't shared between versions of Ae; each new install brings new settings files, so these preferences won't carry over.

Internally, all saved settings are stored in the section "Settings\_AutomationBlocks". You cannot use this block to change Ae's internal preferences.

# Files Name & Path | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath/#)

On this page

Files Name & Path
=================

Files (and also Folders) in Automation Blocks are just texts representing the full path of the file. The following blocks will help you to manipulate these paths, which is very helpful for creating new filenames, renaming files, etc.

The blocks have been created to manipulate file paths, but of course they work on any text - so you can also use them to manipulate other texts, too.

Replace In File Path[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath/#block_extendscript_file_path_replace_part "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_file_path_replace_part](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/FilesNameAndPath/block_extendscript_file_path_replace_part.png)

A file path like `path/to/myFile001.png` consists of different sections:

*   the folder `path/to`
*   the name `myFile`
*   the numbers `001`
*   the extension `.png`

With this block, you can replace any of these parts with something new.

When you replace the suffix and your new suffix does not have a dot at the beginning, the block will add one automatically. So, it doesn't matter if you use `jpg`or `.jpg` as the new suffix, for example. In the same way, if you replace the path, it does not matter if your path ends with a `/` (or `\`) or not, since the block will add one if needed.

Tinker File Path[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath/#string_tinker_path "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block string_tinker_path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/FilesNameAndPath/string_tinker_path.png)

This block is your Swiss army knife when it comes to manipulating a file path. It's like an advanced version of the [Replace in File Path block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath#block_extendscript_file_path_replace_part)
. It decomposes a file path into its ingredients - like splitting `/path/to/myFile001.jpg` into `/path/to`,`/`, `myFile`, `001` and `.jpg`. You can then recombine those parts however you like.

The block extracts the following parts from the path:

*   **$FOLDER** the folder where the file is located.
*   **$NAME** the name of the file without numbers at the end (if the file is part of an image sequence, for example) and without the extension
*   **$NUMBERS** the numbers at the end of the filename (for an image sequence, for example). If the filename has no numbers at the end, this is empty.
*   **$EXT** The file extension, i.e. the suffix like `.mp4`or `.mov` including the dot.
*   **$SEP** the separator symbol used in the path - either `/` or `\`.

The block allows you to combine these parts again in whatever way you want by specifying a pattern.

The example above turns the file name `path/to/my/file012.jpg` into the result `path/to/my/file_now_png_00012.png`, for example.

#### number of digits in $NUMBERS[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath/#number-of-digits-in-numbers "Direct link to heading")

You can change the number of digits in `$NUMBERS` by adding the number of desired digits in square brackets. So if `$NUMBERS` is `0123`, for example, then `$NUMBERS[5]` is `00123`, resulting in 5 digits. This function only adds or removes zeros, but no other digits, so `$NUMBERS[1]` will give `123` and not `3`.

#### parent folders[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath/#parent-folders "Direct link to heading")

For `$FOLDER` you can go up the parenting chain by adding the number of levels you want to go up in square brackets. So if `$FOLDER` is `C:\path\to\my` then `$FOLDER[1]` is `C:\path\to` and `$FOLDER[2]` is `C:\path`.

#### More Examples[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath/#more-examples "Direct link to heading")

The following block constructs a new path from the project file path pointing to a folder `footage`, which is a sibling of the folder where the project is created.

![Project File Example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/string_tinker_path_example.png)

So if the project is `C:\my\project\pr\myproject.prproj`, for example, then the result is `C:\my\project\footage`

Text Pattern[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath/#string_pattern "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Text Pattern block is very useful for creating file paths, but also for all kinds of other strings. For this reason, it's [documented in the Text category](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#string_pattern)
.

*   [Replace In File Path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath/#block_extendscript_file_path_replace_part)
    
*   [Tinker File Path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath/#string_tinker_path)
    
*   [Text Pattern](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath/#string_pattern)

# Files Text | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText/#)

On this page

Files Text
==========

These blocks read and write text files from your hard-drive. You don't need to worry about details like opening or closing files. Automation Blocks opens the files when you try to read/write them for the first time and closes them automatically when the script is done.

##### Text Formats

These blocks only support the simple text format (.txt) and cannot parse rich text (.rtf)

Write Text File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText/#block_extendscript_text_file_write "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_text_file_write](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/FilesText/block_extendscript_text_file_write.png)

Writes the given text to a text file. If the text file already exists, the new text is appended to the end of the file. If you don't want that, [delete the file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_delete_file)
 first.

If the file does not yet exist, it will be created.

#### Create Folder if Not Existing[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText/#create-folder-if-not-existing "Direct link to heading")

If this option is enabled, the folder where the text file is located (and any of its parent folders) are created if they don't yet exist. This means if you want to create the file in a new folder, you don't have to worry about creating the folder before writing the file.

##### format with pattern

If the text you want to write has a complex structure, you can combine the _Write Text File_ block with the [Text Pattern Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#string_pattern)
. The script _export marker to srt file_ is a good example of this.

### Forcing File Writes to Disk[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText/#forcing-file-writes-to-disk "Direct link to heading")

For performance reasons, Automation Blocks delays writing files to disk until the script execution is complete.

If you need a file to be saved earlier (e.g., to [execute it](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_execute_file)
 immediately or [use an .sqpreset file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#block_pr_new_sequence)
 in subsequent blocks), you can force Automation Blocks to save all open files by running the following code in an [Execute Code block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther#pr_execute_code)
:

`$._extAutomationBlocks.all.fileCollection.cleanup()`

This applies to both text and spreadsheet files.

See also [this forum discussion](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro-discussions/create-custom-resolution-sequences-from-a-spreadsheet-using-automation-blocks/m-p/15161360#M552965)
.

For All Lines of Text File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText/#extendscript_text_file_for_all_lines "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block extendscript_text_file_for_all_lines](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/FilesText/extendscript_text_file_for_all_lines.png)

This block reads a text line by line.

The blocks that you nest inside this block are executed once for each line of the text. During that execution, the text of the line is accessible in the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `my line`.

Read Text File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText/#extendscript_text_file_read "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block extendscript_text_file_read](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/FilesText/extendscript_text_file_read.png)

Reads either the entire contents of a text file, or just a single line. If you choose to read a single line, it will start with the first line and each subsequent call will read the next line. However, to read the entire file line by line, the [For All Lines of Text File Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText#extendscript_text_file_for_all_lines)
 is a better option.

End of File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText/#extendscript_text_file_eof "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block extendscript_text_file_eof](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/FilesText/extendscript_text_file_eof.png)

When you read a file line by line using the [Read Text File Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText#extendscript_text_file_read)
, you can use this block to check if the end of the file has been reached. It returns the logic value `true` if the end of the file has been reached and `false` if there is still content left to read.

*   [Write Text File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText/#block_extendscript_text_file_write)
    *   [Forcing File Writes to Disk](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText/#forcing-file-writes-to-disk)
        
*   [For All Lines of Text File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText/#extendscript_text_file_for_all_lines)
    
*   [Read Text File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText/#extendscript_text_file_read)
    
*   [End of File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText/#extendscript_text_file_eof)

# Functions | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/functions/#)

On this page

Functions
=========

Overview[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/functions/#overview "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Functions allow you to structure your block program. Just as a precomp wraps an entire collection of layers into a single layer, a function can wrap a stack of blocks into a single block.

![Creating a function](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/procedures_defnoreturn_example.png)

Create Function[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/functions/#procedures_defnoreturn "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block procedures_defnoreturn](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Functions/procedures_defnoreturn.png)

With the _Create Function_ block you can make your own function. Just replace the text _"do something"_ with a text which describes what your function does and plug the blocks inside of it, which the function should execute.

**Example**

![Creating a function in Automation Blocks](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/procedures_defnoreturn_example_bins.png)

Here we replaced _"do something"_ with _"create my usual bins"_ and plugged the blocks inside which create project bins for audio, video and precomps. As a result, among the blocks in the category "functions" you now find a new block called _"create my usual bins"_, which creates all these bins. You can think of this little block as a shorthand for the three blocks that you have plugged into the function definition.

##### Structure Your Scripts

If your scripts tend to be long, it can help to structure them into individual parts using functions. Say you need to first import some footage, then create some comps, and finally put something in the render queue. You can wrap each of these three tasks into a separate function, so that your main script just consists of three function calls.

This is a very good way of documenting what your script is doing. If you wrap a part of your code into a function, you effectively give that part of your code a descriptive name and isolate it from other parts.

##### Collapse Functions

You can right-click on a "Create Function" block and choose "collapse". That way, the entire content of the function is hidden and saves you a lot of space.

### Functions with Arguments[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/functions/#functions-with-arguments "Direct link to heading")

If you think of functions as precomps, then arguments are your master properties. With master properties you can use the same precomp many times, but customize it each time you use it by setting the master properties to a different value. In the same way, a function can have arguments and each time you call the function you can give it a different value for that argument.

You can add, remove and rename arguments by clicking on the blue cog wheel icon of the block which defines the function. Here, we added an argument with the name "my argument" to a function:

![Creating a function with an Arguments](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/procedures_defnoreturn_example_with_argument.png)

For each argument, the function block creates a [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 and when the function is executed, the variable will have the value that you gave as an argument to the function call.

![Creating a function with an Arguments 2](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/procedures_defnoreturn_example_with_argument_2.png)

In this example the function "say hello" is called two times, once with the argument "Victoria" and again with the argument "Sue". So, during the first execution the function gives the message "Hello Victoria!" and during the second execution the message is "Hello Sue!"

### Return in Functions[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/functions/#procedures_ifreturn "Direct link to heading")

When a function is executed, usually the entire block stack of the function is executed. But with an **If Return** block you can stop the execution of a function anywhere in the middle of a block stack.

Here is an example:

![Block procedures_defreturn](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Functions/procedures_ifreturn.png)

This function shows two messages using an [alert](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#block_extendscript_alert)
 block. The first message is always executed. But the second message is only shown if there is an active comp in the After Effects project. We achieve this with the "not has active comp" condition, which quits the execution of the function if there is no active composition.

### Functions with Return Values[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/functions/#procedures_defreturn "Direct link to heading")

In the category "Functions" of the block library, you'll also find a block to create functions with an extra return parameter:

![Block procedures_defreturn](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Functions/procedures_defreturn.png)

These functions have corresponding function call blocks with a round shape, which indicates that they actually return a value. The functions work in exactly the same way, except that after all blocks you've plugged in to the function have been executed, the value you specify in the _"return"_ parameter is used as the end result.

**Example**

![Creating a function which returns a value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/procedures_defreturn_example.png)

This "get random label color index" block returns a different random number in the range 1-16 each time it is called. Note that in this case, the main code block of the function definition is empty. But if the calculation of the random number required any preparational steps, you could plug these into the function definition.

*   [Overview](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/functions/#overview)
    
*   [Create Function](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/functions/#procedures_defnoreturn)
    *   [Functions with Arguments](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/functions/#functions-with-arguments)
        
    *   [Return in Functions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/functions/#procedures_ifreturn)
        
    *   [Functions with Return Values](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/functions/#procedures_defreturn)

# Ae Project Items | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#)

On this page

Ae Project Items
================

The blocks in this section allow you to do all kinds of things with project items - i.e. all the footage, compositions, solids, bins etc that are listed in the project panel (and its sub-bins). Note that if a project item is a composition, you can also use the blocks in the section [Ae Comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp)
 with it, which gives you access to functionality that is specific to comps only.

### Accessing a Particular Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#accessProjectItem "Direct link to heading")

Automation Blocks represents project items with a text - like a path indicating where the item can be found. You can specify a particular project item as follows:

*   **Project Item** and **Project Bin** blocks. The [Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_path)
     and the [Project Bin Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_itembin_path)
     are the easiest way to specify an item path, if the item already exists in your project. Just select the item in your project, click the block's _refresh_ button and it will generate the item path for you.
    
*   **Name:** If the project item has a unique name, just use a text block with the name `my item name`
    
*   **Path:** You can also give a path to where the item can be found, like `/bin/subbin/my item name`. If you provide the full path instead of a name, Automation Blocks does not need to search your entire project, which makes the script faster. Also. you'll avoid getting into trouble if multiple project items have the same name. Note that a string `Comp 1` searches for a project item in all bins of the project panel, whereas `/Comp 1` only looks for it in the root bin.
    
*   **Layer or Property** If you have a [layer](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer)
     or [property of a layer](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayerProperties#ae_layer_properties_access)
     you can use it as argument of any block that expects a project item. Automation Blocks will convert it to the project item of the layer's source automatically. If the layer has no corresponding project item (i.e. is a text, shape, camera or light layer, for example) the project item of the comp is used instead. You can also use the layer's [source item attribute](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer#ae_layer_set_attribute_source_item)
    .
    
*   **Compostions** Since compositions are project items, you can use compositions (like the [Active Comp block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp#ae_active_comp)
    , for example) as arguments for any blocks which expect a project item.
    

Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_path "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_item_path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProjectItems/ae_item_path.png)

This block is used inside other blocks whenever you need to specify a particular project item. Just select a project item in the project panel and click the block's _refresh_ icon to choose it.

### Project Item vs Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#project-item-vs-text "Direct link to heading")

Technically, a _Project Item_ block is a simple _Text_ block which allows you to create the texts of project item paths very conveniently.

Say you have a project item "myVideo.mp4" inside the project bin "footage" - you can simply reference it with a [Text block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#text)
 and enter the text `footage/myVideo.mp4`. But instead of a _Text_ block, it is much more convenient to use a _Project Item_ block. With the _Project Item_ block you don't need to type the path manually, and instead just use the refresh button to generate it automatically.

Project Bin[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_itembin_path "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_itembin_path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProjectItems/ae_itembin_path.png)

This block is identical to the [Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_path)
, except that it only allows you to select project bins and no other items. By default, this block is empty, which represents the root bin of the project.

The Only Selected Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_get_only_selected_item "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_ae_get_only_selected_item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProjectItems/block_ae_get_only_selected_item.png)

In situations where you expect the user to have selected exactly one item in the project panel, you can use this block to access this selected item. The block will stop with an error message if you have no or multiple project items selected.

**Item vs Bin**

In the dropdown list you can set an option to accept

*   only items
*   only bins
*   both items and bins

In the first case, the block also stops with an error if the only selected project item is a bin and in the second case it stops with an error if it is not a bin.

For Each Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_for_all_items_in_bin "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_ae_for_all_items_in_bin](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProjectItems/block_ae_for_all_items_in_bin.png)

The block is a [loop](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops)
 which allows you to do something with all project items (or all selected project items). The blocks you plug into this block are executed many times - names one time for each of the project items.

### Parameters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_for_all_items_in_bin_params "Direct link to heading")

**my item variable**

The block creates a [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `my item`. Each time the blocks you plug into the loop are executed, this variable has a different value - namely the [Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
 that the loop is processing at that time.

**project bin**

Here you can choose a bin of your project to only loop over the items contained in that bin. If you leave this control empty, the root bin of the project panel will be used. Using the dropdown before the parameter, you can choose to process only the items in the project bin, or also the ones contained in subbins.

**only selected items**

With this checkbox you can limit the block to only loop over project items which are selected in the project panel. If you want to loop over all selected items of the project, make sure to set the option _project bin_ to `project bin and sub-bins`, because otherwise only the selected items in the root bin of the project are considered.

**allowed types**

With these checkboxes you can limit the block to only loop over items of specific types. To loop over all comps in the project, for example, just keep the _composition_ checkbox checked and uncheck all others.

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#example "Direct link to heading")

This example shows one alert message for each project item in the root bin of the project panel. In the message box, the name of the respective item is shown.

![Example: Looping over project items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_ae_for_all_items_in_bin_example.png)

List of Project Items[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_get_items_in_bin "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_ae_get_items_in_bin](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProjectItems/block_ae_get_items_in_bin.png)

This block is similar to the block [For Each Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_for_all_items_in_bin)
, but instead of looping over the project items, it creates a list of them. The parameters are exactly the same as the [parameters of the For Each Project Item block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_for_all_items_in_bin_params)
.

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_get_items_in_bin_example "Direct link to heading")

This script uses the _List of Project Items_ in combination with the [Select Project Items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_item_select)
 block, to select all footage items in the project bin with the name "scene 1". Note that we have chosen `in bin and sub-bins` so that items included in sub-bins of the bin "scene 1" will also be selected.

![Example: Looping over project items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_ae_get_items_in_bin_example.png)

For Each Layer with Source Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_for_all_layers_with_source_item "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_ae_for_all_layers_with_source_item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProjectItems/block_ae_for_all_layers_with_source_item.png)

This block is a [loop](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops)
 which allows you to do something with all the layers (in the entire project) which use a particular project item as source. If the project item is a composition, it will loop over all layers which are precomps using exactly that composition, for example. And if the project item is a video clip, it will loop over all layers which contain this video clip as source.

**my layer**

This block executes the blocks you plug into it many times, namely once for each layer that has the given item as source. Before those blocks are executed, the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `my layer` is set to the layer that is considered in this repetition of the loop. If the project item is used in three layers, for example, then the blocks are executed three times and each time the variable `my layer` is one of these three layers.

**item(s)**

Here you can either specify a single project item or an entire [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 (array) of project items. The block will then loop over all layers which use one of these project items as their source.

Get Attribute of Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_get_attribute "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_item_get_attribute](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProjectItems/ae_item_get_attribute.png)

Returns one of the attributes of the given project item, like its file, framerate, or label color. For a description of all available attributes, see [Set Attribute of Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute)
. Note that compositions are also project items. To access properties that are specific to compositions you need to use the block [Get Attribute of Comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp#ae_comp_get_attribute)
 (which also accepts a project item as argument, if this project item is a comp).

Set Attribute of Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_item_set_attribute](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProjectItems/ae_item_set_attribute.png)

Sets any of the available attributes of a project item. After you choose an attribute, the inputs of the block change as needed for that attribute. Note that compositions are also project items. To set properties that are specific to compositions, you need to use the block [Get Attribute of Comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp#ae_comp_get_attribute)
 (which also accepts a project item as argument, if this project item is a comp).

The available attributes of project items are listed below. If an attribute is marked as read-only, you can only get but not set this attribute.

### alpha mode[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#alpha-mode "Direct link to heading")

The alpha mode of After Effects' Interpret Footage dialog. If you get this value, it will be one of the following [texts](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text)

1.  `IGNORE`
2.  `STRAIGHT`
3.  `PREMULTIPLIED`

If you set this value, you can either set it to one of the texts above, or to one of the numbers 1-3. Upper/lower case is ignored. So, instead of the text `IGNORE`, you can also use the number 1, or the text `ignore`.

You can get and set this attribute for items of [type](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_type)
 `FootageNoSolidItem`, for other items it will produce an error.

### comment[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#comment "Direct link to heading")

The comment text for a project item. To see the comments in the project panel, right-click on the title row of the project panel and choose `Columns->Comment` from the menu. This will reveal an extra column in the project panel which shows the item comment.

### duration (read-only, except for comp items)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#duration-read-only-except-for-comp-items "Direct link to heading")

The duration of the project item in seconds. For details on time values see [Ae Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime)
.

### file[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_file "Direct link to heading")

The [file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
 of the project item. If you set this value, the result is the same as if you right click the item in the project panel and choose `Replace Footage->File...`.

### footage missing (read-only)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#footage-missing-read-only "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating if the file of the project item is missing on the hard-drive.

### frame duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#frame-duration "Direct link to heading")

The duration of a single frame in seconds. If the frame rate of the comp is 25fps, for example, the duration is 1/25s = 0.04s. Don't use this value for items which have no video (like bins or still images).

### frame rate[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#frame-rate "Direct link to heading")

The number of frames per second of the project item. Don't use this value for items which have no video (like bins or still images).

You can get and set this attribute for items of [type](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_type)
 `FootageNoSolidItem` and `CompItem`, for other items it will produce an error.

Setting the `frame rate` attribute on footage items has the same effect as the "conform to frame rate" option of Ae's Interpret Footage dialog

### frame rate (native)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#frame-rate-native "Direct link to heading")

Same as the `frame rate` attribute, unless you change the frame rate of a footage item using the "conform to frame rate" option of Ae's Interpret Footage dialog (or by setting the `frame rate` attribute with Automation Blocks). In that case, the `frame rate (native)` still contains the original frame rate of the footage file, whereas `frame rate` contains the conformed frame rate.

### has alpha (read-only)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#has-alpha-read-only "Direct link to heading")

A[logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating if the item has an alpha channel. This value is only relevant for items of [type](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_type)
 `FootageNoSolidItem`. For comps it is always `true` and for bins and solids it is always `false`.

### has audio (read-only)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#has-audio-read-only "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating if the project item has audio contents.

### has video (read-only)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_has_video "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating if the project item has video contents (this is also `true` for still images).

### height[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_height "Direct link to heading")

The height of the video in pixels. Only use this if the attribute [has video](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_has_video)
 is `true`. See also the attributes [width](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_width)
 and [size](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_size)
.

### id (read-only)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#id-read-only "Direct link to heading")

A unique and persistent identification number used internally to identify an item between sessions. The value of the ID remains the same when the project is saved to a file and later reloaded. However, when you import this project into another project, new IDs are assigned to all items in the imported project. The ID is not displayed anywhere in the user interface.

### invert alpha[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#invert-alpha "Direct link to heading")

A[logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` which corresponds to the "Invert Alpha" option of Ae's Interpret Footage dialog. You can get and set this attribute for items of [type](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_type)
 `FootageNoSolidItem`, for other items it will produce an error.

### is still[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#is-still "Direct link to heading")

A[logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating if this item is a still image. This value is only relevant for items of [type](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_type)
 `FootageNoSolidItem`.It is always false for comps, bins and solid items.

### label color HexCode (read-only)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#label-color-hexcode-read-only "Direct link to heading")

The label color of the project item as hex color string (like `#ff0000` for 100% red, for example).

### label color id[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_label_color_id "Direct link to heading")

The ID of the label color (0 for None, or 1 to 16 for one of the preset colors in the _Labels_ preferences)

### label color name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_label_color_name "Direct link to heading")

The label color name of the project item as configured in the _Labels_ preferences (like `Red`, `Sea Foam`, or `None`).

By default, the block to set the label color name shows a convenient dropdown where you can pick any of the default label colors. Note that this dropdown does not update when you have configured custom label color names. If you have configured custom label colors in the preferences, you can either drag a [Text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#text)
 block on top of the dropdown and enter the custom label name manually, or you set the label color using the [color id](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_label_color_id)
.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_item_set_attribute_label_color_name.gif)

### loop[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#loop "Direct link to heading")

A number corresponding to the loop option of Ae's Interpret Footage dialog. You can set this attribute for items of [type](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_type)
 `FootageNoSolidItem`, for other items it will produce an error.

### name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#name "Direct link to heading")

The name of the project item as shown in the project panel.

##### Renaming breaks variables

Note that project items are accessed in Automation Blocks by name or path. If you have stored in a [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 the path `footage/myVideo.mp4`, for example, and you then rename the project item to `new name`, the old variable cannot access the project item anymore, since there is no item with the name `myVideo.mp4` in the bin `footage` anymore.

Workaround: Usually it works best to only rename a project item after you've done all the other things you might want to do with it (so you don't need to access it anymore). Alternatively, you can update the variable to `footage/new name` (for example with the block [Replace in Text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#string_replace)
).

### parent bin[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_parent_bin "Direct link to heading")

The bin of the project panel, in which the project item is located. Set this value to move the item to another project bin. If you enter a path of a project bin, which does not yet exist, the block creates the bin for you.

This example moves the project item `Comp 1` from the folder `work in progress` to the folder `final`:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_item_set_attribute_parent_bin.gif)

### pixel aspect[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#pixel-aspect "Direct link to heading")

The pixel aspect ratio of the video (1 for the default, square pixels).

### proxy[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_proxy "Direct link to heading")

The proxy [file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
 of this item or [null](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic#logic_null)
 if the item has no proxy. When you set a proxy, it is enabled automatically. To enable/disable it later, use the attribute [user proxy](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_use_proxy)
.

To remove existing proxies, set this value to [null](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic#logic_null)
.

### selected[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_selected "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating whether the item is selected in the project panel. Note that setting this attribute for an item does not change the selection of any other items (i.e. keeps everything else selected that has been selected before).

To change the selection in the project panel, you can either set this attribute or use the block [Select Project Items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_item_select)
 .

### size \[w,h\][​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_size "Direct link to heading")

A [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 (array) containing the width and height of the video in pixels. Only use this if the attribute [has video](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_has_video)
 is `true`. If you only need one of the two values, use the attributes [width](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_width)
 and [height](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_height)
 instead.

### time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#time "Direct link to heading")

The current time of the item when it is being previewed directly from the Project panel (in seconds). For details on time values see [Ae Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime)
.

### tree path (read-only)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#tree-path-read-only "Direct link to heading")

The full path of the project item in the project panel. For the item `myVideo.mp4` in the bin `footage` the tree path would be `footage/myVideo.mp4`. To move the item into a different project bin, set the [parent bin attribute](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_parent_bin)
.

If you need the path of the corresponding footage file instead of the path of the item itself, use the [attribute file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_file)
 instead.

### type (read-only)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_type "Direct link to heading")

One of the following [text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text)
 values:

*   `CompItem` if the project item is a [Composition](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp)
    
*   `FootageNoSolidItem` if the video is a footage file (video or audio or image) but not a solid (which would technically also be footage).
*   `SolidItem` if the project item is a solid
*   `FolderItem` if the project item is a project bin

### type name (localized)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#type-name-localized "Direct link to heading")

A user-readable name for the item type; for example, `Folder`, `Footage`, or `Composition`. These names are application locale-dependent, meaning that they are different depending on the application’s interface language. Note that `Footage` can also be a solid item. To distinguish between solid and other footage, and get language-independent values, use the attribute [Type](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_type)
 instead.

| en\_US | Composition | Folder | Footage |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| de\_DE | Komposition | Ordner | Footage |
| es\_ES | Composición | Carpeta | Material de archivo |
| fr\_FR | Composition | Dossier | Métrage |
| it\_IT | Composizione | Cartella | Metraggio |
| ja\_JP | コンポジション | フォルダー | フッテージ |
| ko\_KR | 컴포지션 | 폴더  | 푸티지 |
| pt\_BR | Composição | Pasta | Gravação |
| ru\_ru | Композиция | Папка | Видеоряд |
| zh\_CN | 合成  | 文件夹 | 素材  |

### use proxy[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_use_proxy "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false`. When true, a proxy is used for the item.

You can set this value if you want to enable or disable an already configured proxy for this project item. To set a new proxy (or remove it) use the attribute [proxy](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_proxy)
 instead.

### used in (comp list) (read-only)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#used-in-comp-list-read-only "Direct link to heading")

A [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 (array) of the [compositions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp)
 which uses this footage item.

### width[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_width "Direct link to heading")

The width of the video in pixels. Only use this if the attribute [has video](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_has_video)
 is `true`. See also the attributes [height](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_height)
 and [size](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_size)
.

Select Project Items[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_item_select "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_ae_item_select](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProjectItems/block_ae_item_select.png)

Use this block to control which items are selected in the project panel. As an argument you can either give a single [Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
 or a [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 (array) of project items.

### Parameters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#parameters "Direct link to heading")

*   `select project items` selects all the given project items and deselects everything else that has been selected before.
*   `add to project selection` selects all the given project items and also keeps everything selected that has been selected before.
*   `remove from project selection` deselects all given project items (and ignores the ones that are not selected).

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_item_select_example "Direct link to heading")

If you select some footage layers in the active composition and then run this script, it will select their corresponding project items in the project panel, too:

![Example: select the project items of selected layers](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_ae_item_select_example.png)

To retrieve a list of the selected footage layer, we use the block [List of Layers](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer#ae_layer_list)
. Also note that Automation Blocks notices that the _Select Project Items_ block expects project items, but you can give it layers instead. Therefore, [according to the rules outlined here](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#accessProjectItem)
, Automation Blocks converts the list of layers into a list of their corresponding project items automatically.

Another example for the _Select Project Items_ block can be found [here](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_get_items_in_bin_example)
.

##### Get/Set Selected Attribute

With the blocks [Get Attribute of Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_get_attribute)
 and [Set Attribute of Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute)
, you can also retrieve and change the selection status of an individual project item (by choosing the attribute [Selected](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_set_attribute_selected)
).

Delete Project Items[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_item_delete "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_ae_item_delete](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProjectItems/block_ae_item_delete.png)

This block deletes project items from the project panel. You can give either a single [Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
 or a [List of Project Items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_get_items_in_bin)
 as an argument.

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#example-1 "Direct link to heading")

This script deletes all project items which are currently selected in the project panel. To create a list of all selected project items, it uses the block [List of Project Items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#block_ae_get_items_in_bin)
.

![Example:Delete all selected items from project panel](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_ae_item_delete_example.png)

Project Item Exists[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems/#ae_item_exists "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_item_exists](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeProjectItems/ae_item_exists.png)

This block returns a [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 which is `true` if the given project item exists.

See also the [Property Exists](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayerProperties#ae_property_exists)
 block.
