# Math | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#)

On this page

Math
====

The blocks in this section are your pocket calculator - use them for all kinds of mathematical calculations.

Number[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_number "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_number](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Math/math_number.png)

This block represents a number.

Arithmetic[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_arithmetic "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_arithmetic](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Math/math_arithmetic.png)

Here you can add, subtract, multiply and divide numbers. Superheroes can also do power calculations with the `^` operator. `2^10` means 2 to the power of 10, for example.

When you combine several math operations, be aware of the order of the operations:

![order of operations](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/math_arithmetic_example_1.png)

In the example on the left, we calculate `(1+2)*3`, whereas on the right we calculate `1+(2*3)`.

Math Function[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_single "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_single](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Math/math_single.png)

This block gives you access to the most commonly used mathematical functions:

*   **square root**
*   **absolute** (makes negative numbers positive and keeps positive numbers unchanged)
*   **\-** (multiplies by -1, i.e. makes negative numbers positive and vise versa)
*   **ln** calculates the natural logarithm of the basis e.
*   **log10** calculates the logarithm of the basis 10.
*   **e^** calculates e to the power of x
*   **10^** calculates 10 to the power of x

Trigonometry[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_trig "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_trig](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Math/math_trig.png)

Calculates trigonometric functions like sine or cosine. The angle is given in _degrees_.

Math Constant[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_constant "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_constant](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Math/math_constant.png)

This block gives you access to various mathematical constants.

Math Number Property[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_number_property "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_number_property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Math/math_number_property.png)

Gives you access to mathematical constants like _pi_ or _e_.

Round Number[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_round "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_round](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Math/math_round.png)

Rounds a point number up. By default, rounds to the nearest number, but you can also force it to always round up or always round down.

Math on List[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_on_list "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_on_list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Math/math_on_list.png)

This block performs math operations on lists (of numbers):

*   _sum_ returns the sum of all items in the list
*   _min_ returns the smallest item in the list
*   _max_ returns the largest item in the list
*   _average_ returns the average of the list items (their sum divided by the number of elements)
*   _median_ returns the [median](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Median)
     of the list items.
*   _modes_ returns a list of the most common item(s) in the list
*   _standard deviation_ returns the [standard deviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_deviation)
     of the list items
*   _random item_ returns a random element from the list

Remainder Of[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_modulo "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_modulo](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Math/math_modulo.png)

Returns the remainder of dividing the two numbers.

Constrain Number[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_constrain "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_constrain](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Math/math_constrain.png)

Constrains the number to be between the specified limits (inclusive).

### Example: constrain user input[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#example-constrain-user-input "Direct link to heading")

![constrain example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/math_constrain_example.png)

This example asks the user to enter a number; the script then makes sure that the resulting number is between 0 and 100. If the user enters something bigger than 100, for example, the result will still be 100 and if the user enters a negative number, the result will be 0.

Random Number[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_random_int_or_float "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_random_int_or_float](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Math/math_random_int_or_float.png)

This block returns a random number in the given range. When you generate integers, the boundaries are inclusive, i.e a random number from 0 to 10 can result in any of those numbers, including 0 and 10.

If you generate point numbers, the right boundary is exclusive, i.e. a floating point number between 0 and 10 can be 0 or 9.9999999 but never 10.

Random Fraction[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_random_float "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_random_float](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Math/math_random_float.png)

This block returns a random point number between 0 (inclusive) and 1 (exclusive). Effectively, this is just a more compact notation for

![random fraction example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/math_random_float_example.png)

*   [Number](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_number)
    
*   [Arithmetic](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_arithmetic)
    
*   [Math Function](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_single)
    
*   [Trigonometry](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_trig)
    
*   [Math Constant](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_constant)
    
*   [Math Number Property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_number_property)
    
*   [Round Number](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_round)
    
*   [Math on List](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_on_list)
    
*   [Remainder Of](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_modulo)
    
*   [Constrain Number](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_constrain)
    *   [Example: constrain user input](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#example-constrain-user-input)
        
*   [Random Number](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_random_int_or_float)
    
*   [Random Fraction](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/math/#math_random_float)

# Lists | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#)

On this page

Lists
=====

Create List[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_create_with "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block lists_create_with](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Lists/lists_create_with.png)

This block creates a list with all the items you give it. Here is an example that creates a list with two items, namely 0.5 and 1:

![create list example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/lists_create_with_example_1.png)

This type of list can, for example, be used to [set the position of a clip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters#pr_component_parameter_set_value)
 (where the first item is used as x and the second item as y value).

If you click on the blue cogwheel icon, you can change the number of list elements (and even reorder them):

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/lists_create_with_add_items.gif)

If you remove all entries, you can also create an empty list:

![create empty list example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/lists_create_with_example_empty.png)

List Repeat[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_repeat "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block lists_repeat](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Lists/lists_repeat.png)

This creates a list with many identical values. These two blocks do exactly the same thing:

![repeat list example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/lists_repeat_example.png)

Length of List[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_length "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block lists_length](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Lists/lists_length.png)

This block returns the length of the list (i.e. the number of items contained in the list).

Is Empty List[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_isEmpty "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block lists_isEmpty](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Lists/lists_isEmpty.png)

This block returns the [Logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` if the given list is empty and `false` otherwise. The following two are identical:

![list empty example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/lists_isEmpty_example.png)

Find in List[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_indexOf "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block lists_indexOf](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Lists/lists_indexOf.png)

This block searches though the items in the given list and when it finds one that has the given value, it returns the (one-based) index of that element. If none of the elements has the given value, it returns 0.

![find in list example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/lists_indexOf_example.png)

This example searches in the list `0,200,50` for the value 50. Since 50 is the third element in the list, the result is 3. If you searched for 60 instead, the result would be 0 (since 60 is not in the list at all).

Get in List[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_getIndex "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block lists_getIndex](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Lists/lists_getIndex.png)

This returns and/or removes the item from the list. If you choose `#`, you need to give the index of the item you want to retrieve, so 1 for the first list item, for example.

Set in List[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_setIndex "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block lists_setIndex](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Lists/lists_setIndex.png)

This block can set (i.e. overwrite) an item in the list or insert a new one. Note that indices are 1-based, so #3 is the third element of the list.

Get Sub-list[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_getSublist "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block lists_getSublist](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Lists/lists_getSublist.png)

Returns a smaller list containing only a subset of the items of the given list.

List From/To Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_split "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block lists_split](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Lists/lists_split.png)

If you have a text that consists of several values separated by a comma (or other delimiter symbol), this block can turn the text into an actual list. These two blocks have exactly the same result:

![split list example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/lists_split_example.png)

Note that the conversion does not remove any empty space symbols around the delimiter. Therefore, if your list elements are separated by a comma and a space, i.e. `item1, item2, item3,...` instead of `item1,item2,item3,...` then you should use `,` (a comma and a space) as separator because otherwise the list entries will have a space at the beginning. Of course, you can also remove whitespace with the [Trim Spaces block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#text_trim)
.

Sort List[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_sort "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block lists_sort](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Lists/lists_sort.png)

This block can sort the items in a list. Numeric is useful for lists of numbers and alphabetic for lists of texts.

*   [Create List](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_create_with)
    
*   [List Repeat](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_repeat)
    
*   [Length of List](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists/#lists_length)

# Files | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#)

On this page

Files
=====

### Accessing a Particular File (or Folder)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#accessFile "Direct link to heading")

Automation Blocks represents files (and folders) with a text which corresponds to the full path to the file on your hard-drive. So whenever a block requires you to provide a file, just give it the path of that file.

The [File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_file)
 and the [Folder block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_folder)
 offer a convenient method to create this type of file path text, but you can use any other method you want to to create this text when you want to access a file. A simple text block would work, too - the following two blocks have exactly the same result:

![File Block vs Text Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_extendscript_file_example.png)

If you want the user to choose a file, you can use the [Open File Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#block_extendscript_open_file_dlg)
 and [Save File Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#block_extendscript_save_file_dlg)
. If you want to create or modify file names, the blocks in the [File Names & Path Chapter](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath)
 will help you to tinker with your path texts.

### Opening & Closing Files[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#opening--closing-files "Direct link to heading")

You don't need to worry about opening or closing files manually — Automation Blocks takes care of everything for you in the background. Whenever you read from or write to a file, Automation Blocks checks if the file is already open and continues reading or writing from the current position. It also ensures that all files are properly closed once the script finishes executing.

For performance reasons, read and write operations on spreadsheet and text files are cached. When you first access a value from a spreadsheet, the entire file is loaded into memory, and all subsequent read or write operations use this cache. The cache is written back to the file only when the script has finished running. Usually, you don't need to think about these details. However, for long-running scripts, it’s important to note that changes to the spreadsheet file will only appear after the script completes — not while it’s still executing.

#### Forcing Files to Close[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#forcing_files_to_close "Direct link to heading")

In most cases, Automation Blocks' caching system improves performance and speeds up your scripts. However, there are situations where you need to ensure that changes are immediately written to disk — for example, if you write a file and then want to [execute](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_execute_file)
 it. Or if you want to [write an sequence preset file and then use that preset in the next block](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro-discussions/create-custom-resolution-sequences-from-a-spreadsheet-using-automation-blocks/m-p/15161360#M552965)
. To guarantee that the file exists on disk before proceeding, you can manually force Automation Blocks to close all open files by running the following command inside an [Execute Code block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther#pr_execute_code)
:

    $._extAutomationBlocks.all.fileCollection.cleanup()

This command forces Automation Blocks to close all currently open files and write any cached changes to disk.

File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_file "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Files/block_extendscript_file.png)

This block represents a file on your hard-drive. In Automation Blocks, a file is just the absolute path of a file represented as a text, so technically this block is the same as a normal text block. But it has this extra folder icon at the end, which allows you to choose a file more conveniently. If you click the icon, an Open File dialog opens and the text of the block is replaced with whatever file you have chosen.

##### Open and Save File Dialogs

If you want to give the user the freedom to choose a file each time the script is executed, you can use the [Open File Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#block_extendscript_open_file_dlg)
 or the [Save File Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#block_extendscript_save_file_dlg)
 instead of the File block.

Embedded File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_embedded_file "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_embedded_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Files/block_extendscript_embedded_file.png)

Embedded files can be used exactly as normal [File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_file)
 blocks, but the actual contents of the file will be saved as part of your block script. This way, you can ensure that the file is always available, as well as if you run the script on other machines, for example.

### Size Limit[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#size-limit "Direct link to heading")

For performance reasons, embedded files cannot be larger than 2MB. They are great to embed

*   [Ae Preset Files](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer#ae_layer_apply_preset)
    ,
*   [Media Encoder Preset Files](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering#block_media_encoder_render)
    
*   [Premiere Pro Sequence Presets](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#block_pr_new_sequence)
    
*   or even small image files.

But they are not suited to embed large video files, for example.

### Technical Background[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#technical-background "Direct link to heading")

Say you create an _Embedded File_ block and select the file `myText.txt` in your documents folder. The block then saves the entire contents of the file as part of your blockscript and when the script is executed, it creates a copy of that file in the following folder

*   _Mac OS_: `~/Library/Application Support/aescripts/automationblocks/`
*   _Windows_: `%AppData%/aescripts/automationblocks/`

Then the script uses this copy of the file, instead of the original from your documents folder.

To open the folder, where the _Embedded File_ block creates its files, you can right-click the block and choose _"Open Embedded Files Folder"_.

Folder[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_folder "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_folder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Files/block_extendscript_folder.png)

This block represents a folder on your hard-drive. In Automation Blocks, a folder is just the absolute path of a folder represented as a text, so technically this block is the same as a normal text block. But it has this extra folder icon at the end which allows you to choose a folder more conveniently. If you click the icon, an Open Folder dialog opens and the text of the block is replaced with whatever folder you have chosen.

##### Open Folder Dialog

If you want to give the user the freedom to choose a folder each time the script is executed, you can use the [Open Folder Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#block_extendscript_open_folder_dlg)
 instead of the Folder block.

List of Files[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_get_files_in_folder "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_get_files_in_folder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Files/block_extendscript_get_files_in_folder.png)

Creates a list of all files (and/or folders) inside the given folder of your hard drive.

Execute File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_execute_file "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_execute_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Files/block_extendscript_execute_file.png)

This block executes the chosen file. This is the same as double-clicking the file in a file browser like Windows Explorer or Mac OS Finder.

You can use this to execute bat files in Windows, for example, or to open images in the system default program for that image type.

Rename File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_rename_file "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_rename_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Files/block_extendscript_rename_file.png)

This block changes the name of a file on your hard-drive. If a file with the new name already exists in the same folder, you can choose if

*   the user should be asked if the existing file should be replaced
*   the existing file should be replaced
*   the file should not be renamed
*   the script should stop with an error

If the old and new name of the file are identical, the block does nothing. So if you batch-update many filenames, you don't need to test for that case explicitly.

##### New in Automation Blocks 1.0.018

This block has been added in Automation Blocks 1.0.018 and does not exist in earlier versions.

Delete File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_delete_file "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_delete_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Files/block_extendscript_delete_file.png)

Deletes a file from your hard-drive. The file is **not** put in the trash.

If you enable the `simulate only` option, then instead of truly deleting the file, the script just writes a message on the console mentioning the file it would have deleted. This is very useful for testing your script during development to ensure you don't delete any files accidentally.

##### Cannot be undone!

You cannot undo the operation and the deleted file will not be in the trash. It is recommended that you prompt the user for permission before deleting (by using the delete _after confirm_ option) and that you use the "simulate only" option while developing your script.

##### Delete before writing to files

The blocks which write content to text or spreadsheet files normally append new data to the existing file. If you want to overwrite the existing file's content instead, simply use this Delete File block before you start writing to the file.

Copy File or Folder[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_copy_file "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_copy_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Files/block_extendscript_copy_file.png)

Copies a single file or the entire contents of a folder to a new location. In the example above, the file `~/Desktop/myFile.png` will be copied to `~/Desktop/target/myFile.png`.

If you copy a folder, note that the contents of the folder is copied, not the folder itself.

![copy folder example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_extendscript_copy_file_example_copy_folder.png)

That means that in this example, if you have a file `~/Desktop/src/myFile.txt`, it will be copied to `~/Desktop/target/myFile.txt` and not to `~/Desktop/target/src/myFile.txt`.

If you copy a single file, as destination you can either give a [File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_file)
 or a [Folder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_folder)
. If you provide a folder, the file is copied to that location and keeps its existing filename. If you provide a file, the copy is renamed accordingly.

![rename example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_extendscript_copy_file_example_rename.png)

In this example, the file `myFile.png` on the desktop is copied into the sub-folder `target` of the desktop and the copy is named `newName.png`.

#### if target folder does not exist[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#if-target-folder-does-not-exist "Direct link to heading")

Here you have the following options:

1.  _create folder_: the target folder and any parent folders are created if necessary
2.  _do nothing_: if the target folder does not exist, nothing is copied and the script continues
3.  _stop with error_: if the target folder does not exist, nothing is copied and the script stops with an error

#### if a file exists[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#if-a-file-exists "Direct link to heading")

If a file already exists in the target location, you have the following options:

1.  replace with new file
2.  keep existing file
3.  keep both (the new copy of an existing file `myFile.png` will be named `myFile copy.png`, and if that file also exists, it will be named `myFile copy 2.png` etc.)

If you copy an entire folder, note that if the target folder exists, the copied content is combined with the existing content and the old content is **not** deleted before the new content is copied (although individual files in the target folder might be overwritten if you choose the _replace with new file_ option).

File Exists[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_exists_file "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_exists_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Files/block_extendscript_exists_file.png)

This block returns a [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 which is `true`, if the file or folder with the given path exists on the hard-drive. If you choose the option _"file"_ and the path exists but is actually a folder, it returns `false` (and analogously the _"folder"_ option is only `true` if the path really corresponds to an existing folder and not a file).

File has Type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_file_has_type "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_file_has_type](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Files/block_extendscript_file_has_type.png)

Checks if the given file has any of the following types (based on the file extension)

*   **video:** 'mov', 'avi', 'mpg', 'mpeg', 'mpe', 'dv', 'qt', 'mp4', 'mxf', 'r3d', 'm1v', 'm2v', 'm4v', 'm2t', 'm2ts', 'mts', 'wmv', 'hvec', 'mkv', 'mk3d', '3gp', 'asf', 'f4v', 'vob', 'cine'
*   **image (bitmap):** 'ptl', 'prtl', 'psd', 'bmp', 'dib', 'rle', 'ico', 'gif', 'cin', 'dpx', 'exr', 'mxr', 'sxr', 'jpeg', 'jpg', 'png', 'iris', 'bw', 'rgb', 'rgba', 'sgi', 'bpx', 'ivb', 'pix', 'targa', 'tga', 'tig', 'tif', 'tiff', 'tim', 'dng', 'psq'
*   **image (vector):** 'ai', 'eps', 'svg', 'pict', 'pct', 'pic', 'pdf'
*   **audio**: 'mp3', 'wav', 'aif', 'aiff', 'ogg', 'wma', 'bwf', 'm4a', 'mka', 'ac3', 'asnd', 'aac'
*   **any media**: any of the above (video, image or audio)
*   **Premiere Pro project**: 'prproj'
*   **After Effects project**: 'aep'

Create Folder[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files/#block_extendscript_create_folder "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_file_has_type](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Files/block_extendscript_create_folder.png)

Creates the given folder on your hard-drive, if it does not yet exist. It also creates the parent folders, if needed.
