# Pr Marker | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#)

On this page

Pr Marker
=========

### Accessing a Particular Marker[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#accessMarker "Direct link to heading")

In contrast to [projects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#accessProject)
, [project items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
, [sequences](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#accessSequence)
 and [clips](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#accessSequenceClip)
, markers are **not** represented as texts in Automation Blocks. Therefore, the only way to access a particular marker is by using the blocks in this section (specifically [For Each Marker](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker#pr_marker_for_all_of_project_item)
 and [Get Marker](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker#pr_marker_get)
).

For Each Marker[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#pr_marker_for_all_of_project_item "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_marker_for_all_of_project_item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMarker/pr_marker_for_all_of_project_item.png)

This block iterates over all markers (or all markers of a particular type) in the given sequence or project item. The blocks you nest inside this block are called one time for each of those markers. During this execution, the respective marker is stored in the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `my marker`.

#### sequence or project item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#sequence-or-project-item "Direct link to heading")

Here you can specify the sequence or project item whose markers you want to modify. You can also provide a clip here; if you do, the block uses the markers of the project item of that clip (which are also the markers that Premiere Pro shows on clips in the timeline).

#### type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#type "Direct link to heading")

With the type filter, you can limit the result to only markers of a particular type. The available types are the same as in Premiere Pro's _Edit Marker_ Dialog:

*   _all markers_
*   _comment marker_
*   _chapter marker_
*   _segmentation marker_
*   _web link_
*   _Flash Clue Point_

Get Marker[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#pr_marker_get "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_marker_get](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMarker/pr_marker_get.png)

#### marker number[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#marker-number "Direct link to heading")

The numbering of markers starts with `1` for the first marker. To figure out how many markers (or how many markers of a particular type) a sequence or project item has, you can use the [Get Number of Markers Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker#pr_marker_get_num)
. If you want to do something with each marker, the [For Each Marker Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker#pr_marker_for_all_of_project_item)
 is the much faster and easier solution.

#### type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#type-1 "Direct link to heading")

With the type filter you can limit the results to only the markers of a particular type. If you choose `comment marker`, for example, marker `1` is the first marker of type comment. The available types are the same as in Premiere Pro's _Edit Marker_ Dialog:

*   _all markers_
*   _comment marker_
*   _chapter marker_
*   _segmentation marker_
*   _web link_
*   _Flash Clue Point_

#### sequence/project item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#sequenceproject-item "Direct link to heading")

Here you can specify the sequence or project item whose markers you want to modify. You can also provide a clip here. If you do, the block uses the marker of the project item of that clip (which are also the markers that Premiere Pro shows on clips in the timeline).

##### use variables

Usually, you want to do several things with the same marker (like changing both its name and comment, or start and end time). Instead of creating one copy of this block each time you need to access the marker, it is better to use this block just one time and store the result in a [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
. Then you can use the variable to perform all the changes on the marker.

This not only creates cleaner code, it also calculates much faster.

Get Number of Markers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#pr_marker_get_num "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_marker_get_num](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMarker/pr_marker_get_num.png)

This block returns the number of markers of the given sequence, project item or clip.

#### type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#type-2 "Direct link to heading")

With the type filter you can limit the result to only markers of a particular type:

*   _all markers_
*   _comment marker_
*   _chapter marker_
*   _segmentation marker_
*   _web link_
*   _Flash Clue Point_

Create Marker[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#pr_marker_create "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_marker_create](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMarker/pr_marker_create.png)

Creates a new marker in the given sequence, project item or clip at the given time.

#### Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#time "Direct link to heading")

The time is given in seconds (see the blocks in [Pr Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime)
 for converting between different time and timecode formats). For sequences, a time of `0` corresponds to the beginning of the sequence, and for clips and project items, a time of `0` corresponds to the beginning of the corresponding footage.

#### Save it in Variable[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#save-it-in-variable "Direct link to heading")

The block saves the created marker in the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `my marker`. So, if you want to set marker properties like the name, comment, type, duration, color etc., you can use this variable with the [Set Property of Marker Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker#pr_marker_set_property)
.

Get Property of Marker[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#pr_marker_get_property "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_marker_get_property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMarker/pr_marker_get_property.png)

This block can retrieve any of the properties of a marker listed below. As a marker, you need to give a marker [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
, as created with the [For Each Marker Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker#pr_marker_for_all_of_project_item)
, the [Get Marker Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker#pr_marker_get)
, or the [Create Marker Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker#pr_marker_create)
.

### start time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#start-time "Direct link to heading")

The start time of the marker in seconds. For sequence markers, `0` is the beginning of the sequence, for project items and clips, `0` is the beginning of the corresponding footage.

### end time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#end-time "Direct link to heading")

The end time of the marker in seconds. For sequence markers, `0` is the beginning of the sequence, for project items and clips, `0` is the beginning of the corresponding footage.

### duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#duration "Direct link to heading")

The duration of the marker in seconds. If you set the duration, it will change the end time and keep the start time unchanged.

### name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#name "Direct link to heading")

The name of the marker.

### comments[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#comments "Direct link to heading")

The comments of the marker. In contrast to the marker name, this text can have multiple lines - you can use the _multiline text block_ in the _Text_ section for that.

### type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#type-3 "Direct link to heading")

The type of the marker. One of these texts:

*   `Comment`
*   `Chapter`
*   `Segmentation`
*   `WebLink`

### color[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#color "Direct link to heading")

The color of the marker. One of these texts:

*   `Green`
*   `Red`
*   `Purple`
*   `Orange`
*   `Yellow`
*   `White`
*   `Blue`
*   `Cyan`

### color index[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#color-index "Direct link to heading")

The color index - a number between `0` and `7`, where `0` represents `Green` and `7`represents `Cyan`. If you set this value, you can also give higher (or even negative) numbers, and Automation Blocks will then start to loop the colors. So `8`is the same as `0`, `9`is the same as `1`, etc.

### web link URL[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#web-link-url "Direct link to heading")

The web link URL (only for markers of type `WebLink`). If you set this value, the marker type is switched to `WebLink` automatically.

### web link frame target[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#web-link-frame-target "Direct link to heading")

The web link frame target (only for markers of type `WebLink`). If you set this value, the marker type is switched to `WebLink` automatically.

### guid (unique ID)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#guid-unique-id "Direct link to heading")

The unique identifier of the marker, created at the time of creation.

Set Property of Marker[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#pr_marker_set_property "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_marker_set_property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMarker/pr_marker_set_property.png)

This block can set all kinds of properties of a marker. For a detailed description of which properties can be set, read the documentation of the [Get Property of Marker Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker#pr_marker_get_property)
.

Delete Marker[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#pr_marker_delete_one "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_marker_delete_one](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMarker/pr_marker_delete_one.png)

This block deletes a single marker of the given sequence, project item or clip. Marker numbers start with `1` for the first marker.

#### type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#type-4 "Direct link to heading")

If you choose a particular type of marker, the marker number only takes markers of this type into account. So, if you choose `comment marker` instead of `all markers`, `3` denotes the third comment marker, for example.

Delete All Markers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker/#pr_marker_delete_all "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_marker_delete_all](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMarker/pr_marker_delete_all.png)

This block deletes all markers within the given sequence, project item or clip. You can also choose to only delete all markers of a particular type.

# Logic | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#)

On this page

Logic
=====

Overview[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#overview "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Logic blocks are about yes/no questions. Is this project item a sequence? Has this clip been used in a sequence at least once? In programming languages, these kinds of questions can be formulated with (boolean) logic and the answer to the question is either the value `true` (for yes) or `false` (for no).

In Automation Blocks, logic values have pointed edges - in contrast to texts or numbers, which have rounded edges:

![Block shapes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/logic_shape_example.png)

So whenever you see these pointed shapes, you know that a logic value (or yes/no question) is expected at that point.

If[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#controls_if "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block controls_if](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Logic/controls_if.png)

The _if_ block is the the most essential block in the logic category. Whereas all other logic blocks are about formulating logic questions, the _if_ block is about what should actually be done if the answer to the question is yes (or what else should be done when the answer is no).

At the top of the _if_ block, you need to insert a logic value, aka a yes/no question, and the blocks you plug inside the if block are only executed if the answer to this question is yes (i.e. `true`). This example writes `the sequence has markers` to the console if the active sequence has at least one marker (and otherwise does nothing):

![Block shapes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_if_example_1.png)

### If ... Else[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#controls_if_else_1 "Direct link to heading")

When you click on the blue cogwheel icon on the top left of the _if_ block, you can also add an _else_ section too. The _else_ section will only be executed if the answer to the question is no (i.e. `false`). Let's extend our example so it outputs `the sequence has NO markers` if the sequences does NOT have at least one marker:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/if_else_example.gif)

### If ... Else If ... Else[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#if--else-if--else "Direct link to heading")

Finally, you can have an _if_ block that asks multiple questions by adding _else if_ sections to the block. When the answer to the first question is `false` (and only in that case), it does not go to the _else_ block immediately, but first asks the second question. Here is an example:

![Block shapes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_if_example_2.png)

This example checks the type of the project item with name `my item name`. The _if_ block executes exactly one of the "write to console" blocks in order to state what type the project item actually is.

Compare[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#logic_compare "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block logic_compare](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Logic/logic_compare.png)

This block can compare two numbers. Use it to ask questions like "Are the numbers equal?" or "Is the first number greater than the second number?".

### Example: Has Footage been used?[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#example-has-footage-been-used "Direct link to heading")

![Block logic_compare](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/logic_compare_example.png)

Here we use the Compare block to ask if a footage item has been used at least once ("Is the number of times the footage item has been used greater or equal to one?"):

And/Or[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#logic_operation "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block logic_operation](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Logic/logic_operation.png)

With this block you can combine two questions into one. If you choose "and", the result is only `true` if both questions are answered with `true`. For "or" the result is `true` if at least one of the two questions is answered with `true`.

![or example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/logic_operation_example.png)

This example returns `true` if either the video or the audio of the footage item (or both audio and video) has been used at least once.

Not[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#logic_negate "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block logic_negate](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Logic/logic_negate.png)

This block turns a `true` into a `false` and vice versa.

### Example: End of Textfile[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#example-end-of-textfile "Direct link to heading")

![or example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/logic_negate_example.png)

The [End of File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText#extendscript_text_file_eof)
 block returns `true` if the script has read the entire contents of the text file already. If you plug it into a _Not_ block, it returns `true` if there is still content left to read.

Boolean (Logic Value)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#logic_boolean "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block logic_boolean](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Logic/logic_boolean.png)

This block always returns `true` or always returns `false` (depending on which of the two you have chosen in the dropdown).

Checkbox[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#block_checkbox "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_checkbox](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Logic/block_checkbox.png)

This block is identical to the normal boolean block, just with a different visual representation. If the checkbox is checked, it has the value `true` and otherwise `false`.

Null[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#logic_null "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block logic_null](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Logic/logic_null.png)

The JavaScript value [null](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/null)
, which represents the intentional absense of any object value. You can also use Null directly in a logic test (as the condition of an [if block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic#controls_if)
, for example). In a logic test, Null will behave like `false` whereas any other object behaves like `true`. For example, see the [Ternary Test](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic#logic_ternary)
 below.

Ternary Test[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#logic_ternary "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block logic_ternary](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Logic/logic_ternary.png)

This block is similar to an [if...else](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic#controls_if_else_1)
 statement. The difference is the following: with the _if...else_ block, you can plug in two block stacks (where one block stack is executed when the condition is `true` and the other when the condition is `false`). In the _Ternary Test_ you plug in two values instead of two block stacks and the first value is returned if the condition is `true` and the second is returned if the value is `false`. In other words, this is a mini-version of the _if....else_ block that is just about calculating a single value instead of executing arbitrary blocks.

![simple ternary example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/logic_ternary_example_simple.png)

In this example, the block on the left returns `first text` whereas the block on the right returns `second text`.

### Example: null and markers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#example-null-and-markers "Direct link to heading")

This script prints on the console `first sequence marker is at 5 seconds` if the active sequence has its first marker at that time and if the active sequence has no marker, it prints `sequence has no marker` instead.

![Complex ternary and null example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/logic_ternary_example_complex.png)

In the first line, we save the first marker of the active sequence in the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `my marker`. If the sequence has no marker, we give the variable the value `null` instead, to indicate that there is no such marker object.

When we want to use the marker, we need to first check if the marker actually exists or is `null`. To do this, we test the `my marker` variable in an _if_ block. Since `null` is equivalent to `false` (whereas any other object, like a valid marker, is equivalent to to `true`), the first _write to console_ block is only executed if a marker exists, and otherwise the second one only.

# Infos for ChatGPT | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#)

On this page

Infos for ChatGPT
=================

This guide explains how ChatGPT and other AI chatbots can generate **Automation Blocks** scripts. These scripts are XML files that users can load directly into **Automation Blocks for Premiere Pro** or **Automation Blocks for After Effects**.

If You Are Human[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#if-you-are-human "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you're a human looking to get ChatGPT to write a specific Automation Blocks script for you, follow these best practices to achieve high-quality results:

*   Use a reasoning model like **ChatGPT o4-mini-high**.
*   Start your prompt with the following template:

    Please create a script for Automation Blocks for Premiere Pro as a downloadable xml file.  Follow the details explained at https://mamoworld.com/chatGPT. Read that entire page carefully and follow every advice given there. The script should do the following:...

*   If the result **does not work as expected**, **don’t give up**. Instead, explain to ChatGPT **exactly what’s wrong**, including:
    
    *   What the script was supposed to do
    *   What actually happened
    *   Any error messages or unexpected behavior
    *   What you already tried to fix it
    
    The more precisely you describe the issue, the better ChatGPT can help you resolve it.
    

General Strategy for Chatbots to Create Automation Blocks Scripts[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#general-strategy-for-chatbots-to-create-automation-blocks-scripts "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Automation Blocks** for **Adobe Premiere Pro** and **Adobe After Effects** is built on **Google Blockly XML**. The core strategy for generating scripts is to use a special block that can run arbitrary Adobe ExtendScript code:

*   For Premiere Pro: `<block type="pr_execute_code">`
*   For After Effects: `<block type="ae_execute_code">`

This means the most reliable and straightforward method is:

1.  First, write the entire script logic in **Adobe ExtendScript**.
2.  Then wrap that code inside the appropriate **execute code** block.

💡 If your script should include a user interface (e.g., input fields, checkboxes, or dropdowns), you'll need to combine the execute block with additional input\_arg blocks. For details, see the Creating User Interfaces section below.

#### Template for Premiere Pro[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#template-for-premiere-pro "Direct link to heading")

    <xml xmlns="https://developers.google.com/blockly/xml">  <block type="pr_execute_code" id="%UNIQUE_ID%">    <mutation items="0" resultType="none"/>    <field name="RESULT_TYPE">none</field>    <value name="CODE">      <shadow type="text_multiline">        <field name="TEXT">          <!--            1. Replace %YOUR_ESCAPED_CODE% with your ExtendScript,               but first XML-escape every special character:                 &  → &amp;                 <  → &lt;                 >  → &gt;                 "  → &quot;                 '  → &#39;            2. Turn each line break into &#10; (or leave literal newlines               if your CMS preserves them).                        Example:              var binName = "stock footage";            becomes:              var binName = &quot;stock footage&quot;;              ...          -->          %YOUR_ESCAPED_CODE%        </field>      </shadow>    </value>  </block></xml>

For After Effects, use this template

#### Template for After Effects[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#template-for-after-effects "Direct link to heading")

    <xml xmlns="https://developers.google.com/blockly/xml">  <block type="ae_execute_code" id="%UNIQUE_ID%">    <mutation items="0" resultType="none"/>    <field name="RESULT_TYPE">none</field>    <value name="CODE">      <shadow type="text_multiline">        <field name="TEXT">          <!--            Same rules as above:             • & → &amp;             • < → &lt;             • > → &gt;             • " → &quot;             • ' → &#39;          -->          %YOUR_ESCAPED_CODE%        </field>      </shadow>    </value>  </block></xml>

#### Key encoding steps[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#key-encoding-steps "Direct link to heading")

1.  Escape XML specials in your ExtendScript:

*   `&` → `&amp;` (and of course `&&` by `&amp;&amp;`)
*   `<` → `&lt;`
*   `>` → `&gt;`
*   `"` → `&quot;`
*   `'` → `&#39;`

2.  Paste the resulting single string into the … placeholder.

With this in place, any valid ExtendScript you write will be correctly wrapped, escaped and ready for Automation Blocks to parse next time.

Creating User Interfaces[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#creating-user-interfaces "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Automation Blocks scripts can include custom user interfaces. When the script is loaded, the interface is displayed in the **Inputs Panel** of Automation Blocks, allowing users to interact with the script via text fields, checkboxes, dropdowns, and more.

### Basic Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#basic-example "Direct link to heading")

The following example shows how to create a very simple UI for **Premiere Pro**: a single-line text field labeled “text to alert” with a default value of “Hello World”. When the script runs, it passes the value from the input into the `pr_execute_code` block and alerts it using ExtendScript.

#### Premiere Pro Version[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#premiere-pro-version "Direct link to heading")

    <xml xmlns="https://developers.google.com/blockly/xml">    <block type="pr_execute_code" id="%iY3K8}7bn%l.Zc]]6Zp">        <mutation items="1" resultType="none"></mutation>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_0">userText</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_0">other</field>        <field name="RESULT_TYPE">none</field>        <value name="ADD0">            <block type="input_arg" id="(5;552lWsig;%)81`7l)">                <mutation inputType="TEXT_SINGLELINE"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">TEXT_SINGLELINE</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">text to alert</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD">Hello World</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="CODE">            <shadow type="text_multiline" id="ER6sdOaHs09=6shK54:m">                <field name="TEXT">alert(userText)</field>            </shadow>        </value>    </block></xml>

In this example:

*   A text input labeled **"text to alert"** is defined using an `input_arg` block.
    
*   Its value is passed to the global variable `userText`.
    
*   The `pr_execute_code block` uses `alert(userText)` to display it.
    

#### After Effects Version[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#after-effects-version "Direct link to heading")

The same UI setup works in **After Effects** by using the `ae_execute_code` block instead:

    <xml xmlns="https://developers.google.com/blockly/xml">    <block type="ae_execute_code" id="*(K2*X#o%hhJ0#X=iI|v">        <mutation items="1" resultType="none"></mutation>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_0">userText</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_0">other</field>        <field name="RESULT_TYPE">none</field>        <value name="ADD0">            <block type="input_arg" id="zABhfFxR/eVIzn6~i%mW">                <mutation inputType="TEXT_SINGLELINE"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">TEXT_SINGLELINE</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">text to alert</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD">Hello World</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="CODE">            <shadow type="text_multiline" id="aYiy?/U)k8G:02-Ix7(1">                <field name="TEXT">alert(userText)</field>            </shadow>        </value>    </block></xml>

In both cases, the value of the input field is available in the ExtendScript code via the global variable userText.

### Comprehensive Example Featuring All Available UI Elements[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#comprehensive-example-featuring-all-available-ui-elements "Direct link to heading")

To create more advanced user interfaces, you can add multiple `input_arg` blocks to your script. Each input defines one UI control, such as text fields, checkboxes, dropdowns, or file/folder pickers.

The following example for **Premiere Pro** includes **all available UI elements**. The corresponding values are passed into the `pr_execute_code` block as global variables.

    <xml xmlns="https://developers.google.com/blockly/xml">    <block type="pr_execute_code" id="%iY3K8}7bn%l.Zc]]6Zp" x="17" y="37">        <mutation items="9" resultType="none"></mutation>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_0">mySingleLineText</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_0">other</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_1">myMultiLineText</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_1">other</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_2">myInt</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_2">other</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_3">myFloat</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_3">other</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_4">myCheckBox</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_4">other</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_5">myDropDown</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_5">other</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_6">myOpenFilePath</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_6">other</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_7">mySaveFilePath</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_7">other</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_8">myFolderPath</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_8">other</field>        <field name="RESULT_TYPE">none</field>        <value name="ADD0">            <block type="input_arg" id="(5;552lWsig;%)81`7l)">                <mutation inputType="TEXT_SINGLELINE"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">TEXT_SINGLELINE</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">sample single line</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD">Hello World</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="ADD1">            <block type="input_arg" id="JJ[;v0]Y5=ZEi1x^ZAx1">                <mutation inputType="TEXT_MULTILINE"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">TEXT_MULTILINE</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">sample multi line</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD">line 1&amp;#10;line 2</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="ADD2">            <block type="input_arg" id="$g#6:9MaCLyjfc;flJlO">                <mutation inputType="NUMBER_INT"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">NUMBER_INT</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">sample int</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD">0</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="ADD3">            <block type="input_arg" id="krdEOpOs#|LA%{NO[Ker">                <mutation inputType="NUMBER_FLOAT"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">NUMBER_FLOAT</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">sample float</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD">0</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="ADD4">            <block type="input_arg" id="k|#)5eQvX7-Ic]20@e4+">                <mutation inputType="CHECKBOX"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">CHECKBOX</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">sample checkbox</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD">TRUE</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="ADD5">            <block type="input_arg" id="nToJwv5vHbl5A1:zW;tQ">                <mutation inputType="DROPDOWN"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">DROPDOWN</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">sample dropdown</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD">option 1|option 2|option 3</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="ADD6">            <block type="input_arg" id="=SOky3W[L*^Tg7cU-)|w" inline="false">                <mutation inputType="OPEN_FILE"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">OPEN_FILE</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">sample open file</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD"></field>                <field name="NO_FILE_BEHAVIOR">CONTINUE</field>                <field name="FILE_DOES_NOT_EXIST_BEHAVIOR">CONTINUE</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="ADD7">            <block type="input_arg" id="B+Ae9,62M)o_-{UyFD,Z" inline="false">                <mutation inputType="SAVE_FILE"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">SAVE_FILE</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">sample save file</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD"></field>                <field name="NO_FILE_BEHAVIOR">STOP</field>                <field name="PARENT_FOLDER_NOT_EXISTS_BEHAVIOR">STOP</field>                <field name="FILE_EXISTS_BEHAVIOR">CONFIRM_DELETE</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="ADD8">            <block type="input_arg" id="Z6dYS8b!0aWomPU,j7%;" inline="false">                <mutation inputType="CHOOSE_FOLDER"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">CHOOSE_FOLDER</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">sample folder picker</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD"></field>                <field name="NO_FILE_BEHAVIOR">CONTINUE</field>                <field name="FILE_DOES_NOT_EXIST_BEHAVIOR">CONTINUE</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="CODE">            <shadow type="text_multiline" id="ER6sdOaHs09=6shK54:m">                <field name="TEXT">alert("sample single line value is " +                    mySingleLineText);&amp;#10;alert("sample multi line value is "+                    myMultiLineText);&amp;#10;alert("sample int value is                    "+myInt);&amp;#10;alert("sample float value is "+myFloat);&amp;#10;alert("sample                    checkbox value is "+ (myCheckBox ? "checked":"unchecked"));&amp;#10;alert("in                    the dropdown you have chosen the text                    "+myDropDown);&amp;#10;&amp;#10;if(myOpenFilePath =="") alert("you didn't pick a                    file in the sample open file picker")&amp;#10;else {&amp;#10; var file = new                    File(myOpenFilePath);&amp;#10; alert("you picked the file with path                    "+myOpenFilePath+ " to be opened and it "+ (file.exists? "exists" : "does not                    exist"));&amp;#10;}&amp;#10;&amp;#10;if(mySaveFilePath == "") alert("you didn't                    pick a file in the sample save file picker")&amp;#10;else {&amp;#10; var file =                    new File(mySaveFilePath);&amp;#10; alert("you want to sav to the file path                    "+mySaveFilePath+ " and this file "+ (file.exists? "exists" : "does not                    exist"));&amp;#10;}&amp;#10;if(myFolderPath == "") alert("you didn't pick a                    folder in the sample folder picker")&amp;#10;else {&amp;#10; var folder = new                    Folder(myFolderPath);&amp;#10; alert("you picked the folder with path                    "+myFolderPath+ " and this folder "+ (folder.exists? "exists" : "does not                    exist"));&amp;#10;&amp;#10;}</field>            </shadow>        </value>    </block></xml>\
\
### What This Example Does[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#what-this-example-does "Direct link to heading")\
\
This example generates a UI containing:\
\
*   A single-line text field\
*   A multiline text field\
*   Integer number input\
*   Float number input\
*   A checkbox\
*   A dropdown with options: `"option 1"`, `"option 2"`, `"option 3"`\
*   A file picker to select a file to open\
*   A file picker to select a file path for saving\
*   A folder picker\
\
Each UI value is passed into a global variable and used inside the ExtendScript to display alerts with the input values.\
\
This example is helpful if you want to:\
\
*   Understand how to define different input types\
*   Learn how to bind UI input values to ExtendScript variables\
*   See the structure of a more complex UI setup\
\
### Passing UI Element Values into the Execute Code Block[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#passing-ui-element-values-into-the-execute-code-block "Direct link to heading")\
\
Execute Code blocks may contain tags like `<value name="ADD0"><block type="input_arg">DETAILS OF INPUT HERE</block</value>`. Use "ADD0" for the first input, "ADD1" for the second one, etc. If you have a `<value name="ADD0">` in your execute code block, for example, you must also have `<field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_0">SOME VARIABLE NAME HERE</field>` and `<field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_0">other</field>`. Before the ExtendScript code is executed, Automation Blocks then creates a global variable of the given name and assigns the value of the block contained in ADD0 to it. With the "FIELD\_VAR\_TYPE" you can specify that the result of the block code should be converted to a particular object before binding it to the global variable. But usually you can keep this as "other", which means no conversion.\
\
Also, the execute code block must contain a mutation tag like "", which describes the number of variables. So if you have entries ADD0,...,ADD5 you need the tag to be "".\
\
### Passing UI Element Values into the Execute Code Block[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#passing-ui-element-values-into-the-execute-code-block-1 "Direct link to heading")\
\
To pass user input values into your ExtendScript code, each `input_arg` block must be properly connected to the `execute_code` block using three key components:\
\
1.  **Value Tag**  \
    Each input must be included in a `<value name="ADDn">` tag (where `n` is the input index, starting from 0):\
    \
        <value name="ADD0">  <block type="input_arg">...</block></value>\
    \
2.  **Field Variable Mapping**  \
    You must define a corresponding variable for each input in the block using:\
    \
\
    <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_0">yourVariableName</field><field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_0">other</field>\
\
    This tells Automation Blocks to create a global variable (e.g., `yourVariableName`) and assign it the value from the `ADD0` input block.\
\
*   `FIELD_VAR_NAME_0` matches `ADD0`\
*   `FIELD_VAR_TYPE_0` is typically set to `"other"` (no type conversion)\
\
3.  **Mutation Tag**  \
    The `<mutation>` tag declares how many inputs/variables are connected. For example, if you use `ADD0`, `ADD1`, and `ADD2`, you must set:\
\
       <mutation items="3" resultType="none"></mutation>\
\
> 💡 All three components—`<value name="ADDn">`, `FIELD_VAR_NAME_n`, and `mutation items="..."`—must be aligned. If any part is missing or the counts don't match, the script may fail or behave unpredictably.\
\
### More Examples[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#more-examples "Direct link to heading")\
\
Here’s a simple example that demonstrates how to collect two integer inputs from the user and alert their sum:\
\
    <xml xmlns="https://developers.google.com/blockly/xml">    <block type="pr_execute_code" id="?6ou@P!7pk2.X3C!A|nK" x="-412" y="293">        <mutation items="2" resultType="none"></mutation>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_0">num1</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_0">other</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_NAME_1">num2</field>        <field name="FIELD_VAR_TYPE_1">other</field>        <field name="RESULT_TYPE">none</field>        <value name="ADD0">            <shadow type="text" id="kA$mZBxg)(4WbxlI/8ph">                <field name="TEXT">variable value</field>            </shadow>            <block type="input_arg" id="}}5~D!hkf4/E?:b.=?Fi">                <mutation inputType="NUMBER_INT"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">NUMBER_INT</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">first number</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD">0</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="ADD1">            <block type="input_arg" id="]KgWl(VXhGM0iugtlJWA">                <mutation inputType="NUMBER_INT"></mutation>                <field name="INPUT_TYPE">NUMBER_INT</field>                <field name="INPUT_LABEL">second number</field>                <field name="DEFAULT FIELD">0</field>            </block>        </value>        <value name="CODE">            <shadow type="text_multiline" id="=|xGua9(i-iN^f#%3phN">                <field name="TEXT">var sum =                    num1+num2;&amp;#10;alert("the sum is "+sum);</field>            </shadow>        </value>    </block></xml>

In this script:

*   Two number inputs are created using `input_arg` blocks with labels **"first number"** and **"second number"**.
*   Their values are passed into the global variables `num1` and `num2`.
*   The ExtendScript code calculates their sum and displays it with `alert("the sum is " + sum)`.

* * *

General Block Attributes[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#general-block-attributes "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

All `<block>` tags must include a unique `id` attribute — typically a random string.  
Top-level blocks may also include optional `x` and `y` attributes to define their position on the canvas. These coordinates are **purely visual** and have no effect on the program logic.

* * *

Tips for Writing Script Logic[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#tips-for-writing-script-logic "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Use `log()` for Debugging[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#use-log-for-debugging "Direct link to heading")

To output messages to the **Automation Blocks console panel**, use this function inside your ExtendScript code:

    $._extAutomationBlocks.all.log("My log message here");

This is especially useful for debugging and troubleshooting when users report unexpected script behavior. Logging intermediate steps makes it easier to trace what's going on.

### Avoid Skipping Items When Modifying Collections[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#avoid-skipping-items-when-modifying-collections "Direct link to heading")

If you're moving or deleting items in a collection (e.g., clips in a bin or on a track), iterate in reverse order. This helps avoid accidentally skipping elements. For example:

*   If you move the second item in a bin, the third one becomes the new second.
*   If you loop forward, this could cause elements to be skipped.

### File Input and Output: Always Provide a UI if No Method Is Specified[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#file-input-and-output-always-provide-a-ui-if-no-method-is-specified "Direct link to heading")

When the user asks for a script that **reads from** or **writes to** a file or folder — but does **not** specify how the file should be selected — the chatbot should **create a user interface for it** using the `input_arg` blocks.

#### Why This Matters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#why-this-matters "Direct link to heading")

Hardcoding file paths is:

*   Not portable across different systems
*   Often not what the user intends
*   Prone to errors and user frustration

Instead, always assume that the user wants to be prompted to pick a file or folder.

#### How to Do It[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#how-to-do-it "Direct link to heading")

Use the appropriate input type in an `input_arg` block:

| Task | Recommended Input Type |
| --- | --- |
| Select a file to open | `OPEN_FILE` |
| Select a file to save to | `SAVE_FILE` |
| Select a folder | `CHOOSE_FOLDER` |

For example, to let the user pick a file to read, use:

    <block type="input_arg" id="=SOky3W[L*^Tg7cU-)|w" inline="false">    <mutation inputType="OPEN_FILE"></mutation>    <field name="INPUT_TYPE">OPEN_FILE</field>    <field name="INPUT_LABEL">sample open file</field>    <field name="DEFAULT FIELD"></field>    <field name="NO_FILE_BEHAVIOR">CONTINUE</field>    <field name="FILE_DOES_NOT_EXIST_BEHAVIOR">CONTINUE</field></block>\
\
You must also make sure that:\
\
*   The input is passed as a global variable to the `execute_code` block\
*   The script logic handles cases where the user did not select a file (e.g., variable is empty)\
\
#### Example Use Case[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#example-use-case "Direct link to heading")\
\
If a user says:\
\
> "Create a script that exports a list of clip names to a file"\
\
…but does **not** mention a file path or method for choosing the destination, the chatbot should automatically create a **Save File Picker** UI using `inputType="SAVE_FILE"`.\
\
> ✅ Always prioritize flexibility and usability — if a path isn't specified, prompt the user with a UI.\
\
### No Top-Level `return` Statements in ExtendScript Code[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#no-top-level-return-statements-in-extendscript-code "Direct link to heading")\
\
When writing ExtendScript for an `execute_code` block in Automation Blocks, **do not use `return` at the top level** of your code.\
\
#### Why?[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#why "Direct link to heading")\
\
Automation Blocks uses JavaScript's `eval()` function to execute the ExtendScript code. In JavaScript, `eval()` **does not allow `return` statements at the top level** — doing so will throw a syntax error.\
\
#### What to Do Instead[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#what-to-do-instead "Direct link to heading")\
\
If you need to exit early from a script (e.g., because a file couldn’t be opened), wrap your logic in a function or use conditional blocks.\
\
#### ❌ Invalid Example (will cause a syntax error):[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#-invalid-example-will-cause-a-syntax-error "Direct link to heading")\
\
    if (!outputFile.open("w")) {    alert("Cannot open file: " + filePath);    return;}\
\
#### ✅ Valid Alternative:[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#-valid-alternative "Direct link to heading")\
\
    if (!outputFile.open("w")) {    alert("Cannot open file: " + filePath);} else {    // Continue with the rest of the logic here}\
\
✅ Tip: If your script is large and needs structured flow control, consider wrapping the entire logic in a function and calling it explicitly.\
\
Output Format: Don't Show XML — Provide a Download Link[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#output-format-dont-show-xml--provide-a-download-link "Direct link to heading")\
\
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\
\
### Output Format[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#output-format "Direct link to heading")\
\
When generating scripts or other code for Automation Blocks, the assistant **must**:\
\
1.  Write the code into a `.xml` file.\
2.  Provide a direct download link to that file (e.g. `sandbox:/mnt/data/<filename>.xml`).\
3.  **Do not** paste the raw XML inline in the chat.\
\
When ChatGPT (or any chatbot) creates an Automation Blocks script, it should **not** display the raw XML code to the end user.\
\
Instead, it should:\
\
1.  **Save the XML as a downloadable file**, ideally named based on the script's purpose (e.g., `moveStockFootage.xml`).\
2.  **Provide a download link** to that file.\
\
This is important because:\
\
*   Raw XML is hard to read and error-prone to copy-paste.\
*   Automation Blocks expects `.xml` files to be imported or placed in specific locations.\
\
### How Users Should Use the XML File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#how-users-should-use-the-xml-file "Direct link to heading")\
\
After downloading the file, users have **two options** to use it:\
\
#### Option 1: Load the File Manually[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#option-1-load-the-file-manually "Direct link to heading")\
\
1.  Open Automation Blocks in Premiere Pro or After Effects.\
2.  Click the **Load** button in the Automation Blocks panel.\
3.  Select the downloaded `.xml` file.\
\
#### Option 2: Add the File to the User Library[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#option-2-add-the-file-to-the-user-library "Direct link to heading")\
\
1.  Move the `.xml` file into your **Automation Blocks User Library** folder.\
2.  Restart Automation Blocks to make the script appear in the library.\
\
To learn where your User Library is located, see:  \
👉 [Where is the User Library?](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#where-is-the-user-library)\
\
> ⚠️ After adding files to the User Library manually, **Automation Blocks must be restarted** to detect the new files.\
\
More Resources[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/forChatGPT/#more-resources "Direct link to heading")\
\

# Loops | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#)

On this page

Loops
=====

Overview[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#overview "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Loops allow you to repeat a particular task many times. For a more detailed explanation, see the section [My First Loop](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/searchReplaceProjectItems#firstLoop)
 of the tutorial "_Search & Replace in Project Items_"

Specialized Loops[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#specialized-loops "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The blocks in this section are general purpose loops, but you'll find some more specialized loops in other sections:

*   [loop over all lines of a text file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText#extendscript_text_file_for_all_lines)
    
*   [loop over all rows/columns of a spreadsheet](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesSpreadsheet#spreadsheet_for_each_row)
    
*   [loop over all matches of a regular expression in a text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#string_for_all_regex_matches)
    

**Premiere Pro**

*   [loop over all open Pr projects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#block_pr_for_all_open_projects)
    
*   [loop over Pr project items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#block_pr_for_all_items_in_bin)
    
*   [loop over clips in a sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#block_pr_for_all_sequence_items)
    
*   [loop over markers of a clip or sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker#pr_marker_for_all_of_project_item)
    
*   [loop over parameters of a clip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters#block_pr_for_all_component_params_of_clip)
    

**After Effects**

*   [loop over Ae project items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems#block_ae_for_all_items_in_bin)
    
*   [loop over frames of a composition](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp#for_each_frame)
    
*   [loop over layers of a composition](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer#ae_layer_for_all)
    
*   [loop over layers with a particular source item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems#block_ae_for_all_layers_with_source_item)
    
*   [loop over properties of layers](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayerProperties#ae_property_for_all)
    

Repeat Loop[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#controls_repeat_ext "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block controls_repeat_ext](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Loops/controls_repeat_ext.png)

This loop just executes the blocks you plug inside it as many times as you want. It is the simplest loop and therefore useful to understand the concept of loop blocks. However, in practice you will use the [For Loop](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops#controls_forcontrols_for)
 much more often, which is very similar, but more powerful.

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#example "Direct link to heading")

![Block controls_repeat_ext](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_repeat_ext_example.png)

This example prints the following output to the console:

    I am firstthis block is repeatedthis block is repeatedthis block is repeatedI am last

As you can see, the block that is plugged into the block is executed three times, whereas the other blocks above and below are just executed once.

While/Until Loop[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#controls_whileUntil "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block controls_whileUntil](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Loops/controls_whileUntil.png)

In the top row of this loop you need to put a condition (a [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 that evaluates to either `true` or `false`). The loop executes the blocks you plug into it again and again, but before it starts a new round of the loop, it checks the condition, and if this condition evaluates to `false` it stops looping. In other words, the While/Until loop repeats until the condition is not `true` anymore.

### While vs Until[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#while-vs-until "Direct link to heading")

In the dropdown of the block you can choose between "while" and "until". The only difference is that for "while", the condition is also evaluated before the very first loop execution. In other words, the "until" loop always executes at least once, whereas the "while" loop does not execute at all if the condition already evaluates to `false` at the very beginning.

### Example - Import Multiple Folders[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#example---import-multiple-folders "Direct link to heading")

![Block controls_whileUntil](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_whileUntil_example.png)

This script imports multiple footage folders into your Premiere Pro project. The blocks plugged into the loop first show a "Choose Folder" dialog and then import the folder that the user has chosen. Since we plugged the blocks into the loop, the script then asks "Do you want to import more footage?" and as long as the user chooses "yes", the script repeats the process. Note that we have chosen the "until" option, so that the question is not asked before the first folder import.

By the way, if you're wondering what the [Tinker File Path block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesNameAndPath#string_tinker_path)
 here does: from the full path (say `C:\projects\myProject\footage`), it just takes the name (like `footage` in our example) and puts a `\` in front of it to indicate that we want to import it into a bin in the root bin of the project (`\footage` denotes a bin "footage" in the root bin, whereas `footage` denotes a bin with name "footage" that could be anywhere - so Automation Blocks would search your project to see if it can find a bin of that name).

Count With[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#controls_for "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block controls_for](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Loops/controls_for.png)

The "Count With" loop is similar to the simple [Repeat Loop](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops#controls_repeat_ext)
, but it offers a [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `i`, which you can use for counting how often the loop has already repeated.

##### Count with is NOT a real For loop

If you know a `for(...)` loop, you might get the impression that "Count with" is the same, but there is a subtle difference: When you "count from x to y" and y is smaller than x, then the count goes backwards (whereas the _for_ loop would not execute at all). To achieve the usual _for_ loop behavior, it's better to use a _while_ loop:

![Count with is not a for loop](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_for_example.png)

### By parameter[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#by-parameter "Direct link to heading")

![for loop simple example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_for_example_1.png)

This block prints the following to the console:

    this is loop repetition 1this is loop repetition 2this is loop repetition 3this is loop repetition 4this is loop repetition 5this is loop repetition 6this is loop repetition 7this is loop repetition 8this is loop repetition 9this is loop repetition 10

As you can see, the variable `i` is incremented by 1 each time the loop repeats. If we change the `by` parameter to 4, the variable `i` grows by 4 in each iteration of the loop:

![for loop simple example 2](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_for_example_2.png)

    this is loop repetition 1this is loop repetition 5this is loop repetition 9

### Example - Export Stills[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#example---export-stills "Direct link to heading")

![for loop simple example 3](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_for_example_3.png)

This loop exports still images from the active sequence. In the example, the 0.5 at the end of the first line results in the export of one still image every 0.5 seconds of the sequence. The loop [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `i` is also used to name the exported stills. The created output files are

    frame_at_0s.jpgframe_at_0.5s.jpgframe_at_1s.jpgframe_at_1.5s.jpg...

If you prefer frame numbers or timecodes instead of seconds in the file names, you could use the blocks in the category [Pr Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime)
 to convert the time in seconds into your preferred format.

For Each Loop[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#controls_forEach "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block controls_forEach](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Loops/controls_forEach.png)

The _For Each_ loop takes a list and then executes the blocks you plug into it once for each list element. The list element is stored in the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `j`.

![for each example 1](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_forEach_example_1.png)

This example calls the "write to console" block three times. The first time j is `Monday`, the second time `Tuesday`, and the third time `3`. So, the script writes the following on the console:

    MondayTuesday3

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#example-1 "Direct link to heading")

![for each example 2](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_forEach_example_2.png)

This scripts adds the sequences with names `very first draft`, `my sequence final` and `final final 03` to the render queue. Note that the variable `j` is used both for the sequence name that is rendered and for the output file.

Loop Break/Continue[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#controls_flow_statements "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block controls_flow_statements](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Loops/controls_flow_statements.png)

The break/continue block can be plugged into any loop block to stop its execution early. Here is a basic example, where the loop is configured to repeat ten times, but inside the loop, we check if the 5th repetition has been reached, and if so we break out of the loop:

![break example 1](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_flow_statements_example_1.png)

As a result, the loop only executes 5 times and the script shows this in the console:

    12345

### Example - Break out of Search[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#example---break-out-of-search "Direct link to heading")

Here is a more practical example, where we search for a sequence item. We loop over all project items, but as soon as we've found one that is a sequence, we stop.

![break example 2](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_flow_statements_example_2.png)

The same pattern can be used for all kinds of search operations, when you don't want to continue the loop after you've found what you were looking for.

### Continue option[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#continue-option "Direct link to heading")

If you choose the `continue with next operation` instead of `break out`, the loop only stops the current iteration and immediately continues with the next one.

![continue example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/controls_flow_statements_example_3.png)

In this example, each iteration writes two messages to the console - one before and one after the _if_ condition. In the fifth iteration, the condition is `true`, so we execute the `continue with next iteration` statement. As as result, the second console output is skipped in the 5th iteration and there is no line `this is still iteration 5` in the console output:

    1this is still iteration 12this is still iteration 23this is still iteration 34this is still iteration 456this is still iteration 67this is still iteration 78this is still iteration 89this is still iteration 910this is still iteration 10

*   [Overview](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#overview)
    
*   [Specialized Loops](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#specialized-loops)
    
*   [Repeat Loop](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#controls_repeat_ext)
    *   [Example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#example)
        
*   [While/Until Loop](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#controls_whileUntil)
    *   [While vs Until](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops/#while-vs-until)
