# Pr Mogrts & Clip Parameters | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#)

On this page

Pr Mogrts & Clip Parameters
===========================

The blocks in this section allow you to access and change some of the properties of sequence clips, which you can find in the _Effect Controls Panel_ and the _Essential Graphics Panel_. You can get and set values, and even create keyframes.

Replace Ae Mogrt Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#block_pr_replace_mogrt_text "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_replace_mogrt_text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMogrtsAndClipParameters/block_pr_replace_mogrt_text.png)

This block does a search and replace for texts in motion graphics templates. This is very useful to translate title texts or lower thirds into multiple languages, for example.

#### search and replace text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#search-and-replace-text "Direct link to heading")

Just give the text you want to replace as the first parameter, and the text that it should be replaced with as the second parameter. The block then searches though all the texts of all motion graphics template clips in the given sequence (or you can specify a specific clip instead of a sequence, as described below). Whenever the text equals exactly the text you search for, the block replaces it with the new text. Note that the search replaces all occurrences of the text, and does not stop after the first hit.

#### in sequence (or clip)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#in-sequence-or-clip "Direct link to heading")

By default, the block looks at all clips in the active sequence, but you can also specify a different sequence, or a single clip instead. If the text you want to replace is just contained in a single clip and you know which clip that is, it makes sense to provide this clip as an argument, instead of the entire sequence, because the block will process much faster if it does not need to search through the entire sequence. For all the ways to specify a specific clip, see [Accessing a Particular Sequence Clip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#accessSequenceClip)
.

#### case sensitive[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#case-sensitive "Direct link to heading")

If you enable this checkbox, the search is case sensitive. If you disable it, a search for `#TEXT1` would also replace the text `#teXT1`, for example.

##### Help us to support other templates, too!

There are two flavors of Motion Graphics Templates: templates created inside Premiere Pro, and templates created inside After Effects. Currently, this block only supports templates that have been created in After Effects. We would love to support all other templates and titles too, but this is only possible if the Premiere Pro team extends their scripting API. Please [submit a feature request](https://www.adobe.com/products/wishform.html)
 to encourage the Premiere Pro team to add that feature.

##### use #IDs

Say you plan to locate a text in English, German and Spanish. Instead of starting with the English texts and then replacing them with the German and Spanish texts, you can start with a version that only contains placeholder IDs instead of the actual texts like `#text1`, `#text2` etc. Keep the IDs in a spreadsheet together with the actual texts in English, German and Spanish - then you can always quickly create your English, German and Spanish variants at any time.

If you start with the English texts instead, things can become messy when you decide to change any of them later (for IDs, there should be no need to change them at any time).

##### use clones

If you want to keep your original sequence and make the replacements in a copy, you can use the [Clone Sequence Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#block_pr_sequence_clone)
 for that.

Get Clip Parameter Value[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_get_value "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_component_parameter_get_value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMogrtsAndClipParameters/pr_component_parameter_get_value.png)

This block can return the value of all kinds of properties that are listed in the _Effect Controls Panel_ - like the position or scale of the clip, but also any parameters of effects.

#### clip param[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#clip-param "Direct link to heading")

Here you can choose the parameter whose value you want to have (like the opacity or scale of the clip). The dropdown where you can choose the parameter by default contains a list of parameters that most clips have. However, you can also access other parameters - like the effect controls of effects you applied to a particular clip. Just select a clip (or several of them) in your sequence and click the refresh button behind the dropdown list (the circular arrow symbol). The block then reads all parameters that it can find within the selected clip(s) and populates the dropdown list with them. Now you can choose the parameter in the dropdown easily.

Instead of choosing a parameter from the dropdown, you can also drag a text block or [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 here to specify the name of the clip parameter. You can retrieve the name of an individual parameter with the [Clip Param block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_path)
 or [iterate over all clip params](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#block_pr_for_all_component_params_of_clip)
 to get all their names.

#### clip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#clip "Direct link to heading")

Specify the clip from which you want to read a parameter value here. In the example above, the `::V1\1` represents the first clip in track V1 of the active sequence. For all the ways to specify a specific clip, see [Accessing a Particular Sequence Clip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#accessSequenceClip)
.

#### if clip does not have this parameter[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#if-clip-does-not-have-this-parameter "Direct link to heading")

Some properties are not available on all clips (for instance, the controls of a particular effect are only available on clips to which this effect has been applied). If the clip does not have the parameter you want to read, you have two options:

*   return no value
*   stop with error

#### if parameter has keyframes[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#if-parameter-has-keyframes "Direct link to heading")

If the parameter you want to read has keyframes, the value changes over time. For this situation, you have the following options:

*   _get value at current time_: returns the value at the time where the current time indicator of the sequence is.
*   _get value before keys where added_: when a parameter is keyframed, Premiere Pro still remembers the value that the parameter had before keyframing was activated. You can access this value with this option.
*   _stop with error_: choose this error if you want to be sure that the parameter has no keyframes.

Set Clip Parameter Value[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_set_value "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_component_parameter_set_value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMogrtsAndClipParameters/pr_component_parameter_set_value.png)

This block can change the value of all kinds of properties that are listed in the _Effect Controls Panel_, like the position or scale of the clip, but also any parameters of effects.

#### clip param[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#clip-param-1 "Direct link to heading")

The dropdown where you can choose the parameter by default contains a list of parameters that most clips have. However, you can also access other parameters - like the effect controls of effects you applied to a particular clip. Just select a clip (or several of them) in your sequence and click the refresh button behind the dropdown list (circular arrow symbol). The block then reads all parameters that it can find on the selected clip(s) and populates the dropdown list with them. Now you can choose the parameter in the dropdown easily.

Instead of choosing a parameter from the dropdown, you can also drag a text block or [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 here to specify the name of the clip parameter. You can retrieve the name of an individual parameter with the [Clip Param block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_path)
 or [iterate over all clip params](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#block_pr_for_all_component_params_of_clip)
 to get all their names.

#### clip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#clip-1 "Direct link to heading")

Specify the clip whose parameters you want to change here. In the example above, the `::V1\1` represents the first clip in track V1 of the active sequence. For all the ways to specify a specific clip see [Accessing a Particular Sequence Clip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#accessSequenceClip)
.

#### set value to[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#set-value-to "Direct link to heading")

By default (dropdown option `set value to`), the existing parameter value is replaced with the new value. But this block also supports other operations to modify the existing value with the new one:

*   _set value to_: replaces the old value with a new one
*   _multiply value with_: multiplies the existing value with the new one. If you want to make a layer twice as big, for example, no matter how it has been scaled before, you can multiply its scale value by 2. (This only makes sense for number and list values, not for boolean or text values)
*   _multiply value with %_: multiplies the existing value with the new value divided by a hundred. So if you want to make your layer half as big as before, you can multiply its scale parameter by 50% (e.g. pass number 50 as new value). (This only makes sense for number and list values, not for boolean or text values)
*   _add to value_: adds the new value to the existing one - so if you want to lower the opacity of a clip by 20%, you can just add the number -20 to the existing value, for example. (This only makes sense for number and list values, not for boolean or text values)
*   _keep current value (ignore new value)_: This does not change the value at all. It does not make a lot of sense for this block, but works the same way on the [Set Clip Parameter Value At Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters#pr_component_parameter_set_value_at_time)
     block, where it creates a new keyframe at the given time but keeps the existing value.

#### value[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#value "Direct link to heading")

The parameter dropdown lists, behind each parameter in parentheses, give an example value and a type:

![value types](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_component_parameter_set_value_type_example.png)

You need to specify a value of that type, but Automation Blocks does its best to convert whatever you provide to that type.

The types are:

*   _number_: a single number (you find a block for that in the Math section). Texts are converted to numbers, too.
*   _array2_: the position or anchor point of a clip has two values (for x and y). Therefore you need to provide a list with two entries as values (see section _Lists_ for blocks that create lists). Also note that position and anchor point are not measured in pixels. Instead, the top left corner of the frame corresponds to `[0,0]` and the bottom right corner to `[1,1]`. The default value of `[0.5,0.5]` is thus always the center of the frame, no matter what resolution the sequence has.
*   _boolean_: Parameters which correspond to checkboxes need a logic value `true` or `false`. You'll find blocks for these in the Logic section. Texts are converted to booleans as follows: if the string is empty or is exactly the text `false` it is converted to the logic value `false`; otherwise it is converted to `true`. All numbers except `0` are converted to `true` (also negative numbers).
*   _string_: a text

#### if clip does not have this parameter[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#if-clip-does-not-have-this-parameter-1 "Direct link to heading")

Some properties are not available on all clips (like the controls of a particular effect are only available on clips to which this effect has been applied). If the clip does not have the parameter you want to read, you have two options:

*   _do nothing_
*   _stop with error_

#### if parameter has keyframes[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#if-parameter-has-keyframes-1 "Direct link to heading")

If the parameter you want to change has keyframes, the value changes over time. For this situation, you have the following options:

*   _delete keyframes_: deletes all existing keyframes on the parameter before setting the new value.
*   _stop with error_: choose this error if you want to be sure that the parameter has no keyframes.

If you want to create keyframes (i.e. change the value only at the current time), you need to use the [Set Clip Parameter Value At Time Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters#pr_component_parameter_set_value_at_time)
 instead.

Get Clip Parameter Value At Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_get_value_at_time "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_component_parameter_get_value_at_time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMogrtsAndClipParameters/pr_component_parameter_get_value_at_time.png)

Reads the value from a keyframed parameter at the given time. This block is very similar to the [Get Clip Parameter Value Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters#pr_component_parameter_get_value)
, except that it supports an additional time value to specify at which point in time the value should be read. For parameters which are not keyframed, the two blocks behave identically, but if the value changes over time, this block can read that keyframed value.

#### at clip time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#at-clip-time "Direct link to heading")

Here you need to specify the time (in seconds) where the keyframed value should be read. You have different options for how exactly to specify this time:

*   _clip time_: `0` means the beginning of the footage file that this clip is using
*   _sequence time_: `0` means the beginning of the sequence
*   _time from in point_: `0` means the beginning of the clip in the sequence
*   _time from out point_: `0` means the end of the clip (and `-1` means one second before the end of the clip, for example)

#### other controls[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#other-controls "Direct link to heading")

For everything else that this block has to offer - in particular how to access properties which are not listed in the dropdown by default - see the documentation for the [Get Clip Parameter Value Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters#pr_component_parameter_get_value)
, which is identical to this one, except for the missing time value.

Set Clip Parameter Value At Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_set_value_at_time "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_component_parameter_set_value_at_time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMogrtsAndClipParameters/pr_component_parameter_set_value_at_time.png)

Adds (or modifies) a keyframe to a parameter at the given time. This block is very similar to the [Set Clip Parameter Value Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters#pr_component_parameter_set_value)
, except that it supports:

*   an additional time value to specify at which point in time the value should be read.
*   a control to choose the keyframe type

#### at clip time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#at-clip-time-1 "Direct link to heading")

Here you need to specify the time (in seconds) where the keyframe should be created. You have different options for exactly how to specify this time:

*   _clip time_: `0` means the beginning of the footage file that this clip is using
*   _sequence time_: `0` means the beginning of the sequence
*   _time from in point_: `0` means the beginning of the clip in the sequence
*   _time from out point_: `0` means the end of the clip (and `-1` means one second before the end of the clip, for example)

#### with interpolation[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#with-interpolation "Direct link to heading")

Here you can choose the type of the keyframe, i.e. how the keyframe interpolates the values. This is sometimes also called the keyframe easing. You have the following options:

*   _linear_
*   _hold_
*   _bezier_
*   _time_
*   _time transition start_
*   _time transition end_
*   _easy in (obsolete)_
*   _ease out (obsolete)_
*   _ease in & out (obsolete)_

##### Premiere Pro Bug

Due to a bug in Premiere Pro 2021, the scripting API can only create keyfames with linear easing. If you try to create a keyframe with another type in a Premiere Pro version that has this bug, Automation Blocks will detect it and stop with a helpful error message. Please switch the interpolation option to linear to avoid this problem.

We reported this bug (bug DVAPR-4231653) to the Premiere Pro team and hope they will fix it fast. If you have this issue and need other keyframe types, please encourage the Premiere Pro team to fix bug DVAPR-4231653 asap. The more people that complain, the more priority they will give to this.

#### other controls[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#other-controls-1 "Direct link to heading")

For everything else that this block has to offer - in particular how to access properties which are not listed in the dropdown by default - see the documentation of the [Set Clip Parameter Value Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters#pr_component_parameter_set_value)
, which is identical to this one, except for the missing time and easing controls.

Remove Keyframes[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_remove_key_range "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_component_parameter_remove_key_range](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMogrtsAndClipParameters/pr_component_parameter_remove_key_range.png)

By default, this block removes all keyframes from the given clip param between its in point and its out point.

#### start & end time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#start--end-time "Direct link to heading")

You can specify the time range in which the keys should be deleted (both start and end time are inclusive, i.e. keys which are exactly at the start or end time are removed, too). For details how exactly the start and end time can be specified see the [Set Clip Parameter Value At Time block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters#pr_component_parameter_set_value_at_time)
, which has the same options (i.e. the time can be specified relative to the in- or out-point, or in clip time or sequence time).

#### other controls[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#other-controls-2 "Direct link to heading")

For everything else that this block has to offer - in particular how to access properties which are not listed in the dropdown by default - see the documentation of the [Set Clip Parameter Value Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters#pr_component_parameter_set_value)
, which is identical to this one, except for the missing time and easing controls.

For Each Parameter of Clip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#block_pr_for_all_component_params_of_clip "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_component_parameter_set_value_at_time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMogrtsAndClipParameters/block_pr_for_all_component_params_of_clip.png)

This block loops over all parameters of the specified clip. This includes the usual parameters that most clips have (like opacity or scale, or the volume parameters for audio clips) but also any parameters of effects which are applied to the clip. The block executes the blocks you nest inside of it for each of those properties. Each time those nested blocks are executed, the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `my param` includes the name of the respective property. To retrieve the value of the property, for example, just drag the variable `my param` onto the block [Get Clip Parameter Value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_get_value)
:

![Block example: For Each Parameter of Clip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_pr_for_all_component_params_of_clip_example.png)

Clip Param[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_path "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_component_parameter_path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMogrtsAndClipParameters/pr_component_parameter_path.png)

This block offers a dropdown where you can choose a clip parameter (like the opacity or scale of the clip) and returns the name of that parameter. Usually, this block is just embedded as a part of other blocks like the [Get Clip Parameter block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_get_value)
. However, it is also available as a standalone block if you ever want to figure out the name of a particular clip parameter.

By default, the dropdown lists the clip paramters which most clips have. However, you can also access other parameters - like the effect controls of effects you applied to a particular clip. Just select a clip (or several of them) in your sequence and click the refresh button behind the dropdown list (the circular arrow symbol). The block then reads all the parameters that it can find within the selected clip(s) and populates the dropdown list with them.

Keyframeable Clip Param[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_path_keyframeable "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_component_parameter_path_keyframeable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrMogrtsAndClipParameters/pr_component_parameter_path_keyframeable.png)

This block works exactly as the [Clip Param block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_path)
, except that the dropdown only lists the clip parameters that can be keyframed.

Troubleshooting Clip Param Issues[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#troubleshooting-clip-param-issues "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Could not find component parameter[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#could-not-find-component-parameter "Direct link to heading")

If a component parameter could not be found by Automation Blocks, you typically see a message like this in the console:

    could not find component parameter mySequence::V1\1\Motion\Scale

It indicates, that in the sequence with name "mySequence" in Track V1 the clip numer 1 has no clip parameter "Scale" in the group "Motion". This can have two reasons:

1.  The parameter really does not exist (maybe you try to access the scale of an audio clip?)
2.  The parameter has a different name, since you use a different UI language version of Pr.

In German Pr, for example, "Scale" is named "Skalieren" and "Motion" is "Bewegung". Automation Blocks 1.1.001 or newer knows the names of all parameters in all user interface languages. That means, if it looks for a property "Scale" and cannot find it, it will check if it finds a property "Skalieren" instead, for example. Therefore, **updating to Automation Blocks 1.1.001 solves most language issues**.

However, for third party effects, it is not aware of their localizations, for example. Hence, if you run into any issues, you might want to check if switching the UI language of Pr to English solves your issue.

#### Changing the UI langage of Pr[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#changing-the-ui-langage-of-pr "Direct link to heading")

You can change the language without reinstalling Pr:

1.  in Pr use the keyboard shortcut to open up the console window: Mac: fn + command + F12 Win: ctrl + F12
    
2.  in the Console, make sure the Debug Database view is open:
    
    ![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/PrConsoleWindowDebugDatabaseView.png)
3.  search in the Console for "ApplicationLanguage" and set it to "en\_EN" for English
    
4.  restart Pr
    

*   [Replace Ae Mogrt Text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#block_pr_replace_mogrt_text)
    
*   [Get Clip Parameter Value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_get_value)
    
*   [Set Clip Parameter Value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_set_value)
    
*   [Get Clip Parameter Value At Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_get_value_at_time)
    
*   [Set Clip Parameter Value At Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_set_value_at_time)
    
*   [Remove Keyframes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters/#pr_component_parameter_remove_key_range)

# Color Palette Workflow | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#)

On this page

Color Palette Workflow
======================

Overview[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#overview "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Automation Blocks script **link to color palette** offers a very easy and powerful color workflow in After Effects. Store your colors in one central color palette comp, then link anything in your project to it. When you modify the color palette, all linked color values in your scene update instantly.

Usage[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#usage "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Linking Colors (and creating palette comp)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#linking-colors-and-creating-palette-comp "Direct link to heading")

Just select some color properties, Text layers, or Solid layers in your project and run the script. A dialog pops up where you can pick the color that you want to link to:

![After Effects Script: Link to Color Palette](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/colorPaletteLinkDialog.png)After you choose a color, two things will happen:

1.  if there is no color palette comp in your project yet, it will create one
2.  it links the selected color properties to the chosen color of the color palette

If you didn't select any color properties, but a Text or Solid layer (or several of them), it creates a fill effect on those layers and links the fill color to the palette.

**Saturation & Brightness**

If you change the _Saturation_ or _Brightness_ values in the link dialog, the linked color will not be exactly the same color as in the palette, but a modified (desaturated/darker/brighter/...) variant of it.

### Changing the Color Palette[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#changing-the-color-palette "Direct link to heading")

The color theme comp contains a shape layer with (by default) five shapes - each representing one of the colors in the palette. If you change the fill color of one of the shapes, all linked colors in your project change with it, instantly.

Customizations[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#customizations "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Adding More Colors (or Changing Default Colors)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#adding-more-colors-or-changing-default-colors "Direct link to heading")

If you open the block code of the script, at the very beginning you'll see a list of the default colors of the color palette. You can change those default colors and even add more colors to the list. After you've done this, delete the color palette comp from your project (if it already has one), so that the next time you execute the script a new color palette comp (with the new colors) is created.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/colorPaletteChangeColors.gif)

### Customizing Name & Location of Color Palette Comp[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#customizing-name--location-of-color-palette-comp "Direct link to heading")

Right below the list, where you can customize the default colors of the palette, you'll also find variables, where you can customize the name of the color palette comp and the project bin where it is located.

![Custom Color Palette Name](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/colorPaletteChangeCompName.png)

To store the color palette in a different bin, for example, just enter the name of that bin (or a path like `/bin/subbin`), or select an existing bin in your project and click the _refresh_ icon of the [Project Bin](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems##ae_itembin_path)
 block.

##### Keyboard Shortcut

If you use this script a lot, note that you can also give Automation Blocks scripts a keyboard shortcut.

*   [Overview](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#overview)
    
*   [Usage](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#usage)
    *   [Linking Colors (and creating palette comp)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#linking-colors-and-creating-palette-comp)
        
    *   [Changing the Color Palette](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#changing-the-color-palette)
        
*   [Customizations](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#customizations)
    *   [Adding More Colors (or Changing Default Colors)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#adding-more-colors-or-changing-default-colors)
        
    *   [Customizing Name & Location of Color Palette Comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes/#customizing-name--location-of-color-palette-comp)

# Pr Rendering | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#)

On this page

Pr Rendering
============

Add to Media Encoder Queue[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#block_media_encoder_render "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_media_encoder_render](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrRendering/block_media_encoder_render.png)

This block adds the given sequence or project item to Adobe Media Encoder's render queue. This block does not yet start the render queue. You can do that with the [Start Render Queue Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering#block_media_encoder_start_render_queue)
.

#### sequence or project item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#sequence-or-project-item "Direct link to heading")

You can provide any sequence or project item here, from which a frame should be rendered. Alternatively, you can also provide a clip of a sequence here, which is then converted to the corresponding project item automatically.

#### format preset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#format-preset "Direct link to heading")

Here you can either choose one of the export presets from the dropdown, or specify a Media Encoder Preset file (\*.epr). The dropdown just includes the most popular presets - if you want something more special, you can drag a [File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_file)
 or [Embedded File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_embedded_file)
 on top of the dropdown to load a custom epr preset file:

![Using custom epr preset](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/epr_file_example.png)

You can create this type of epr file as follows.

1.  Add something to Adobe Media Encoder's queue.
2.  click on the _Preset_ of the new queue entry and change the render settings however you want them to be.
3.  click the _Save Preset_ icon next to the preset name.

![Save Preset](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_media_encoder_render_save_preset.png)

4.  In the dialog that opens, choose a name for the preset. Then a preset file with the same name (and the extension .epr) will be created in your **documents folder** in the subfolder `Adobe/Adobe Media Encoder/VERSION_NUMBER/Presets`.

#### output file[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#output-file "Direct link to heading")

Here you need to specify the file path of the resulting image file. See [Accessing a Particular File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
 for all the ways you can specify a file.

#### range[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#range "Direct link to heading")

For the range of the clip that should be rendered, you have the following options:

*   _entire clip_
*   _in point to out point_
*   _work area_

Note that if you render a clip of a sequence and choose "_in point to out point_", the clip is converted to its project item. Hence, the in point and the out point of the project item, not those of the clip, are used.

#### remove upon completion[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#remove-upon-completion "Direct link to heading")

If you enable this option, the entry will be removed from Adobe Media Encoder's render queue once it is done rendering.

##### preview format preset

If you choose the property _preview file format_ in the [Get Property of Sequence Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#pr_sequence_get_property)
, it will return the .epr file that is used to render the previews of a sequence.

Add Single Frame To Media Encoder Queue[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#block_media_encoder_render_still "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_media_encoder_render_still](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrRendering/block_media_encoder_render_still.png)

This block adds to Adobe Media Encoder's render queue a job to render a single still image of the given sequence or project item. This block does not yet start the render queue. You can do that with the [Start Render Queue Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering#block_media_encoder_start_render_queue)
.

#### sequence or project item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#sequence-or-project-item-1 "Direct link to heading")

You can provide any sequence or project item here, from which a frame should be rendered. Alternatively, you can also provide a clip of a sequence here, which is then converted to the corresponding project item automatically.

#### format[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#format "Direct link to heading")

You can choose one of the following formats:

*   jpg
*   png
*   tiff

#### time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#time "Direct link to heading")

Here you need to specify the time of the sequence or project item at which a still frame should be rendered. Time is given in seconds and `0` corresponds to the beginning of the sequence or the project item's footage. See the section [PrTime](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime)
 for blocks that convert between different time and timecode formats.

#### remove upon completion[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#remove-upon-completion-1 "Direct link to heading")

If you enable this option, the entry will be removed from Adobe Media Encoder's render queue once it is done rendering.

#### output file[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#output-file-1 "Direct link to heading")

Here you need to specify the file path of the resulting image file. See [Accessing a Particular File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
 for all the ways you can specify a file.

##### 0 at end of filename

To export a single image, Automation Blocks needs to use a workaround to export image sequences with the duration of a single frame. As a side effect of that Premiere will always append a 0 to the end of the filename.

Start Render Queue[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#block_media_encoder_start_render_queue "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_media_encoder_start_render_queue](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrRendering/block_media_encoder_start_render_queue.png)

This block tells Adobe Media Encoder to start the render queue. The program continues immediately and does **not wait** until the render queue is finished. If you want to do things after the render queue is done, use the [After Media Encoder Queue is Done Rendering Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering#block_media_encoder_on_rendering_done)
.

After Media Encoder Queue is Done Rendering[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#block_media_encoder_on_rendering_done "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_media_encoder_on_rendering_done](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrRendering/block_media_encoder_on_rendering_done.png)

The blocks you nest inside this block are executed as soon as the render queue of Adobe Media Encoder is finished rendering. The blocks below this block are executed immediately (while the render queue is still rendering). This allows your script to perform some tasks while the render queue is still busy, and other tasks when it is done rendering.

Export Preset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#export-preset "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_media_encoder_get_preset](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrRendering/block_media_encoder_get_preset.png)

This block gives you access to the export preset files that are included in Automation Blocks. Usually, this block is only used as part of the [Add to Media Encoder Queue block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering#block_media_encoder_render)
, but you can also use it as a standalone block. This is useful, for example, if you want to choose an export preset at the top of your script and then use the chosen preset for any _Add to Media Encoder Queue_ blocks later in the script.

Here is a basic example, where we store a preset in a [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 in the first line and then use it in the second block below:

![store preset in variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_media_encoder_get_preset_example.png)

*   [Add to Media Encoder Queue](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#block_media_encoder_render)
    
*   [Add Single Frame To Media Encoder Queue](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#block_media_encoder_render_still)
    
*   [Start Render Queue](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#block_media_encoder_start_render_queue)
    
*   [After Media Encoder Queue is Done Rendering](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#block_media_encoder_on_rendering_done)
    
*   [Export Preset](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering/#export-preset)

# Programming a Highlight Tool | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/programmingAHighlightTool/#)

On this page

Programming a Highlight Tool
============================

In this course you will create an Automation Blocks tool that adds one of the following highlight shapes around each layer that you currently have selected.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-ae/highlightPreviews.gif)

You will learn

*   how to create shape layers
*   how to apply presets
*   how to loop over selected layers
*   how to set properties like the scale or position of a layer
*   how to measure the bounding box (source rect) of a layer

### Using the Highlight Tools[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/programmingAHighlightTool/#using-the-highlight-tools "Direct link to heading")

### Just Give Me These Tools![​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/programmingAHighlightTool/#just-give-me-these-tools "Direct link to heading")

If you just want to use the highlight tools and don't want to learn how they are created, you can download them from our [Community Library](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#access-community-library)
 in the folder `Rigs and Presets/Shapes/Burst and Highlight Shapes`.

### Fast Track: Create Your Own Highlight Tool[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/programmingAHighlightTool/#fast-track-create-your-own-highlight-tool "Direct link to heading")

To create your own variant of the highlight tool with a custom design, there is no need to fully understand the code. Just start with one of the existing highlight shapes, change the shape layer however you want, and save its contents & effects in a new animation preset. To create a new tool based on your new preset, you just need to customize three small parts in the code:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-ae/customizeHighlightTool.png)

If you want to fully undestand the code from start to finish, watch the following classes:

### Class 1: Creating a Shape Layer and Applying a Preset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/programmingAHighlightTool/#class-1-creating-a-shape-layer-and-applying-a-preset "Direct link to heading")

In this class, you'll learn how to create a new shape layer and apply a preset to it. This is the easiest way to create any custom shapes - you can also add effects, keyframes, and even expressions using this approach in no time.

### Class 2: Customizing the Preset for Each Selected Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/programmingAHighlightTool/#class-2-customizing-the-preset-for-each-selected-layer "Direct link to heading")

In this class we extend our tool so that instead of creating a single shape layer, it creates a separate one for each currently selected layer. We measure the size and location of all these selected layers so we can adust the position and scale of the hightlight shapes we create accordingly.

*   [Using the Highlight Tools](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/programmingAHighlightTool/#using-the-highlight-tools)
    
*   [Just Give Me These Tools!](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/programmingAHighlightTool/#just-give-me-these-tools)
    
*   [Fast Track: Create Your Own Highlight Tool](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/programmingAHighlightTool/#fast-track-create-your-own-highlight-tool)
    
*   [Class 1: Creating a Shape Layer and Applying a Preset](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/programmingAHighlightTool/#class-1-creating-a-shape-layer-and-applying-a-preset)
    
*   [Class 2: Customizing the Preset for Each Selected Layer](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/programmingAHighlightTool/#class-2-customizing-the-preset-for-each-selected-layer)

# Overview | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#)

On this page

Overview
========

Finding the Blocks You Need[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#finding-the-blocks-you-need "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The block reference describes all blocks which are available in Automation Blocks for Premiere Pro and After Effects. In order to find the blocks you need quickly, you need to understand the categories that the blocks are grouped into. So, this page will give you an overview of these categories.

The categories are grouped into three main sections. Let's take a look at each of them quickly.

![preferences](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/block_categories.png)

This screenshot is from Premiere Pro. In After Effects the structure is identical, except that instead of the blocks which are specific to Premiere Pro, you have blocks which are specific to After Effects.

General Programming[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#general-programming "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

In the unlikely case that you are a programmer, the blocks you find here willb be self-explanatory: They contain the same basic ingredients as any programming language. In the much more likely case that you're not a programmer, these might be overwhelming, so my advice is: don't worry to much about them for now. You will learn how to use them step by step in our tutorial series. At the moment, it's only important to develop a vague intuition for what these blocks might be good for. So, let me give you an overview:

### Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#text "Direct link to heading")

Your scripts often deal with texts. Maybe you want to rename a project item? Well, that name is a text. Or perhaps you want to add some metadata to it, like a copyright notice? That metadata is text, too. Text is everywhere, and the functions in this section allow you to work with texts - create them, search within them, or change them in all kinds of ways. The most basic text block is this one (and you've used it already, without really noticing):

![text block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/bin_subbin_text.png)

Later, when you're a more experienced automation hero, instead of "use the bin with name `bin\subbin`" you can say things like "use the bin with the name that you get when taking the name of the active sequence, replacing `final` with `almost final` and adding `-revision003`. And all the tools for this kind of text tweaking are found within this very category.

### Variables[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#variables "Direct link to heading")

This is a more advanced topic (if you have no programming experience). In a nutshell, it allows you to save a value in a variable and then reuse it in later steps of your script. We will use variables for the first time in the tutorial [Search & Replace in Project Items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/searchReplaceProjectItems)
. If you can't wait, here's a quick example:

![variables example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/variables_example.png)

In the first block, we create a variable named `scene name` and give it the value `my awesome scene 1`. Then we create a project bin with that name and two sub-bins named `audio` and `video` inside of it. Finally, we also create a sequence with the name `my awesome scene 1`. The great thing is that when you move on to your next awesome scene, you just need to update the name in the very first block, instead of searching though your entire script for every occurrence of `my awesome scene 1`.

### Loops[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#loops "Direct link to heading")

Again, advanced. In your scripts, you'll often want to do something many times - like looping over all sequences of your project, in order to add all of them to the render queue. Or when you search for something, you might want to loop over all project items. This category only contains some "general purpose" loops and you will need them very rarely. You'll find more specialized (and hence simpler to use) loops in other categories. In the "Files Text" category, for example, you'll find a block to loop over all the lines of a text file, and in the category "Pr Sequence" there's a block to loop over all the clips of a sequence. To save you the effort of browsing through all the categories, here's a list of all those specialized loops:

*   [loop over all lines of a text file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText#extendscript_text_file_for_all_lines)
    
*   [loop over all rows/columns of a spreadsheet](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesSpreadsheet#spreadsheet_for_each_row)
    
*   [loop over all open projects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#block_pr_for_all_open_projects)
    
*   [loop over project items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#block_pr_for_all_items_in_bin)
    
*   [loop over clips in a sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#block_pr_for_all_sequence_items)
    
*   [loop over markers of a clip or sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMarker#pr_marker_for_all_of_project_item)
    
*   [loop over parameters of a clip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prMogrtsAndClipParameters#block_pr_for_all_component_params_of_clip)
    

You'll always recognize those loop blocks easily, based on their green color. But let's postpone this topic to _Automation Superhero Class 3_, a.k.a the [Search & Replace in Project Item Tutorial](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/searchReplaceProjectItems)
.

![Loop example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/loop_example.png)

### Math[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#math "Direct link to heading")

Want to save the project `my project 001.prpro` as `my project 002.prpro`? Or add 10 frame handles to the in and out points of your clips? Or figure out how to scale a 2000x1000 pixel image to fit exactly into a 1920x1080 comp? For all these things, you need to do basic math like adding or multiplying numbers. Consider the blocks in this section to be your pocket calculator. Here, I combined two Math blocks to calculate the square root of (1+2), for example:

![Math example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/math_example.png)

### Logic[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#logic "Direct link to heading")

Does the name of this footage item contain the text "final"? Is this footage item used at least once? Is it offline? Does this sequence have markers? Is this footage an audio file? The blue logic blocks allow you to formulate these types of yes/no questions. Programmers call the result of such a question a boolean value, and it is either `true` (for yes) or `false` (for no). The [If block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic#controls_if)
 in this category allows your script to behave in a different way depending on whether the answer to such a question is yes or no. So whenever your script needs to distiguish between different cases, you'll need the logic blocks.

![Math example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/logic_example.png)

### Lists[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#lists "Direct link to heading")

Some values consist of several components. The position or size of a clip, for example, consists of an X and a Y value.

![Lists example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/lists_example.png)

Automation Blocks represents these as lists (programmers also call them arrays). There is no limit to the length of a list, and you can also use lists to collect values. When you import footage, for example, maybe you only want to import very specific files that you pick based on some custom logic tests (say only files of a particular file type). Then you can collect all footage items which pass your tests into a list, and pass this list to a block that imports all the files in that list.

UI and Files[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#ui-and-files "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The blocks in these categories allow you to create your own user interfaces and to work with files - like reading or writing text or spreadsheet files.

### User Interface & Comments[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#user-interface--comments "Direct link to heading")

These blocks allow you to create some basic user interfaces, like showing an _Open File_ dialog or a prompt where the user can enter some text. It also contains the _comment_ blocks which we can use to document our scripts.

![UI example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/ui_example.png)

The block on the left saves the current project to the file `myProject.prproj` on your desktop. By contrast, each time the block on the right side is executed, it shows a "Save File" dialog and then saves it to the file you've chosen.

### Files[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#files "Direct link to heading")

These are like the block equivalent of your Windows Explorer or Mac OS Finder. Copy or delete files, look at (or create a list of) all the files in a particular folder, or create folders. All these basic file operations can be found in this category.

![files example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/files_example.png)

### Files Name & Path[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#files-name--path "Direct link to heading")

Consistent naming of files and folders is an important part of effective workflows. So, when you automate your workflows, you often have to modify file paths. If you want to export a video and give it the same name as your Premiere Pro project, for example, you'll need to somehow turn the text `myProject.prproj` into `myProject.mp4`. The blocks in this section are your swiss army knife to work with file paths.

So, the next time on of your colleagues didn't follow the naming conventions and wonders how to efficiently rename those 1000 png files, you'll be able to hack a script together in a few minutes that does just that, leaving plenty of time for your colleague to invite you for lunch.

![filenames example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/filenames_example.png)

### Files Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#files-text "Direct link to heading")

Here you'll find blocks to read and write text files. Maybe you want a list of all the footage files used in your project? Or do fancier things like creating .srt files (SubRip Subtitles), or exporting the markers of your sequence as YouTube chapter markers. Whenever you want to read or write text files, you need these blocks.

This example prints each line of the file `test.txt` within Automation Block's console:

![text files example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/files_text_example.png)

### Files Spreadsheet[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#files-spreadsheet "Direct link to heading")

Here you'll find blocks to read and write spreadsheet files. They work similar to the text file blocks, but instead create or read spreadsheet files that you can use with Microsoft Excel, Apple Numbers, Google Docs, or any other spreadsheet app. Yes, it's really that easy to create a spreadsheet:

![spreadsheet files example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/files_spreadsheet_example.png)

Premiere Pro specific[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#premiere_pro_specific_blocks "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

These are the blocks that directly talk to, and remote-control, Premiere Pro. Those are only available in Premiere Pro but we have a similar selection for [Blocks for After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#after_effects_specific_blocks)

### Pr Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#pr-project "Direct link to heading")

Here you'll find blocks to open, save and create Premiere Pro projects, but also to create new bins and import footage (you know the [Sync Folder Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#block_pr_sync_project_bin_with_folder)
 already). Some project settings, like setting scratch disk paths, are also available.

![project example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/pr_project_example.png)

### Pr Project Items[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#pr-project-items "Direct link to heading")

These blocks cover everything you can do with an individual project item. A project item is anything that is listed in your project panel (or its sub-bins), like a footage item, a bin, or a sequence. You can loop over the project items and read or write things like their name, metadata, or label color, for example.

![project items example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/pr_project_item_example.png)

### Pr Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#pr-sequence "Direct link to heading")

A sequence is a project item, so if you want to do something with it that you would normally do in the project panel (like renaming it or moving it to another bin), you can use the blocks in the category "_Pr Project Items_" for this. But a sequence is much more than a "normal" project item. Everything that you can only do with sequences, but not with other project items, can be found in this category. The functionality ranges from creating new sequences, to inserting clips, to retrieving properties like the position of the playhead.

![sequence example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/pr_sequence_example.png)

### Pr Sequence Clips[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#pr-sequence-clips "Direct link to heading")

If you want to work with individual clips inside a sequence, you'll need the blocks in this category. Note the difference between clips and project items: In Premiere Pro, you can right-click on an item in the project panel and choose "_Rename..._" or you can right-click on a clip inside a sequence and choose "_Rename..._" These are two very different things! The former renames the item itself, whereas the latter only renames that single occurrence of that item in the sequence. In the same way, blocks in the category "_Pr Project Items_" act on the items in the project panel, whereas the blocks in this category act on the clips (i.e. occurrences of project items) in a sequence.

![sequence clip example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/pr_sequence_clip_example.png)

The block on the left returns the name of the first clip in track `V1` of the sequence with the name `mySequence`. The block on the right (which comes from the category "_Pr Project Items_" and not "_Pr Sequence Clips_") returns the name of the corresponding project item instead.

### Pr Mogrts & Clip Parameters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#pr-mogrts--clip-parameters "Direct link to heading")

These blocks give you access to the things that are included in Premiere Pro's _Effect Controls_ panel and the _Edit_ tab of the _Essential Graphics_ panel. You can set parameters, or even keyframe them! You can replace the texts in Motion Graphics Templates, too:

![mogrts example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/pr_mogrts_example.png)

### Pr Marker[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#pr-marker "Direct link to heading")

The blocks in this category give you access to the markers of sequences and project items. If you make videos for YouTube, search in the script library for the example "export markers to YouTube chapter text". This will export your markers to chapters that YouTube visualizes directly inside the video player.

![marker example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/pr_marker_example.png)

### Pr Rendering[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#pr-rendering "Direct link to heading")

These blocks allow you to add sequences or clips to the Adobe Media Encoder render queue, start the queue, etc.

![rendering example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/pr_rendering_example.png)

### Pr Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#pr-time "Direct link to heading")

These blocks help you to convert between different time and timecode formats. Automation Blocks represents all time values in seconds (as point numbers), because this way you can easily add and subtract those values. Automation Blocks also does its best to convert time values from other formats wherever necessary. If you export time values to a spreadsheet, for example, and need them in a particular format, the blocks in this category will do that for you.

Since these time conversions are essentially math, the blocks have a bluish color, similar to the blocks in the Math category.

![time example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/pr_time_example.png)

After Effects specific[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#after_effects_specific_blocks "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

These are the blocks that directly talk to, and remote-control, After Effects. They are only available in After Effects, but and we have a similar selection of [Blocks for Premiere Pro](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#premiere_pro_specific_blocks)
.

### Ae Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#ae-project "Direct link to heading")

Here you find the basic project operations like saving or closing a project, but you can also import footage and create project bins. You can even sync entire folders on your hard-drive to import all their content at once.

This category also has one additional, very special block: The block [Execute Menu Command](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject#ae_execute_menu_command)
 allows you to execute any menu command within After Effects. If you want to do anything that can be done with After Effects' main menu, you can do it with this block!

### Ae Project Items[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#ae-project-items "Direct link to heading")

In this category you'll find all the blocks you need to work with the items listed in the project panel. You can loop over all the items in the project - or only those in a particualar project bin, which is useful if you want to search for something or cleanup the project, for example. You can get or set all kinds of item attributes - like their name, source file, or label color, for example. And of course, you can also delete items.

### Ae Comp[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#ae-comp "Direct link to heading")

The blocks in this category allow you to manipulate a comp. You can create a new composition, change the comp settings, or loop over the frames of the composition, for example.

### Ae Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#ae-layer "Direct link to heading")

Here you'll find the blocks to manipulate a layer in a composition. You can create new layers, apply effects or presets to them, and change attributes like the layer's name or label color. You can also duplicate or delete a layer or move it to another comp, for example.

### Ae Layer Properties[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#ae-layer-properties "Direct link to heading")

Properties are the things which you can keyframe (or apply expressions to) in the timeline. Here you'll find the blocks to set their value, expression, or do fancier things like separating their dimensions.

### Ae Keyframes[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#ae-keyframes "Direct link to heading")

These blocks allow you to work with keyframes, but the functionality goes way beyond creating single keyframes. You can load an entire range of keyframes from a property and save them as part of your script, for example, to apply them to other properties easily. If you insert some keyframes which push your layer to the side, wouldn't it be cool if any subsequent keyframes which have been on the layer were already auto-adjusted so they move to the side too? Yes, the blocks in this category give you exactly this kind of high-level manipulation of keyframes.

### Ae Rendering[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview/#ae-rendering "Direct link to heading")

This section contains blocks to operate the After Effects render queue and also to send render jobs to Adobe Media Encoder.
