# Write Your Custom Template Tool | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomTemplateTool/#)

On this page

Write Your Custom Template Tool
===============================

Turn After Effects templates into tools with their own custom user interface! If you frequently render many instances of After Effects templates and want to optimize and automate your workflow, then this course is for you! Learn how to create a script with Automation Blocks for Ae to change texts, footage, colors, and more within a template in no time, as well as how to render it instantly - and you can do all of this with no programming skills!

![user interface of the tool we create](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-ae/writeCustomTemplateToolOverview.png)

If you want to follow along but don't want to write the block code yourself, you'll also find the block code in our [Community Library](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#access-community-library)
 in the folder `Courses/Write Your Custom Template Tool`.

### Class 1: Creating a User Interface[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomTemplateTool/#class-1-creating-a-user-interface "Direct link to heading")

In this class, we use the [Modal Input Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#modal_input_dialog)
 block to create a custom user interface for our tool, where the user can enter all the details they want to customize in the template.

### Class 2: Replacing Texts, Colors and Footage[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomTemplateTool/#class-2-replacing-texts-colors-and-footage "Direct link to heading")

In this class, you'll learn how to update texts, colors and footage files in the After Effects project.

### Class 3: Some Logic[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomTemplateTool/#class-3-some-logic "Direct link to heading")

In this class, you'll learn how to use math and logic in Automation Blocks. We use the math to calculate a scale factor; this way, the user can choose footage of any size and it will be scaled up or down automatically to the size that the Ae template requires. We also use logic so that a single _"color theme"_ dropdown in the user interface can control many color properties at once.

### Class 4: Rendering[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomTemplateTool/#class-4-rendering "Direct link to heading")

In this class, you'll learn how to operate the After Effects render queue with Automation Blocks. We extend our tool so that it not only customizes the template, but also renders it instantly.

*   [Class 1: Creating a User Interface](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomTemplateTool/#class-1-creating-a-user-interface)
    
*   [Class 2: Replacing Texts, Colors and Footage](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomTemplateTool/#class-2-replacing-texts-colors-and-footage)
    
*   [Class 3: Some Logic](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomTemplateTool/#class-3-some-logic)
    
*   [Class 4: Rendering](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomTemplateTool/#class-4-rendering)

---

# Write Your Custom Project Setup Script | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#)

On this page

Write Your Custom Project Setup Script
======================================

Learn how to write your own tools with Automation Blocks - no programming skills required!

In this course, you'll create your own custom project setup script to do all the tedious tasks that need to be done when you start a new project. And since everybody's workflow is different, you'll learn how to write this tool yourself - and how to tailor it exactly to your own needs.

Here are some of the things that our _Project Setup Script_ will be capable of:

*   Ask you for the project name.
*   Create a project folder on your harddrive.
*   Create a new Ae project in that folder.
*   Create your favorite bin structure in the project panel.
*   Check if your camera's memory card is mounted, and if so copy all footage from the card to the project folder, then import it into the project.
*   Create a main composition with guides and a background shape layer.

Most likely your workflow won't need all of these features, but since we will create the script together from start to finish, you can cherry-pick just the parts you need. And even better: The skills you learn in this course will enable you to also write any other kind of tool with Automation Blocks.

If you want to follow along but don't want to write the block code yourself, you'll also find the block code in our [Community Library](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#access-community-library)
 in the folder `Courses/Write Your Custom Project Setup Script`.

### Class 1: Getting Started and Creating Project Bins[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-1-getting-started-and-creating-project-bins "Direct link to heading")

In this class, we get started with writing our own Automation Blocks scripts. We'll write a script that creates a new Ae project and some project bins, as well as giving a quick overview of the available blocks and learn where to find their documentation.

### Class 2: Creating a Basic UI and System Folders[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-2-creating-a-basic-ui-and-system-folders "Direct link to heading")

In this class, we extend our script so it prompts the user for a project name. The script then saves the project using that name, and also sets up a folder structure for us. On the technical side, you'll learn how to work with texts, file paths and also how to use variables.

### Class 3: Copying Files and Working with "If" Blocks[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-3-copying-files-and-working-with-if-blocks "Direct link to heading")

In this class, we extend our script so that it checks if the memory card of our camera is currently mounted. If this is the case, it copies all footage files from the camera to our project folder. Of course, you can also use this method to copy files from USB sticks or other locations.

On the technical side, you'll learn how to work with _if blocks_ and logic values in Automation Blocks. These allow you to execute a part of your script only if certain conditions are true (like if the memory card is mounted, or the active composition has a particular resolution, for example).

### Class 4: Importing Files and Syncing Entire Folders[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-4-importing-files-and-syncing-entire-folders "Direct link to heading")

In this class, you'll learn how to import files into an After Effects project with Automation Blocks. We cover both importing individual files and syncing entire system folders with project bins.

### Class 5: Creating Comps and Guides[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-5-creating-comps-and-guides "Direct link to heading")

In this class, you'll learn how to create new compositions in After Effects with Automation Blocks, and also how to add guides to comps. We also cover how to do mathematical calculations and how to retrieve attributes of compositions - as an example of this, we first retrieve the width of a composition and then divide it by two, in order to place a guide exactly in the middle of the composition.

### Class 6: Creating Shapes[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-6-creating-shapes "Direct link to heading")

In this class, you'll learn how to create solid layers and shape layers with Automation Blocks. While solids can be created with a single block, for shape layers you have to first create the layer itself and then add its contents step by step, which consists of groups, shape paths, fills, strokes etc. This modular approach also allows you to create shape layers with more complex content.

### Class 7: Getting Help, Links and Sharing With the Community[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-7-getting-help-links-and-sharing-with-the-community "Direct link to heading")

Amazing - you're almost done with this course! Last but not least, in this class you'll learn how to [ask the community for help](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#join-the-community)
 if you have any questions while you create your own Automation Block scripts. You'll also learn how to add comments and web links to your scripts and how to share them in our community library.

*   [Class 1: Getting Started and Creating Project Bins](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-1-getting-started-and-creating-project-bins)
    
*   [Class 2: Creating a Basic UI and System Folders](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-2-creating-a-basic-ui-and-system-folders)
    
*   [Class 3: Copying Files and Working with "If" Blocks](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-3-copying-files-and-working-with-if-blocks)
    
*   [Class 4: Importing Files and Syncing Entire Folders](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-4-importing-files-and-syncing-entire-folders)
    
*   [Class 5: Creating Comps and Guides](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-5-creating-comps-and-guides)
    
*   [Class 6: Creating Shapes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-6-creating-shapes)
    
*   [Class 7: Getting Help, Links and Sharing With the Community](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/writeCustomProjectSetupScript/#class-7-getting-help-links-and-sharing-with-the-community)

---

# Ae Other | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#)

On this page

Ae Other
========

### Execute Code[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#ae_execute_code "Direct link to heading")

![Block ae_execute_code](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeOther/ae_execute_code.png)

This block can execute any [Adobe ExtendScript](https://extendscript.docsforadobe.dev/)
 (jsx) code using the [After Effects scripting API](https://ae-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/)
.

**Please refer to the [documentation of the Execute Code block for Premiere Pro](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther#pr_execute_code)
 for a detailed description.** The block is identical for Ae and Pr, except for the available variable types and result types.

### Variable Types[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#variable-types "Direct link to heading")

The following types are available in Ae (both as variable type and result type of the block):

*   other (data is not converted)
*   [File](https://extendscript.docsforadobe.dev/file-system-access/file-object.html)
    
*   [Folder](https://extendscript.docsforadobe.dev/file-system-access/folder-object.html)
    
*   [Item](https://ae-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/items/item.html#item-object)
    
*   [Layer](https://ae-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/layers/layer.html#layer-object)
    
*   [Property](https://ae-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/properties/property.html)
    

Note that Automation Blocks handles subclasses properly. If you choose type "Layer" for a text layer, for example, the actual type will be [TextLayer](https://ae-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/layers/textlayer.html#textlayer-object)
 and if you choose type "Property", the result may also be a [PropertyGroup](https://ae-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/properties/propertygroup.html#propertygroup-object)
.

Execute Code - Examples[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#ae_execute_code_examples "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

We collected all examples shown below in a single Automation Blocks xml file:

[Download Examples](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/blockCode/Execute_Code_Examples_Ae.xml)

### Executing jsx Files[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#executing-jsx-files "Direct link to heading")

You can combine the Execute Code block with the [Read Text File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText#extendscript_text_file_read)
. Since script files take no arguments, you can remove all variable definitions from the Execute Code block in that case.

![Execute jsx Files with Automation Blocks for Ae](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_execute_code_example_exec_jsx_file.png)

##### $.filename

Some script files use the attribute `$.fileName` to retrieve the file path of the currently executed file. Since we first read the file contents and then eval it, this won't work properly. Instead, better use a variable, to pass information like the file path to the jsx.

### System Calls[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#system-calls "Direct link to heading")

The function [System.callSystem](https://ae-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/general/system.html#system-callsystem)
 allows to execute arbitrary sysstem commands. Since system commands are specific for the operating system, our example takes two commands as arguments: The first is executed on Windows and the second on Mac OS systems. The execute code block executes the command for the respecitive operating system and returns the result of the system call, which we save in the variable "command result":

![Execute System Commands with Automation Blocks for Ae](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_execute_code_example_call_system.png)

    var result = null;if($.os.indexOf("Windows") != -1){    result = system.callSystem(systemCommandWindows);}else{    result = system.callSystem(systemCommandMacOs);}result

### Mogrts[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#mogrts "Direct link to heading")

The [CompItem object](https://ae-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/items/compitem.html#compitem-object)
 of the Ae API has various functions for motion graphics templates. Here are some examples how to use those with Automation Blocks:

#### Get Number of Motion Graphics Controllers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#get-number-of-motion-graphics-controllers "Direct link to heading")

In this example, we retrieve the number of controllers with the execute code block and then alert the result. Note that the variable `comp` has the type `Item`, since compositions have type `CompItem` which is a subclass of `Item`.

![Retrieve number of mogrt controllers with Automation Blocks for Ae](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_execute_code_example_mogrt_get_num_controllers.png)

    comp.motionGraphicsTemplateControllerCount

#### Set Name of Motion Graphics Template[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#set-name-of-motion-graphics-template "Direct link to heading")

Here we set the name of the template shown in the Essential Graphics panel for the active composition. If the name does not update instantly in the panel, try to close and repoen the Essential Graphics panel to see the change.

![Set mogrt template name with Automation Blocks for Ae](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_execute_code_example_mogrt_set_name.png)

    comp.motionGraphicsTemplateName=newName

#### Get Name of Motion Graphics Template[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#get-name-of-motion-graphics-template "Direct link to heading")

In this example, we get the name shown in the Essential Graphics panel for the active composition. You can, of course, store the result in a variable, but here we just alert it instead.

![Get mogrt template name with Automation Blocks for Ae](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_execute_code_example_mogrt_get_name.png)

    comp.motionGraphicsTemplateName

#### Add Property to Mogrt[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#add-property-to-mogrt "Direct link to heading")

In this example, we add all selected properties of the active composition to the Essential Graphics panel of the active composition. Properties, which are not supported, are ignored. Note that we only loop over Properties and not over PropertyGroups, since those don't have the [addToMotionGraphicsTemplateAs() method](https://ae-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/properties/property.html#property-addtomotiongraphicstemplateas)
.

![Add property to mogrt with Automation Blocks for Ae](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_execute_code_example_mogrt_add_property.png)

    if(prop.canAddToMotionGraphicsTemplate(comp)){    prop.addToMotionGraphicsTemplateAs(comp, name);}

#### Export Mogrt[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#export-mogrt "Direct link to heading")

In this example, we use [CompItem.exportAsMotionGraphicsTemplate](https://ae-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/items/compitem.html#compitem-exportasmotiongraphicstemplate)
 to export the active composition as motion graphics template to a mogrt file on your desktop. The template name is used as file name. Note that we [save](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject#ae_project_save)
 the project first, since otherwise After Effects will prompt the user to save the project it if has unsaved changes. If your project has no project file, yet, you might want to use the [Save As](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject#ae_project_save_as)
 block instead.

![Export mogrt template with Automation Blocks for Ae](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_execute_code_example_mogrt_export.png)

    comp.exportAsMotionGraphicsTemplate(doOverwriteIfExisting,targetFolder.fsName)

*   [Execute Code](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#ae_execute_code)
    
*   [Variable Types](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#variable-types)
    
*   [Execute Code - Examples](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#ae_execute_code_examples)
    *   [Executing jsx Files](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#executing-jsx-files)
        
    *   [System Calls](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#system-calls)
        
    *   [Mogrts](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#mogrts)

# Ae Comp | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#)

On this page

Ae Comp
=======

### Accessing a Particular Composition[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#accessComp "Direct link to heading")

Comps are project items. So, if you need to give a block a comp as argument, you can access it in exactly the same way as you [access any other project items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
. To access the currently active composition, you can use the block [Active Compositon](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_active_comp)
.

See also [this forum post](https://community.adobe.com/t5/after-effects-discussions/automation-blocks-open-a-comp/m-p/14878440#M256424)
.

Active Composition[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_active_comp "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_active_comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeComp/ae_active_comp.png)

Returns the active composition of the project or gives an error if there is no active composition.

Has Active Composition[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_has_active_comp "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_has_active_comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeComp/ae_has_active_comp.png)

Returns the [Logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating if there is an active composition or not. You can use this with an [If Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic/#controls_if)
 to execute some code only if there is an active composition. This script shows an alert message with the name of the active composition if there is one; otherwise it shows the message `there is no active composition`:

![Example: Checking if project has an active comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_has_active_comp_example.png)

Make Active Composition[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_has_active_comp "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Automation Blocks currently has no block to make a composition the active one. But it can easily be achieved using the [Execute Code Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeOther/#ae_execute_code)
.

![Block ae_new_comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_execute_code_example_make_active_comp.png)

[Download Block Code](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/blockCode/Make%20Active%20Comp.xml)

See also [this forum post](https://community.adobe.com/t5/after-effects-discussions/automation-blocks-open-a-comp/m-p/14878440#M256424)
.

New Composition[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_new_comp "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This block creates a new composition in the project and saves it in the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 myComp (so that you can easily access it for subsequent operations on the comp). There are two ways to create a new composition with this block - either create an empty comp, or create it with the help of some clips.

### Creating an Empty Comp[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#creating-an-empty-comp "Direct link to heading")

![Block ae_new_comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeComp/ae_new_comp.png)

If you choose the option `empty composition`, you can specify

*   **name** of the new comp,
*   **target bin** inside the Ae project where the new comp should be placed (see the [Project Bin Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems#ae_itembin_path)
     for details)
*   **width** and **height** of the new comp in pixels
*   **pixel aspect** (use 1 for square pixels)
*   **duration** of the new comp (like `10s` or `100 frames`; if you specify just a number it will be treated as number of seconds)
*   **frame rate** - the number of frames per second

### Creating a Comp with Clips[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#creating-a-comp-with-clips "Direct link to heading")

![Block ae_new_comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/ae_new_comp_clips.png)

If you choose the option `comp with clip(s)`, if will create a composition whose size, duration etc fits to the clips you provide and inserts the clips into it.

You can specify the following parameters:

*   **name** of the new comp,
*   **target bin** inside the Ae project where the new comp should be placed (see the [Project Bin Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems#ae_itembin_path)
     for details)
*   **source clip(s) or bin(s)** here you can specify either a single [project item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_item_path)
     or [bin](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems#ae_itembin_path)
     or also entire [lists](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
     of them. If you specify a bin, it will use all project items inside the bins (but not items included in subbins). The comp created will include all provided clips and will have the width, height, pixel aspect and frame rate of the first video clip.
*   **layer order** must be one of
    *   `stack`: each clip starts at the beginning of the comp
    *   `sequence`: each clip starts where the previous clip ends
    *   `sequence reverse`: like `sequence` except that the layer order is reversed (i.e layer 1 will be the last clip)
    *   `sequence (audio and video separately)`: like `sequence` except that if you have both audio and video (or image) files, those will be treated as separate groups. Example: If you want to create an image slideshow with music, you can use this option. It will sequence the image files so they show up one after the other and will sequence the music files so they play one after the other while the images are shown.
    *   `sequence (audio and video separately)`: a combination of `sequence reverse` and `sequence (audio and video separately)`
*   **still image duration** is only used if your clips contain still images. You can use values like `10s` or `100 frames`; if you specify only a number it will be treated as a number of seconds.
*   **overlap duration** is only used if you choose one of the `Sequence`options, such as _Layer Order_. In that case, it specifies how much two adjacent clips overlap in time.Use values like `10s` or `100 frames`; if you specify just a number, it will be treated as a number of seconds)

Get Attribute of Comp[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_get_attribute "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_comp_get_attribute](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeComp/ae_comp_get_attribute.png)

Returns one of the attributes of the given composition, like its active camera, number of layers or motion blur settings. For a description of all available attributes see [Set Attribute of Comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute)
. Note that compositions are also project items. To access properties that are available on all project items (like the project bin where it is located) you need to use the block [Get Attribute of Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems#ae_item_get_attribute)
 (which also accepts comps as arguments).

Set Attribute of Comp[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_comp_set_attribute](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeComp/ae_comp_set_attribute.png)

Sets any of the available attributes of a composition. After you choose an attribute, the inputs of the block change as needed for that attribute. Note that compositions are also project items. To set properties that are available on all project items (like the project bin where it is located) you need to use the block [Set Attribute of Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProjectItems#ae_item_set_attribute)
 (which also accepts comps as arguments).

The available attributes of comps are listed below. If an attribute is marked as read-only, you can only get but not set this attribute.

### active camera (read-only)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_active_camera "Direct link to heading")

The layer that is the active camera of the given composition. If the composition has no active camera, it gives an error. TO check if the composition has an active camera, use the attribute [has active camera](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_has_active_camera)
.

### background color[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#background-color "Direct link to heading")

The background color as configured in the composition settings. If you get the property, the result is a hex string (like `#00ff00`). If you set it, you can provide the color in many formats - see [Ae Color](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeColor)
 for details.

### current time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#current-time "Direct link to heading")

The current time (location of the playhead) in seconds. The time is measured relative to the start of the composition, i.e. the first frame is always at 0. If you set the current time, you can specify it in various formats like `1s` or `1:15` `frame 100`. See [Ae Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime)
 for details.

If your comp starts with a different time than 0 and you want to take that into account, you need to add the [Display Start Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_display_start_time)
 to this value.

### display start frame[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_start_frame "Direct link to heading")

Returns the frame number of the first frame of the composition (as configured by the _start timecode_ in the composition settings). Note that this value is a frame number and not a timecode.

### display start time (s)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_display_start_time "Direct link to heading")

Same as [display start frame](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_start_frame)
 but the result is a time in seconds instead of frames.

### downsample factor (resolution)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#downsample-factor-resolution "Direct link to heading")

![After Effects Down Sampling Factor](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/comp_attribute_down_sampling_factor.png)

The factor by which the x and y resolution of the Composition panel is downsampled. You can specify either a single number, or a [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 (array) of two numbers to set different values for x and y.

**Examples:**

To do all calculations in full resolution, you can either set the downsample factor to `1` or to the list `[1,1]`. A value of `4` sets the resolution to _Quarter_.

The list `[1,4]` calculates every pixel in x dimension (100%) but only every 4th pixel in y dimention (25%).

### duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#duration "Direct link to heading")

The duration of the composition in seconds. For details on time values, see [Ae Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime)
.

### Essential Graphics name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#essential-graphics-name "Direct link to heading")

![After Effects Essential Graphics Name](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/comp_attribute_essential_graphics_name.png)

The name for the comp as specified in the Essential Graphics panel (not the same as the [name of the comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_name)
). The name in the Essential Graphics panel is used for the file name of an exported Motion Graphics template.

### frame blending[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#frame-blending "Direct link to heading")

![After Effects Frame Blending Attribute](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/comp_attribute_frame_blending.png)

The [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false`, indicating if Frame Blending is enabled for the composition.

### frame duration (s)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#frame-duration-s "Direct link to heading")

The duration of a single frame in seconds. If the frame rate of the comp is 25fps, for example, the duration is 1/25s = 0.04s.

### frame rate[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#frame-rate "Direct link to heading")

The number of frames per second of the comp.

### has active camera (read-only)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_has_active_camera "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true`or `false` indicating if the comp has an active camera or not. If the value is `true`, you can retrieve the active camera layer with the attribute [active camera](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_active_camera)

### height[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_height "Direct link to heading")

The height of the composition in pixels (see also attribute [width](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_width)
 and [size](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_size)
.

### hide shy layers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#hide-shy-layers "Direct link to heading")

![After Effects Shy Layers](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/comp_attribute_shy.png)

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating whether shy layers should be hidden in the timeline or not.

### motion blur[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_motion_blur "Direct link to heading")

![After Effects Enable Motion Blur](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/comp_attribute_motion_blur.png)

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating whether motion blur is enabled for the comp or not.

Note that you can also activate motion blur [for individual layers](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer#ae_layer_set_attribute_motion_blur)
.

### motion blur - adaptive sample limit[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#motion-blur---adaptive-sample-limit "Direct link to heading")

The _Adaptive Sample Limit_ value in the _Advanced_ tab of the _Composition Settings_. Must be an integer number in the range between 2 and 64. If you provide numbers outside that range, Automation Blocks will clip them for you to stay in that range.

### motion blur - samples per frame[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#motion-blur---samples-per-frame "Direct link to heading")

The _Samples per Frame_ value in the _Advanced_ tab of the _Composition Settings_. Must be an integer number in the range between 20 and 256. If you provide numbers outside that range, Automation Blocks will clip them for you to stay in that range.

### motion blur - shutter angle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#motion-blur---shutter-angle "Direct link to heading")

The _Shutter Angle_ value in the _Advanced_ tab of the _Composition Settings_. Must be an integer number in the range between 0 and 720 degrees. If you provide numbers outside that range, Automation Blocks will clip them for you to stay within that range.

### motion blur - shutter phase[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#motion-blur---shutter-phase "Direct link to heading")

The _Shutter Phase_ value in the _Advanced_ tab of the _Composition Settings_. Must be an integer number in the range between -360 and 360 degrees. If you provide numbers outside that range, Automation Blocks will clip them for you to stay within that range.

### name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_name "Direct link to heading")

The name of the composition (which is identical to the name of the project item in the project panel).

### number of layers (read-only)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#number-of-layers-read-only "Direct link to heading")

The number of layers that the composition has. To do something with all layers, use the block [For Each Layer](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer#ae_layer_for_all)
 and to get a list of all layers, you can also use the block [List of Layers](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer#ae_layer_list)
.

### preserve nested framerate[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#preserve-nested-framerate "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` representing the option _"Preserve frame rate when nested or in render queue"_ in the _Advanced_ tab of the _Composition Settings_.

### preserve nested resolution[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#preserve-nested-resolution "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` representing the option _"Preserve resolution when nested"_ in the _Advanced_ tab of the _Composition Settings_.

### renderer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#renderer "Direct link to heading")

The renderer that you can configure in the _3D Renderer_ tab of the _Composition Settings_. If you get this value, the name will be one of the following internal names:

*   `ADBE Advanced 3d` for the Classic 3D Renderer
*   `ADBE Ernst` for the Cinema 4D Renderer

If you want to set this value, by default the block shows a convenient drop down where you can pick the renderer. However, if you want to read the renderer name from a [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
, for example, you can simply drag the variable on top of the dropdown to replace it. In that case, your variable should contain a string with either an internal name (`ADBE Advanced 3d`, `ADBE Ernst`) or the normal renderer name (`Classic 3D` or `Cinema 4D`). Upper/lower case and spaces in the names are ignored.

### size \[w,h\][​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_size "Direct link to heading")

Returns a [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 (array) containing the width and height of the composition in pixels. (If you just need one of the two values, you can use the attribute [width](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_width)
 or [height](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_height)
 instead)

### uses drop-frame timecode[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#uses-drop-frame-timecode "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` representing the "Drop Frame/Non-Drop Frame" option of the Frame Rate in the Composition Settings.

### width[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_width "Direct link to heading")

The width of the composition in pixels (see also attribute [height](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_height)
 and [size](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_set_attribute_size)
.

### work area duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#work-area-duration "Direct link to heading")

The duration of the work area. For details on time values see [Ae Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime)
. If you change the duration, the start of the work area will stay where it is and the end will update according to the new duration.

### work area end[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#work-area-end "Direct link to heading")

The end of the work area. For details on time values see [Ae Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime)
. If you set the end of the work area, the duration will update accordingly. If the new end is before the current start, the start will update too (so the work area will have a duration of 0).

### work area start[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#work-area-start "Direct link to heading")

The start of the work area. For details on time values see [Ae Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeTime)
. If you set the start, the end won't change with it (unless the new start is behind the old end, which will result in a work area with duration 0)

For Each Frame[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#for_each_frame "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block for_each_frame](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeComp/for_each_frame.png)

This block is a [loop](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/loops)
 which allows you to do something on each frame of the given composition or each frame of the work area. The blocks that you nest inside this block are executed many times, namely one time for each frame. During each round the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `my frame time` is set to the time (in seconds) of the respective frame.

**comp/layers** if you choose the option `layers` instead of `comp` you can replace the parameter `active composition` with either a single layer or a [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 (array) of layers. This is similar to the work area option, except that the time range is limited to the area of the given layers instead of the work area. More precisely, in that case the block will only iterate over the frames which are in between the earliest of the in points of the given layers and the last of their out points.

**only in work area** is a [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 (`true` or `false`), which allows you to only loop over the frames of the work area instead of all frames in the comp.

**step size** allows you to skip frames. Hence, for a value of `10 frames`, the nested blocks will only be executed once for every 10th frame and the frames in between are skipped.

Duplicate Comp[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_duplicate "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_comp_duplicate](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeComp/ae_comp_duplicate.png)

With this block you can duplicate a composition.

**new comp var**

After the comp has been duplicated, the variable `new comp var` will point to that duplicated comp. Hence, if you want to do some changes in the duplicated comp in subsequent blocks of your script, you can access it easily using this variable.

Here is an example, which first duplicates a composition and then sets the name of the duplicate to _"my duplicate comp name"_:

![After Effects script: Duplicate Composition and Rename it](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeComp/ae_comp_duplicate_example.png)

Note how the second block, which does the renaming, uses `new comp var` to access the composition.

**duplicate precomps**

If this[logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 is `true`, the block will check if the composition has any precomps. If it has precomps, then not only the composition but also those precomps will be duplicated. The block makes sure that if the same precomp is used multiple times, that it is duplicated only once.

**fix expressions**

This option will check all expressions in the duplicated composition (and precomps, if that option is enabled). If those expressions contain the name of the comp (or any of the precomps), then this name will be replaced by the name of the duplicate comp. Note that this option only touches expressions in the newly created comps. In all other comps of the project, the expressions are not touched (i.e. they will still point to the original and not to the duplicated comps).

Clear Guides[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_clear_guides "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_comp_clear_guides](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeComp/ae_comp_clear_guides.png)

Removes all guides (in horizontal or vertical direction, or both) from the given composition. You can also give an entire [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 (array) of compositions as argument. In that case, the guides of all those comps are removed.

Add Guides[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_add_guides "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_comp_add_guides](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeComp/ae_comp_add_guides.png)

Add horizontal or vertical guides to the given comp. You can also give an entire [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 (array) of compositions as an argument. In that case, the guides are added to all those comps.

### Adding multiple guides[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#adding-multiple-guides "Direct link to heading")

Instead of a single position, you can also give a [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 (array) of numbers as a position argument. In that case, one new guide is added for each list entry.

List of Guides[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeComp/#ae_comp_get_guides "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block ae_comp_get_guides](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/AeComp/ae_comp_get_guides.png)

Returns a [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 (array) of numbers containing the positions of all vertical or horizontal guides of the given composition. The list is sorted. Note that the positions are not points, but single numbers - for vertical guides, they represent the position of the guide in the x direction and for horizontal guides the position of the guide in the y direction.

# Linking Scripts or Presets to Keyboard Shortcuts | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/keyboardShortcuts/#)

On this page

Linking Scripts or Presets to Keyboard Shortcuts
================================================

In this tutorial, you'll learn how to bind Automation Blocks tools in Adobe After Effects to keyboard shortcuts. You'll also learn how to use this method to bind any After Effects preset to a keyboard shortcut.

Hotkeys and Other Triggers in Pr + Ae[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/keyboardShortcuts/#hotkeys-and-other-triggers-in-pr--ae "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

While the method shown here only works in After Effects, there is also a second method to trigger Automation Blocks scripts with keyboard shortcuts both in Ae and Pr. With this alternative method yo cannot just trigger your scripts using hotkeys, but also from any external sources, like your phone, external devices or other software.

[Learn More about Triggering AB Scripts](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/remoteExecution)

*   [Hotkeys and Other Triggers in Pr + Ae](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-ae/keyboardShortcuts/#hotkeys-and-other-triggers-in-pr--ae)

# Pr Sequence Clips | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#)

On this page

Pr Sequence Clips
=================

In contrast to [Project Items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems)
 (or short: items), which denote an item in the project panel, sequence clips (or short: clips) denote whenm this type of item occurs inside a sequence. If some footage is used several times in a sequence, you can have multiple clips using the same item.

### Accessing a Particular Sequence Clip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#accessSequenceClip "Direct link to heading")

**For Each Clip Block:** Usually the best way to access a sequence clip is to use the [For Each Clip Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#block_pr_for_all_sequence_items)
. This comes with very powerful filtering features to select all kinds of different clips, like only clips in a particular track, only selected clips, or only tracks at the current time.

**Path texts** like project items and sequences, clips are also represented by texts in Automation blocks. These texts consist of two parts separated by `::`. The part before `::` describes the sequence in which the clip is contained, and the part after it describes exactly where it is located in that sequence.

A typical path looks like this: `my bin\my sequence name::V1\3` This text says that there is a sequence named `my sequence name` inside the project bin `my bin` and that the project item we want is the third item in video track V1.

To specify a sequence name (the part of the text before the `::`), you can do everything that is described in the section [Accessing a Particular Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
, i.e. you can specify only a name, a name with a path, and also include a prefix for the project. If you leave the part before the `::` empty, the active sequence is used:

`::A2\1` denotes the first clip in the audio track 2 of the active sequence.

To specify the exact location of the clip, you first need to specify a track, which can be done by index (like `V1`, `V2`... for the video tracks or `A1`, `A2`... for the audio tracks) or by name, like `Video 3`. Note that track names are usually only shown when the track is expanded. See here [how to rename tracks](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro/how-to-rename-video-track/m-p/10910015)
.

The track is followed by a backslash and after it you can put the index or name of a clip. So `::V1\3` denotes the third clip in the track V1 of the active sequence and `::V1\my title` denotes the clip with the name `my title` in the track V1. Note that by default, clips have the same name as the project item they correspond to - so all clips using the same item have the same name. To give your clips in Premiere Pro unique names, you can right-click on a clip in the sequence and choose **rename...** in the menu that pops up. This will only rename the clip in the sequence and not the corresponding project item in the project panel.

For Each Clip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#block_pr_for_all_sequence_items "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_for_all_sequence_items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequenceClips/block_pr_for_all_sequence_items.png)

This block iterates over clips in in the given sequence. The blocks you nest inside this block are executed once for each of those clips. During the execution, the respective clip is stored in the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `my clip`.

With the following options you can configure which clips you want to iterate over:

#### For Each Clip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#for-each-clip "Direct link to heading")

Here you have the following options:

*   For each clip
*   For each selected clip
*   For each selected clip at current time
*   For each clip at current time

#### Tracks[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#tracks "Direct link to heading")

You can limit the block to only the items of a particular track. Your options are

*   all tracks
*   all video tracks
*   all audio tracks
*   just a single audio or video track

#### Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#sequence "Direct link to heading")

By default, this block iterates over the clips of the active sequence. For all possible ways to specify a sequence see [Accessing a Particular Sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#accessSequence)
.

##### Selecting a single clip

You can easily create selections that always consist only of (at most) one clip, like "for each clip at current time in track V1". In those cases you can make sure that the iteration is executed only once (or not at all, if the track is empty at the current time).

Get Attribute of Clip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#pr_track_item_get_property "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_track_item_get_property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequenceClips/pr_track_item_get_property.png)

Returns one of the following properties of the given sequence clip (for all the ways you can specify a clip, see [Accessing a Particular Sequence Clip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#accessSequenceClip)
)

### disabled[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#disabled "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating if the clip is disabled in the timeline.

### duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#duration "Direct link to heading")

Duration of the clip in the sequence in seconds. This is the duration from in to out point. If you need the full length of the footage instead, use the [Get Attribute of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#pr_item_get_property)
 (read only)

### end time in sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#end-time-in-sequence "Direct link to heading")

The time in the sequence where the clip ends (in seconds; a value of 0 corresponds to the beginning of the sequence). At this time, the frame of the footage given by [out point](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#out-point)
 is visible.

For a detailed explanation of in point, out point, sequence start and end time, see the [Convert Time Between Sequence and Clip Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime#pr_clip_time_to_sequence_time)

### in point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#in-point "Direct link to heading")

The in point of this clip (in seconds; a value of 0 corresponds to the beginning of the footage). Note that this is not the same as the in point of the project item (which you can get by using this clip with the [Get Attribute of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#pr_item_get_property)
) instead of the _Get Property of Clip_ Block. Each clip that uses a project item has its own in and out points, which correspond to the section of the footage that this clip uses. The project item itself has yet another in and out point, which corresponds to the in and out points you choose in the source monitor.

For a detailed explanation of in point, out point, sequence start and end time, see the [Convert Time Between Sequence and Clip Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime#pr_clip_time_to_sequence_time)

### is adjustment layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#is-adjustment-layer "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating if the clip is an adjustment layer. (read only)

### match name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#match-name "Direct link to heading")

The [match name of the trackitem](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/item/trackitem.html#trackitem-matchname)
 internally used by Pr. (read only)

### media type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#media-type "Direct link to heading")

A text, either `Audio` or `Video`. (read only)

### name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#name "Direct link to heading")

The name of the clip. Note that this is not the same as the name of the project item of this clip. By default these two are identical, but you can right-click on a clip in your sequence and rename it. This will not rename the project item, only the sequence clip.

### nodeId[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#nodeid "Direct link to heading")

The [nodeId of the trackitem](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/item/trackitem.html#trackitem-nodeid)
 internally used by Pr. (read only)

### out point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#out-point "Direct link to heading")

The out point of this clip (in seconds; a value of 0 corresponds to the beginning of the footage). Note that this is not the same as the out point of the project item (which you can get by using this clip with the [Get Attribute of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#pr_item_get_property)
) instead of the _Get Property of Clip_ Block. Each clip that uses a project item has its own in and out points, which correspond to the section of the footage that this clip uses. The project item itself has yet another in and out point, which corresponds to the in and out points you choose in the source monitor.

For a detailed explanation of in point, out point, sequence start and end time, see the [Convert Time Between Sequence and Clip Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime#pr_clip_time_to_sequence_time)

### project item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#project-item "Direct link to heading")

The [project item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems)
 of this clip or [null](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic#logic_null)
, if the clip has no corresponding project item. Note that whenever a block expects a project item as argument and you give it a sequence clip instead, Automation Blocks converts it to the corresponding project item for you (if the clip has a corresponding project item). Therefore, accessing the project item attribute explicitly is often not needed. But it is very helpful to check if this attribute is null, if you want to check if there is a corresponding project item.

### selected[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#selected "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating if the clip is currently selected.

### speed factor[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#speed-factor "Direct link to heading")

The speed multiplier applied to the clip (read only)

### speed is reversed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#speed-is-reversed "Direct link to heading")

A [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `true` or `false` indicating if the clip speed is reversed. (read only)

### start time in sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#start-time-in-sequence "Direct link to heading")

The time in the sequence where the clip starts (in seconds; a value of 0 corresponds to the beginning of the sequence). At this time, the frame of the footage given by [in point](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#in-point)
 is visible.

For a detailed explanation of in point, out point, sequence start and end time, see the [Convert Time Between Sequence and Clip Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime#pr_clip_time_to_sequence_time)

### track name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#track-name "Direct link to heading")

The name of the track in which the item is located (read only)

Set Attribute of Clip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#pr_track_item_set_property "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_track_item_set_property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequenceClips/pr_track_item_set_property.png)

Sets any property of the given sequence clip (for all the ways you can specify a clip, see [Accessing a Particular Sequence Clip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#accessSequenceClip)
)

For a description of the available properties, see the [Get Clip Property Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#pr_track_item_get_property)
. Properties which are read only are only available in _Get Clip Property_, not in _Set Clip Property_.

*   [Accessing a Particular Sequence Clip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips/#accessSequenceClip)

# Pr Time | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#)

On this page

Pr Time
=======

The blocks in this this section help you to convert time and timecode values from and to different formats.

Automatic Time Conversion[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#automatic-time-conversion "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

In general, Automation Blocks returns any time values as a point number in seconds. However, if a block expects a value to be a time, Automation Blocks does its best to also convert any other values into that format.

Specifically, it does the following:

*   if you provide a number, Automation Blocks assumes that this is a value in seconds and uses it as is
*   texts which look like numbers are converted to a number in seconds. This works for texts like `14` or `14.3` and numbers followed by a label like `14s`, `14 secs`, or `14 seconds`
*   texts of the form `00:00`, `3m 4s`, `3 min 4 secs` etc. are converted from minutes and seconds to a value in seconds
*   texts of the form `1h 3m 4s` or `1hrs 3min 4secs` or `1 hour 3 minutes 4 seconds` etc. are converted from hours, minutes and seconds to a value in seconds
*   texts of the form `00:00:00:000` and `00:00:00,000` are considered to be hours, minutes, seconds and milliseconds and converted to a value in seconds

##### Frame Numbers

If you work with frame numbers, Automation Blocks does not convert these automatically. It considers simple numbers to be values in seconds, so if you have frame numbers, you'll need to use the [Convert Timecode to Time Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime#pr_time_from_timecode)
 to convert the frame number to a value in seconds.

##### timecode

Like frame numbers, timecodes also cannot be automatically converted into a value in seconds. The reason is that the timecode `00:00:00:01`, for example, can mean different times - depending on the framerate of the sequence! Therefore, you need to convert timecode values to time using the [Convert Timecode to Time Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime#pr_time_from_timecode)
, which you can use to specify the framerate that should be used.

Format Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#pr_format_time "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_format_time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrTime/pr_format_time.png)

This block can format the given time value in all kinds of ways. By default, all time values that Automation Blocks return are a simple number which corresponds to the time in seconds. This is useful to do calculations with these values, but when you want to write time values to a file, for example, you often want to format it in a different way. With this block, you can convert a time to any of the following formats:

#### available formats[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#available-formats "Direct link to heading")

*   seconds (`148s`)
*   M:SS (`3:04`)
*   MM:SS (`03:04`)
*   H:MM:SS (`0:03:04`)
*   H:MM:SS,MS (`0:03:04,000`)
*   HH:MM:SS,MS (`00:03:04,000`)

If you need to convert the time to a timecode format, use the [Convert Time to Timecode Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime#pr_time_to_timecode)
 or the [Convert Time to Timecode of Sequence Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime#pr_time_to_timecode_of_sequence)
 instead.

##### accuracy

Note that by converting values to these formats, you lose accuracy - even with the format that involves milliseconds. If your time is `1.2345 seconds`, for example, and you convert it to H:MM:SS,MS then the number is rounded to `0:00:01,235`.

Convert Time to Timecode[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#pr_time_to_timecode "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_time_to_timecode](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrTime/pr_time_to_timecode.png)

This block converts the time to any timecode format. For most timecode formats, only the time value - no additional information - is needed for the conversion. But for some timecode formats, like `frames` (which just outputs a frame number), you need to provide the framerate that should be used for this conversion. An extra framerate input appears when you choose one of these formats:

![example framerate](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_time_to_timecode_example_framerate.png)

Instead of a number, you can also provide a sequence as an example, to use the framerate of that sequence:

![example framerate from sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_time_to_timecode_example_sequence.png)

Convert Time to Timecode of Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#pr_time_to_timecode_of_sequence "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_time_to_timecode_of_sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrTime/pr_time_to_timecode_of_sequence.png)

This block works exactly the same as the [Convert Time to Timecode Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime#pr_time_to_timecode)
, except that you don't have to choose a timecode format. Instead, the block uses the timecode format that Premiere Pro is using in the given sequence (and if a framerate is required, it is also taken from that sequence).

This is useful if you want to display timecodes in exactly the same way as Premiere does (if the user changes the time display for a sequence in Premiere Pro, the behavior of the block changes accordingly).

Convert Timecode to Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#pr_time_from_timecode "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_time_from_timecode](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrTime/pr_time_from_timecode.png)

This block converts texts of any of the following formats into a time value in seconds:

*   `00:00:00:00`
*   `00:00:00:00000`
*   `frame 0`
*   `0 frames`

Note that texts of the form `1234` are considered to be a value in seconds, but texts of the form `frame 1234` are considered to be a frame number. Hence, if you have a frame number value, just put the text `frame` before it to make sure it is interpreted correctly. You can use the [Text Pattern](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#string_pattern)
, for example, to add such a prefix. The following example converts the frame number `123` to a value in seconds (assuming the framerate of the active sequence)

![convert frame number to time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_time_from_timecode_frame_number_example.png)

#### frame rate[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#frame-rate "Direct link to heading")

The exact time that a timecode corresponds to depends on the framerate (the number of frames per second). Hence, you need to specify a framerate that is used for the calculation. You can specify the framerate either as a number, like `24` or `23.976023976024`, a text like `24 fps`, or give a sequence, project item or sequence clip object instead, to use the framerate of that sequence or footage.

Convert Time Between Sequence and Clip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#pr_clip_time_to_sequence_time "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_clip_time_to_sequence_time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrTime/pr_clip_time_to_sequence_time.png)

Time values related to a clip in a sequence can be measured in different ways:

*   **clip time** measures the time so that `0` is the beginning of the footage file (and the clip starts in the footage at its [in point](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#in-point)
    )
*   **sequence time** measures the time so that `0` is the beginning of the sequence (and the clip starts in the sequence at its [start time in sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#start-time-in-sequence)
    

![Sequence Time vs Clip Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/sequence_vs_clip_time@2x.png)

This block converts between a time value in sequence time and clip time, or vice versa. If you convert the in point of a clip from clip time to sequence time, for example, you get the sequence start time of the clip.

*   [Automatic Time Conversion](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#automatic-time-conversion)
    
*   [Format Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#pr_format_time)
    
*   [Convert Time to Timecode](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#pr_time_to_timecode)
    
*   [Convert Time to Timecode of Sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#pr_time_to_timecode_of_sequence)
    
*   [Convert Timecode to Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#pr_time_from_timecode)
    
*   [Convert Time Between Sequence and Clip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime/#pr_clip_time_to_sequence_time)

Favorite Clip Importer
======================

Do you have some logos, intros or other clips you need in almost every Premiere Pro project? Learn how to write your own custom tool to do all of this in a single click:

*   import your favorite file in Pr
*   place it in the active sequence
*   modify clip properties like its position or scale

You even learn how to create a user interface for your custom importer tool, such that you can easily pick any of your favorite files:

# Export Clip Details to Spreadsheet | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/exportClipDetailsToSpreadsheet/#)

Export Clip Details to Spreadsheet
==================================

In the Adobe forum [Emily asked](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro-discussions/metadata/m-p/14297414#M484867)
 how to export the in and out points and other details of all clips of a sequence to a spreadsheet.

Since this is a perfect job for Automation Blocks, I created a ten minute tutorial showing how to create an Automation Blocks tool for this purpose. You learn how to create a spreadsheet, how to choose which data to put in which column and also how to convert between time and timecode values to get exactly the spreadsheet you want.

Here is the full code of the tool:

![Automation Blocks script to export clip details to a spreadsheet](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/exportClipDetailsToSpreadsheet/exportClipDetailsToSpreadsheet.png)

##### Existing Spreadsheet File

Make sure the spreadsheet file does not exist, yet, if you run the tool. If it exists already, Automation Blocks will append the new spreadsheet data to the existing file. Of course, you can also add a block to the beginning of the block script, which [deletes the file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_delete_file)
 if it exists, but I wanted to keep the block code simple.

# Custom Metadata Tools | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/metadata/#)

Custom Metadata Tools
=====================

Precision in metadata annotation is key to maintaining efficient workflows, especially within a collaborative team environment. With the help of Automation Blocks for Premiere Pro, you can streamline this process, making it both faster and less error-prone. In this tutorial, we'll guide you through the process of creating your very own custom tools for modifying metadata in Premiere Pro using the extension's low-code approach. By simply combining a handful of blocks, you'll unlock the potential to enhance your video editing experience.

As a practical example, we'll demonstrate how to create a tool that annotates the current editor working on a sequence. However, this technique can be applied to a wide range of metadata customization.

In this tutorial, you'll discover:

*   How to create custom metadata properties.
*   How to set metadata using Automation Blocks.
*   How to design a user-friendly interface with text fields and dropdown lists.
*   How to apply metadata changes to the active sequence or selected project items.

Join us in mastering the art of metadata annotation with Automation Blocks for Premiere Pro and watch your video editing workflow soar to new heights!
