# Comp Time Tools | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/compTimeTools/#)

On this page

Comp Time Tools
===============

Insert Frames at Current Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/compTimeTools/#insertFramesAtCurrentTime "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/insertFramesAtCurrentTime.gif)

Did you ever want to make some part of your animation longer and wondered how to shift everything in time as needed?

This tool offers an easy dialog to insert extra frames in the active composition at the current time:

![Insert Extra Frames In After Effects Comp At Current Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/insertFramesAtCurrentTimeDialog.png)

After've you entered the time, it will

*   change the duration of the active composition
*   shift all keyframes and markers in time as needed, if they are on layers which overlap with the current time
*   shift all layers in time as needed, if they start after the current time
*   also insert extra frames in any precomps (and their precomps), if they overlap with the current time

##### Custom Value Keyframes are not shifted in time

For all keyframed properties which overlap with the current time, the tool shifts the keyframes after the current time as needed. For properties with normal property values like numbers, texts, dropdown options or colors, this works fine. However, if you've keyframed a custom value like the _histogram_ of the _Levels_ effect or the _curves_ of the _Curves_ effect property, these cannot be shifted in time, due to a limitation of the Ae scripting API.

Note that this is only an issue for layers which overlap with the current time. Keyframes of layers before the current time don't need to be shifted, and for layers which start after the current time, the tool shifts the entire layer instead of the keyframes.

If some keyframes could not be moved, a warning message is written to the console of Automation Blocks. If you see any warnings there, just move the keyframes of the properties mentioned manually.

Insert Leading Handle Frames[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/compTimeTools/#insertLeadingHandleFrames "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/insertLeadingCompHandle.gif)

Have you ever needed to add some additional frames at the beginning of a composition? With this tool, there's no need to move layers around manually or fiddle with the timing of precomps.

![Add frames at beginning of Ae comp](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/compAddLeadingHandleDialog.png)

Just tell the tool how much time you want to add, and it will:

*   change the duration and start time code of the active comp
*   shift all layers in time as needed
*   also inserts extra frames at the beginning of precomps, if those precomps start at the beginning of the comp
*   if the active comp is used as precomp in other comps, those precomp layers are adjusted to make sure everything stays where it was after inserting the frames. More precisely, the precomp layers are shifted in time and any keyframes and markers on them are shifted in the opposite direction, so they stay in place.

##### Differences to _Insert Frames at Current Time_

This tool is similar to the [Insert Frames at Current Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/compTimeTools/#insertFramesAtCurrentTime)
 tool, but:

*   it inserts the extra frames at the beginning of the composition instead of the current time
*   it adjusts comps which use the active comp as precomps
*   it processes a bit faster (since inserting frames at the beginning of the comp always just requires shifting layers and never individual keyframes)
*   if changes the _start time code_ of the comp

Set Comp Duration Dialog[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/compTimeTools/#SetCompDurationDialog "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Comp Duration Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/compDurationDialog.png)

This script offers a dialog to quickly change the duration of the active comp. This is very similar to changing the comp duration in the comp settings dialog, except that you can enter the duration in all kinds of formats like:

*   `10` (for 10 seconds)
*   `10s`
*   `0:10`
*   `0:00:10`
*   `250 frames`
*   `1m 3s`

### Customize: Change Other Comp Properties[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/compTimeTools/#customize-change-other-comp-properties "Direct link to heading")

You can customize the dialog to change all kinds of other comp properties, instead of the duration. Just choose a different property in the two dropdowns:

![Customize Script to Change Comp Properties](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/setCompDurationDialogCustomize.png)

*   [Insert Frames at Current Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/compTimeTools/#insertFramesAtCurrentTime)
    
*   [Insert Leading Handle Frames](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/compTimeTools/#insertLeadingHandleFrames)
    
*   [Set Comp Duration Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/compTimeTools/#SetCompDurationDialog)
    *   [Customize: Change Other Comp Properties](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/compTimeTools/#customize-change-other-comp-properties)

# Pr Other | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#)

On this page

Pr Other
========

Execute Code[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#pr_execute_code "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_execute_code](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrOther/pr_execute_code.png)

This block can execute any [Adobe ExtendScript](https://extendscript.docsforadobe.dev/)
 (jsx) code using the [Premiere Pro scripting API](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/)
.

Automation Blocks also uses the Premiere Pro scripting API under the hood and most of its functionality can be used without writing any programming code. But whenever you need a function of the scripting API that is not covered by Automation Blocks natively, you can use this block.

### Tutorial[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#tutorial "Direct link to heading")

Learn how to combine block-based scripting with ExtendScript and ChatGPT to unlock advanced Premiere Pro automation using the Execute Code block.

[Watch Tutorial](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/executeCodeAndChatGPT)

### Variables[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#variables "Direct link to heading")

At the top of the block, you can configure variables, which are passed from Automation Blocks to the ExtendScript code. By default, you have a single variable "myName1", but you can change this name freely. Just make sure to follow the rules for variable names in JavaScript, i.e. in contrast to [variables in Automation Blocks](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
, the name may not contains spaces, for example.

To add more variables, use the blue cog wheel icon at the top left of the block:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_add_variables.png)

### Variable Types[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#variable-types "Direct link to heading")

By default, variables have the type "other", which means that Automation Blocks just passes the data to ExtendScript without any modification. This is fine for numbers, strings, or [lists](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 (which are represented as JavaScrript arrays). But let's say you set the variable value to the [project item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#pr_item_path)
 "\\footage\\logo.png":

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_variable_types.png)

Then with type "other" the variable would contain the string "\\footage\\logo.png", but if we choose the type "ProjectItem", Automation Blocks converts it to a [ProjectItem object](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/item/projectitem.html)
. Therefore, we can call the method `myItem.setOffline()` of the ProjectItem object in the jsx code.

The following types are available:

*   other (data is not converted)
*   [File](https://extendscript.docsforadobe.dev/file-system-access/file-object.html)
    
*   [Folder](https://extendscript.docsforadobe.dev/file-system-access/folder-object.html)
    
*   [Project](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/general/project.html)
    
*   [ProjectItem](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/item/projectitem.html)
    
*   [Sequence](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/sequence/sequence.html)
    
*   [Track](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/sequence/track.html)
    
*   [TrackItem](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/item/trackitem.html)
    
*   [Time](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/other/time.html#time-object)
    

Automation Blocks will do its best to convert anything to the chosen type. So if you choose type "Sequence", for example, and give it a TrackItem, then it will convert it to the sequence, which contains the track item.

### Returning a Result[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#returning-a-result "Direct link to heading")

By default, the execute code block has result type "none", which means that the execution result of the jsx code is discarded. However, if you choose another result type, the shape of the block changes, such that you can plug it into other blocks as a value. Here is a same block with the result type "none" and "other":

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_returning_result.png)

The result of the code is always the value of the last statement, i.e. if you have a code

    statement 1;statement 2;statement 3

then the end result will be the result of statement 3. You cannot use `return` to return a value (since internally the code is executed with eval and it is not a function call). A good pattern is usually to create a variable `result` and then simply put that variable value as the last statement, such that it is returned:

    var result;// do everything you want here and assign your return value to resultresult  // this value will be returned

Execute Code - Examples[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#pr_execute_code_examples "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

We collected all examples shown below in a single Automation Blocks xml file:

[Download Examples](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/blockCode/Execute_Code_Examples_Pr.xml)

### Executing jsx Files[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#executing-jsx-files "Direct link to heading")

You can combine the Execute Code block with the [Read Text File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText#extendscript_text_file_read)
. Since script files take no arguments, you can remove all variable definitions from the Execute Code block in that case.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_exec_jsx_file.png)

##### $.filename

Some script files use the attribute `$.fileName` to retrieve the file path of the currently executed file. Since we first read the file contents and then eval it, this won't work properly. Instead, better use a variable, to pass information like the file path to the jsx.

### ProjectItem: Create Smart Bin[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#projectitem-create-smart-bin "Direct link to heading")

This block creates a new smart bin and saves it in the Automation Blocks variable `smart bin`:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_smart_bin.png)

    var result = parentBin.createSmartBin(    smartBinName,     queryString);result

Note that the variable `parentBin` and the result have type "ProjectItem" whereas the variables `smartBinName` and `queryString` represent basic strings and hence keep the default type "other". Also note that the [documentation of ProjectItem.createSmartBin](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/item/projectitem.html#projectitem-createsmartbin)
 says that it returns 0, but in practice it looks like it is actually returning the item of the new bin. Hence, we also return it.

### ProjectItem: Set Offline[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#projectitem-set-offline "Direct link to heading")

We've shown the example above already: To set a project item offline use the code

    myItem.setOffline();

with a variable `myItem` of type "ProjectItem":

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_variable_types.png)

### ProjectItem: Import Mogrt[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#projectitem-import-mogrt "Direct link to heading")

This example inserts the motion graphics template contained in the file _test.mogrt_ on your desktop into the active sequence into tracks V1 and A1 at time 0s:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_import_mogrt.png)

    var newTrackItem = seq.importMGT(mogrtFile.fsName, time.ticks, videoTrack-1, audioTrack-1);if (!newTrackItem) throw new Error("track item could not be created");if (!(newTrackItem.projectItem)) throw new Error("track item of mogrt has no project item");newTrackItem.projectItem

Note that currently, Automation Blocks does not support track items as result type. Therefore, we return the corresponding project item instead.

### ProjectItem: Audio Channel Mappings[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#projectitem-audio-channel-mappings "Direct link to heading")

This example sets [the audio channel mapping](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/other/audiochannelmapping.html#audiochannelmapping-object)
 of a project item:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_audio_channel_mapping.png)

    if(myItem){    var mapping = myItem.getAudioChannelMapping    if(mapping){        for(var i=0; i< map.length; i++){            mapping.setMappingForChannel(i,map[i]);        }        myItem.setAudioChannelMapping(mapping);    }    else {        throw new Error("Current audio mapping unavailable for item "+myItem.treePath);    }}else {    throw new Error("ProjectItem not found");}

In the screenshot above, we used the [list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 `[0,0]` as map, to map both the left and right channel of the clip to the left media source channel (and replacing a 0 by 1 would map to the right media source channel). For clips with more channels, you can simply use a list with more entries.

Here is a second example, which changes the audio channel mapping of a stereo file, such that it uses two mono tracks instead of one stereo track in the timeline:

![Premiere Pro: Set Stereo Clip to Two Mono Channels with Automation Blocks](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_audio_channel_mapping_2.png)

    if(myItem){    var mapping = myItem.getAudioChannelMapping    if(mapping){        // possible values for audioChannelsType:        // 0=mono; 1=stereo; 2=5.1       mapping.audioChannelsType = 0;         mapping.audioClipsNumber = 2;          myItem.setAudioChannelMapping(mapping);    }    else {        throw new Error("Current audio mapping unavailable for item "+myItem.treePath);    }}else {    throw new Error("ProjectItem not found");}

To chieve this, we set the audioChannesType of the [the audio channel mapping](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/other/audiochannelmapping.html#audiochannelmapping-object)
 to 0 (0=mono; 1=stereo; 2=5.1) and the audioClipsNumber to 2, since we need two mono tracks for the stereo data.

Audio Channel Mappings are also discussed in [this forum thread](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro-discussions/automation-blocks-interpret-footage-automation-audio-channel-mapping/m-p/14473377#M493220)
.

### Track: Set Visibility (Eyeball)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#track-set-visibility-eyeball "Direct link to heading")

This example switches the eyeball icon on/off, which controls the visibility of a video track:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_track_eyeball.png)

    var track = seq.videoTracks[videoTrackIndex-1];track.setMute(eyeballState?0:1)

Note that the function `setMute()` can both mute audio tracks and set the eyeball of video tracks.

### Track: Mute[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#track-mute "Direct link to heading")

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_track_mute.png)

    var track = seq.audioTracks[audioTrackIndex-1];track.setMute(muteState?1:0)

### Track: Set Locked[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#track-set-locked "Direct link to heading")

You can lock tracks using the method `Track.setLocked()`. You needd to give the funtion `1` as argument to lock it and `0` to unlock it. See also [this forum discussion](https://community.adobe.com/t5/after-effects-discussions/how-to-toggle-track-lock-in-premiere-pro-with-automation-blocks-aescript-plugin/m-p/14275932)
.

In this implementation we pass the sequence and a track index as argument to lock tracks A1 and V1, respectively:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_track_lock.png)

**Lock Audio Track:**

    var track = seq.audioTracks[audioTrackIndex-1];track.setLocked(lockedState?1:0)

**Lock Video Track:**

    var track = seq.videoTracks[videoTrackIndex-1];track.setLocked(lockedState?1:0)

Here is an alternative implementation, which can lock both audio and video tracks and which takes the track itself as argument (learn [here](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#accessSequenceClip)
 how to specify a track using a path string):

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_track_lock_track_var.png)

    track.setLocked(lockedState?1:0)

### Clip: Remove[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#clip-remove "Direct link to heading")

In Premiere Pro's scripting API, clips in a sequence are represented by objects of type TrackItem. The function `TrackItem.remove()` removes the clip from a sequence and takes two arguments: `inRipple` and `inAlignToVideo`. See [here](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/item/trackitem.html#trackitem-remove)
 for a description of these arguments.

In this example, we remove the first clip in track V1 of the active sequence. If you wonder what the `::V1\1` exactly means and what other options you have to specify a TrackItem, see [here](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#accessSequenceClip)
.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_clip_remove.png)

    item.remove(inRipple,inAlignToVideo)

### Clip: Apply Effect[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#clip-apply-effect "Direct link to heading")

The official scripting API of Premiere Pro contains no function to apply effects. But there is also the undocumented QEdom, which you can actually use to apply effects. Be aware that this is unsave territory officially not supported by Adobe, but it still might work. This example applies the Gaussian Blur effect to the first clip in track V1 of the active sequence:

![apply effects to clips in Premiere Pro using Automation Blocks](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_apply_effect.png)

    app.enableQE();var qeEffect = qe.project.getVideoEffectByName(effectName);var qeSeq = qe.project.getActiveSequence();var qeTrack = qeSeq.getVideoTrackAt(videoTrackIndex-1);var qeClip = qeTrack.getItemAt(trackItemIndex-1);qeClip.addVideoEffect(qeEffect);

Cudos to NT Productions for [figuring this out](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mdB58gEBQaA)
; see also [this forum discussion](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro-discussions/in-premiere-pro-can-an-effect-mosaic-be-applied-to-an-adjustment-layer-using-automation-blocks/m-p/14478002#M493399)
.

##### trackItemIndex

`qeTrack.getItemAt()` counts the gaps between clips in a track also as "items". Hence, you need to count both the clips and the gaps between them to find the right value for trackItemIndex. See also [this forum discussion](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro-discussions/in-premiere-pro-can-an-effect-mosaic-be-applied-to-an-adjustment-layer-using-automation-blocks/m-p/14478002#M493399)
 to learn how to find the correct index for an item.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_apply_transition_track_item_index.png)

### Clip: Apply Transition[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#clip-apply-transition "Direct link to heading")

![apply a transition to clips in Premiere Pro using Automation Blocks](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_apply_transition.png)

    app.enableQE();var qeSeq = qe.project.getActiveSequence();var qeTrack = qeSeq.getVideoTrackAt(videoTrackIndex-1);var qeClip = qeTrack.getItemAt(trackItemIndex-1);var insertAtStart = (startOrEnd == "at clip start");qeClip.addTransition(qe.project.getVideoTransitionByName(effectName), insertAtStart, durationTimecode,  "0", position );

Applying transitions works very similar to applying effects (and also has the same stumbling block that gaps between clips affect the `trackItemIndex`).

For transitions you have a few extra parameters:

*   **startOrEnd:** Set this to "at clip start" to insert the transition at the beginning of the clip and to any other value otherwise.
*   **durationTimecode:** The total duration of the transition as a timecode - like "10" for 10 frames or "00:00:00:10". Note that this must be a string, not a number value
*   **position**: A number between 0 and 1 determining the exact position of the transition. A value of 0.5 inserts the transition in the middle of the cut. For a value of 0.2, 20% of the total duration of the transition are on the first clip and 80% on the second clip.

Note that `qeClip.addTransition()` also has an extra argument `"0"`. We have no idea what it is doing - if you do, please let us know!

### Source Monitor: Open Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#source-monitor-open-project-item "Direct link to heading")

With the method [app.sourceMonitor.openProjectItem()](https://ppro-scripting.docsforadobe.dev/general/sourcemonitor.html#sourcemonitor-openprojectitem)
 of Premiere Pro's scripting API, you can open any project item in the source viewer. In this example, we open the image "image.jpeg" in the project bin "footage" in the source monitor:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_source_monitor_open_item.png)

    app.sourceMonitor.openProjectItem(myItem)

Here is another variant, which optionally closes all other open clips in the source monitor before opeing the new one:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_execute_code_example_source_monitor_open_item_close_option.png)

    if(closeOthers) app.sourceMonitor.closeAllClips();app.sourceMonitor.openProjectItem(myItem)

*   [Execute Code](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#pr_execute_code)
    *   [Tutorial](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#tutorial)
        
    *   [Variables](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prOther/#variables)

# Rendering Tools | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/renderingTools/#)

On this page

Rendering Tools
===============

Render Clips Of Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/renderingTools/#render_clips_of_sequence "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This tool is useful, if you need to export each single clip of your sequence separately.

![Render Clips of Sequence Separately](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/renderingTools/renderClipsOfSequence.png)

The tool loops over each clip of the active seuqence and adds each of them to the render queue with the in- and out-point set exactly to the range used in the sequence. Note that the tool adds the clips themselves to the render queue, not the respective section of the sequence. This means you obtain a clean render of each individual clip without overlapping content from other tracks and without any effects you may have applied in the sequence.

### Output Folder[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/renderingTools/#output-folder "Direct link to heading")

In the Inputs panel of Automation Blocks the tool shows an input named _"output folder"_ where you can choose where the rendered files should go. Of course, you can change the output folder also in Adobe Media Encoder, before you start rendering.

### Custom Media Encoder Preset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/renderingTools/#custom-media-encoder-preset "Direct link to heading")

After the clips have been added to Adobe Media Encoder, you can select them all in the render queue and customize the render preset. However, if you tend to always use the same render preset, you can also customize the default render preset in the block code.

![Choose the default export preset in the block code](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/renderingTools/renderClipsOfSequenceRenderPreset.png)

### Custom Naming[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/renderingTools/#custom-naming "Direct link to heading")

By default, the name of the rendered clips is composed out of

*   the name of the sequence
*   followed by the number of the clip (simply counting all clips of the sequence starting from one)
*   followed by the name of the clip (as shown in the project panel)

If your sequence is named _"my project"_, for example, and the second clip used in the sequence is named _"ASC102H2"_, then it will be added to the render queue with the name _"my project\_clip\_2\_ASC102H2"_. The clip numbers ensure that even if a clip is used multiple times in the sequence each of them obtains a unique filename.

The file names are created near the very end of the block code. If you want to change the naming, you can customize this part of the code:

![Choose the default export preset in the block code](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/renderingTools/renderClipsOfSequenceCustomNames.png)

The purple "replace slashes in names" blocks ensure that slashes are removed from the names of the sequence and the project items, since slashes are not allowed in filenames.

Render Frames at Markers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/renderingTools/#render_frames_at_markers "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Streamline your still image rendering process in Adobe Premiere Pro! Say goodbye to manually exporting each frame and hello to an efficient workflow that saves you valuable time. Learn how to set sequence markers at desired frames, and then utilize Automation Blocks to automatically render images at all those markers.

In the tutorial we also explain how to customize the block code of the tool to add more fancy naming options.

Render Frames at Equal Distances[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/renderingTools/#render_frames_at_equal_distances "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Say, you need to export one image of your sequence every 10 frames, then this tool is for you. Just choose in the inputs panel how many frames should be between the exported frames as well as the format (jpg, png or tiff) and run the tool:

![Render One Image every 10 frames of your sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/renderingTools/renderFramesAtEqualDistancesUI.png)

Based on [this forum discussion](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro-discussions/automation-blocks-export-batch-stills-feature-and-custom-filenaming/m-p/14316798#M485747)
 I recorded the following video showing how to customize the tool:

In the video we implement the following changes:

*   the name of the sequence is used for the naming of the output files
*   instead of the active sequence all sequences selected in the project panel are processed
*   extra parameters are added to the UI to specify a start and end timecode for the range that should be processed

### Custom Naming[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/renderingTools/#custom-naming-1 "Direct link to heading")

As _naming pattern_ for the file names of the exported images you can enter a text which may contain the following placefolders:

*   _$1_ will be replaced by the number of the image (like a basic image counter starting with 1)
*   _$2_ is the timecode of the image in the timecode format currently chosen for the sequence

# Marker Tools | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/markerTools/#)

On this page

Marker Tools
============

Create Marker With Details[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/markerTools/#create-marker-with-details "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Premiere Pro create marker with custom options](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/markerTools/markerToolCreateAtCurrentTime.png)

You can insert a marker in Premiere Pro quickly using the "M" key. But what if you need to set a lot of markers with specific settings like the marker color, duration, comment etc? With the Automation Blocks tool _"Create Marker at Current Time"_ you can create markers with custom options very quickly.

Colorize Markers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/markerTools/#colorize-markers "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Batch-replace the colors of markers in your timeline quickly! By automating the color replacement, you can easily organize and navigate through crucial points in your footage, saving valuable time and effort while creating a visually appealing and cohesive final product.

#### Cycle Marker Colors[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/markerTools/#cycle-marker-colors "Direct link to heading")

![Premiere Pro cycle marker colors](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/markerTools/markerToolCycleColors.png)

#### Highlight Every 4th Marker[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/markerTools/#highlight-every-4th-marker "Direct link to heading")

![Premiere Pro highlight every 4th marker](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/markerTools/markerToolhighlightEvery4th.png)

Copy Markers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/markerTools/#copy-markers "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Premiere Pro copy markers from clip to sequence and vice versa](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/markerTools/markerToolCopy.png)

Automation Blocks also comes with tools which allow you to seamlessly copy markers between a sequence and the clips within that sequence.

Create Beat Markers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/markerTools/#create-beat-markers "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Creating beat markers in the Premiere Pro timeline can significantly enhance the editing process, allowing precise synchronization of audio and video elements with the rhythm of the music. Automation Blocks offers useful tools to assist you in generating beat markers effortlessly, enabling a more dynamic and engaging audiovisual experience.

However, for users who frequently require beat markers, the extension [BeatEdit for Premiere Pro](https://aescripts.com/beatedit-for-premiere-pro)
 is recommended as the optimal solution. BeatEdit provides even more advanced and specialized functionalities, making the process of adding beat markers smoother and more versatile. So, while Automation Blocks is a great option for occasional use, BeatEdit offers a more comprehensive and powerful toolset for those who rely on beat markers extensively in their editing projects.

[Get BeatEdit for Premiere Pro](https://aescripts.com/beatedit-for-premiere-pro)

Render Frames at Markers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/markerTools/#render-frames-at-markers "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This tool is documented [here](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/renderingTools#render_frames_at_markers)
.

Markers <-> YouTube Chapters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/markerTools/#markers---youtube-chapters "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Tired of manually adding chapter times and titles to your YouTube video descriptions? Say goodbye to the hassle and errors! With Automation Blocks, you can effortlessly create chapters directly in your Premiere Pro timeline, streamlining your workflow like never before.

Here's how it works:

1.  Simply place a sequence marker at the start of each chapter.
2.  Set the marker names to your chapter titles.
3.  Run the AB tool, "Export Markers to YouTube Chapter Text", and let Automation Blocks handle the rest!

YouTube will seamlessly visualize your chapters within the player, enhancing the viewing experience for your audience.

Automation Blocks also offers a unique tool to import YouTube chapter texts as markers, making it a breeze to edit or remix existing YouTube videos directly in your Premiere Pro sequence.

Markers <-> Spreadsheet[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/markerTools/#markers---spreadsheet "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Streamline your marker management like never before by effortlessly importing and exporting markers to and from a spreadsheet. Seamlessly transfer all marker details, including marker color, marker name, and comments, with just a few clicks.

With the ability to export markers to a spreadsheet, you can easily collaborate with your team, share marker data, and keep track of crucial information. No more manual note-taking or back-and-forth communication - stay organized and efficient throughout your editing process.

But that's not all - the Marker Import/Export tool also empowers you to import marker data from a spreadsheet back into Premiere Pro. Reconstructing your markers has never been easier, ensuring consistency and accuracy across all your projects.

Markers <-> SRT Captions[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/markerTools/#markers---srt-captions "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

With Automation Blocks for Premiere Pro you can effortlessly convert markers into SRT caption files and vice versa.

# One-Click Slideshow | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#)

On this page

One-Click Slideshow
===================

Overview[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#overview "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you ever need to create a slideshow from a bunch of photos, this script is for you! Just run the script, choose a folder with images (and optionally also music) and it creates a slideshow from start to finish.

In more detail, the script does the following:

1.  it imports the entire folder you choose (including subfolders) in your Premiere Pro project
2.  it creates a new sequence
3.  it inserts all images it can find in your folder (or subfolders) into that sequence
4.  it scales the images to fill the frame accuratly and adds a subtle pan and zoom (a.k.a. Ken Burns effect) to bring the images to life. The effect is achieved with position and scale keyframes such that you can easily tweak it later, if you want.
5.  if there is any music in your folder (or subfolders), it inserts as many of those songs as necessary into the sequence to cover the entire duration of the slideshow. The last song is also faded out at the end of the slideshow using volume keyframes.

Usage[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#usage "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1.  Run the script _one click slideshow_ with the Automation Blocks Launcher (or with the Automation Blocks Editor)
2.  In the dialog that pops up, choose a folder that contains the images you want to use for the slideshow. If you want background music, make sure the music file(s) are in that folder, too. Both images and music can also be in subfolders.
3.  Wait until the script is done - this may take about a minute.

Customization Tips[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#customization-tips "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you open the block code of the tool, you see a single block where you can customize all basic settings. If you want to see the full block code, you can right-click on the small block below it and choose _expand_ in the context menu:

![customization overview](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/oneClickSlideshow_overview_new.png)

Here is a description for the things you can customize in the main block.

### Footage Folder[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#footage-folder "Direct link to heading")

By default, the tool shows a popup dialog where you can choose the folder with your images. I you want to hard-code a folder directly, you can delete the red _"select folder dialog"_ block and behind it, a [Folder block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_folder)
 will show up, where you can choose the folder you want to use.

### Scale Factor[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#scale-factor "Direct link to heading")

The scale factor controls the zoom at the beginning of each image. It is set to 120 by default, which means that each image starts with a zoom of 120% and ends with 100% (where 100% means that the image exactly fills the frame). If you set the parameter to 100, the images do not zoom at all, since then both start and end are at 100%.

### Preset Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#preset-sequence "Direct link to heading")

This option controls the details of the sequence, like its resolution and frame rate. The dropdown contains Premiere Pro's default presets, which are installed on most systems. If you want to use your own custom preset, you can simply delete the yellow block with the sequence preset dropdown. Behind it, a [File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_file)
 will show up, where you can choose your custom sequence preset (\*.sqpreset) file.

### Preset Export[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#preset-export "Direct link to heading")

If you choose the option to add the slideshow to the render queue, you can choose here the export preset for that. The dropdown contains the most commonly used export presets. If you want to use a custom preset, just delete the green block with the dropdown and behind it a [File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_file)
 will show up, where you can choose your custom export preset (\*.epr) file. Learn [here](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering#format-preset)
 how to create your own epr files.

### Sequence Name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#sequence-name "Direct link to heading")

Here you can choose the name for the sequence for your slideshow.

### Render Option[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#render-option "Direct link to heading")

In this dropdown you have three (pretty self-explanatory) choices:

*   do nothing
*   add to render queue
*   add to render queue and start rendering

### Image Duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#image-duration "Direct link to heading")

To change how long each image is visible, you don't even need to modify the script. Just go to the Premiere Pro Preferences and adjust the `Still Image Default Duration` in the _Timeline_ section to your liking.

![customization overview](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/still_image_default_duration.png)

Bug in Premiere Pro 24.2[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#bug-in-premiere-pro-242 "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

##### Bug in PR2024 - User Canceled Error

In Premiere Pro 2024 versions 24.2 and probably also 24.3 there is a [bug which causes the select dialog to fail](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro-discussions/open-folder-dialog-doesn-t-works-in-pr-2024/m-p/14503742#M494898)
. If your Pr version still has this bug, the slideshow tool will show a "user cancelled" error whenever you try to run the tool.

Workarounds:

1.  use a newer Pr version (the current beta or any release newer than Pr 24.3 already contains the fix)
2.  replace the [select folder dialog block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#block_extendscript_open_folder_dlg)
     by a [folder block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_folder)
     and enter the folder containing your slideshow images directly in that block.

![customization overview](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/oneClickSlideshow_PR2024_bug.png)

*   [Overview](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#overview)

    *   [Footage Folder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/oneClickSlideshow/#footage-folder)

# Sequence Tools | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/sequenceTools/#)

On this page

Sequence Tools
==============

Deep Duplicate Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/sequenceTools/#duplicate_active_sequence_including_nested_sequences "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Usage[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/sequenceTools/#usage "Direct link to heading")

When you duplicate a sequence in Premiere Pro, which contains nested sequences, those nested sequences are not duplicated, which can cause a lot of confusion and frustration. With the Automation Blocks tool **Duplicate Active Sequence Including Nested Sequences** you create a duplicate of the active sequence and all nested sequences easily. Just choose a name for the duplicate and a bin where it should be placed and run the tool:

![Premiere Pro tool to duplicate the active sequence including nested sequences](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/sequenceTools/duplicateActiveSequenceToolUI.png)

**Extra tip**: In the parameter "name of duplicate" you can use the placeholder $1 for the name of the active sequence.

### Customizing the Block Code[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/sequenceTools/#customizing-the-block-code "Direct link to heading")

This tool effectively just wraps a user interface around the [Clone Sequence Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#block_pr_sequence_clone)
. The first four blocks create the user interface. Here you can easily adjust the default values for the different inputs, for example.

![customizing the block code](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/sequenceTools/duplicateActiveSequenceToolBlocks.png)

Set In Out Range[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/sequenceTools/#sequence_set_in_out_range "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Automation Blocks contains two tools to sett the In-Out-Range of the active sequence:

*   **Set In Out to Selected Clips**: Select some clips in the active sequence and run the tool to set the in out range exactly to those clips.
*   **Set In Out to Full Duration**: Sets the in out range of the active sequence to the full duration of the sequence (i.e. it starts at the beginning of the sequence and ends at the end of the last clip).

![Premiere Pro tool to duplicate the active sequence including nested sequences](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/sequenceTools/setInOutRangeToSelectedClips.png)

The two tools have no user interface - just run them and you are done.

*   [Deep Duplicate Sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/sequenceTools/#duplicate_active_sequence_including_nested_sequences)
    *   [Usage](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/sequenceTools/#usage)
        
    *   [Customizing the Block Code](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/sequenceTools/#customizing-the-block-code)

# Expressions | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions/#)

On this page

Expressions
===========

Automation Blocks contains various tools to work with expressions more effectively.

Apply Expressions[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions/#ApplyExpressions "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Applying an expression to multiple properties can be tedious - you always have to first write the expression on one layer, then copy & paste it to the others. Instead, with Automation Blocks you can simply launch the tool **Apply Expression to All Selected Props**, enter the expression in the dialog, and it will be applied to all selected properties instantly.

![apply After Effects expression to all selected properties](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/ApplyExpressionToAllSelectedPropsUI.png)

##### editing expressions

The dialog always remembers the last expression you entered. So, if you want to change the expression that you just applied, simply run the tool again and the dialog will still contain all the code you entered, so you can edit it easily.

Sync Expressions[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions/#SyncExpressions "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Do you sometimes apply expressions to a layer and then duplicate the layer many times? Great, but what if you want to change the expression later?

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/ExpressionSyncToDuplicates.gif)

The **Sync Expressions to Duplicate Layers** tool offers an easy solution for this. Just change the expression(s) on one of the layers, select that one layer, and run the tool. It will find all other layers in the comp which have (almost) the same name, and then copy all expressions from the first selected layer to the others.

I said "almost the same name", since the layers may have a different number at the end, i.e. if you select a layer with name "Red Solid 1", then layers like "Red Solid 5" will also be processed.

Enable/Disable Expressions[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions/#EnableDisableExpressions "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![batch enable/disable expressions in After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/expressionsEnableUI.png)

With the **Enable or Disable Expressions** tool you can enable or disable many expressions at once. In the dialog, you have the option to do this for

*   all expressions in the project
*   all expressions of the active comp
*   all expressions of the active comp and its precomps
*   all expressions on the selected layers of the active comp
*   all expressions on the selected properties of the active comp

Search & Replace in Expressions[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions/#SearchReplaceExppressions "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![search/replace in expressions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/expressionsSearchReplaceUI.png)

With this tool, you can perform a search & replace in

*   all expressions in the project
*   all expressions of the active comp
*   all expressions of the active comp and its precomp
*   all currently selected expressions of the active comp

You have the option to search case sensitive, and an advanced option to use regular expressions.

### Regular Expressions[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions/#regular-expressions "Direct link to heading")

Regular Expressions allow you to search for a pattern instead of a single search term.

If you don't yet know what regular expressions are, I recommend [starting here](https://www.regular-expressions.info/)
.

The pattern must be in Javascript Regular Expression notation and also supports capturing groups. In the replace string you can use the placeholders $0, $1, $2,... where $0 represents the full match, $1 the content of the first capturing group, $2 the content of the second capturing group etc.

#### example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions/#example "Direct link to heading")

*   search: `Arm (\d+)`
*   replace `Leg $1`

This operation would replace `Arm 1` with `Leg 1` and `Arm 2` with `Leg 2`, etc. but would ignore any occurrence of "Arm" that is not followed by a space and digits. The `\d+` is the regular expression for "one or several digits", and the parenteses around it indicate that you want to store this part of the match in a capturing group. In the replace text, the `$1` indicates that the content of the first capturing group should be inserted in that position.

*   [Apply Expressions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions/#ApplyExpressions)
    
*   [Sync Expressions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions/#SyncExpressions)
    
*   [Enable/Disable Expressions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions/#EnableDisableExpressions)
    
*   [Search & Replace in Expressions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions/#SearchReplaceExppressions)
    *   [Regular Expressions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions/#regular-expressions)

# Project Setup Tools | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#)

On this page

Project Setup Tools
===================

New Project From Template[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#new-project-from-template "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

As a video editor, you're all too familiar with the hassle of setting up a new project from scratch every time. Creating folders, copying files, naming sequences – it's a time-consuming process. But what if you could automate all of this, getting everything ready with just a few clicks? Enter Automation Blocks for Premiere Pro.

### Step 1: Setup Your Templates[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#step-1-setup-your-templates "Direct link to heading")

To create new projects, you need to tell Automation Blocks once, what your projects look like. For this you need to create a template folder, which contains all folders and files, which each new projct should contain. In the names of any files and folders, you can use a placeholder, which will be replaced by the actual name of the project. Here is a very basic template folder, with placeholder `_REAL_ESTATE_PROJECT`.

![example project template](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/projectSetupTools/ProjectTemplateRealEstate.png)

You can create multiple templates, if you work on different kinds of projects. Here is another example for a template, this time using the placeholder `_TEMPLATE_NAME_`:

![example project template](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/projectSetupTools/ProjectTemplateWedding.png)

Note that the placeholder can be used in any file or folder names. In this example, we decided that the template should contain a text document for the invoice of the project, for example, and the invoice file contains the placeholder, too.

Our template folder also contain a Premiere Pro project (i.e. a prproj file). Like any other file in the template folder, also the prproj file will be copied for each new project. Hence, this Premiere Pro project should contain all the bins, sequences or also footage items, which you want to have in every new project. Automation Blocks also opens and customizes the Premiere Pro project(s) in the template folder for you. Therefore, also inside the project you can use the placeholder to include the project name into the names of project items:

![also the Premiere Pro project can be customized](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/projectSetupTools/ProjectTemplateProjectPanel.png)

So if you create a project with name "Natalie and James", for example, the bin "_TEMPLATE\_NAME_ getting ready" will become "Natalie and James getting ready".

### Step 2: Customize the New Project Tool[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#step-2-customize-the-new-project-tool "Direct link to heading")

After setting up your template folders (and projects), you need to tell Automation Blocks to actually use those. To do this, open the block code of the tool **New Project From Template Folder** and modify the block code as follows:

*   in the first block choose your template folder
*   in the second block enter the placeholder name you used in your template
*   save the customized tool in your user library

![Customizing the block code to use your templates](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/projectSetupTools/NewProjectFromTemplateFolderBlocks.png)

If you use multiple templates, you can configure one variant of the tool _New Project From Template Folder_ for each of them, such that you have one tool _New Wedding Project_ and one tool _New Real Estate Project_, for example. Alternatively, if you prefer having all variants in a single tool, you can use the tool _New Project From Template Folder Multi_. This tool offers a dropdown to choose any of your templates. The customization of the dropdown is a little bit more complex, please see the video tutorial at the beginning of this section for details.

### Step 3: Create New Projects[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#step-3-create-new-projects "Direct link to heading")

Once you've set everything up, creating new projects is as easy as entering the project name in the inputs panel and running the tool.

![Creating a new project](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/projectSetupTools/NewProjectFromTemplateFolderUI.png)

### Customization: Project Folder Input[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#customization-project-folder-input "Direct link to heading")

This tutorial shows how to customize the Automation Blocks script to include an input for the target folder. Perfect for:

*   Creating multiple projects based on the same template in different locations.
*   Keeping templates and projects in separate locations for better organization.

You find the modified block script in our [Community Library](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#join-the-community)
 at  
`Project Management/Create and Import/New Project from Template Folder with Target Folder Input.xml`  
Here is the direct download link to the file:

[Download Blockscript](https://www.dropbox.com/scl/fi/tn7lbprgm9a9fpmo16hlj/New-Project-from-Template-Folder-with-Target-Folder-Input.xml?rlkey=pvzexnhzitcwmove3igplzi7x&dl=1)

Import Footage and Create Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#import_footage_and_create_sequence "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Automation Blocks tool **Import Footage and Create Sequence** imports an entire folder of footage, including subfolders, and replicates the folder structure in your project panel and inserts it into a new sequence. Just select your folder, set your project bin, name your sequence, and you're ready to roll.

![user interface of Import Footage and Create Sequence Tool](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/projectSetupTools/ImportFootageAndCreateSequence.png)

If you're working with image sequences, don't forget to check the box to include them, since otherwise each image will be imported individually.

To save even more time, you can set default values for the inputs like sequence name and target bins to match your typical requirements. You can customize all default values of the user interface easily in the first block of the tools block code:

![customizing the default UI values](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/projectSetupTools/ImportFootageAndCreateSequenceUIDefaults.png)

*   [New Project From Template](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#new-project-from-template)
    *   [Step 1: Setup Your Templates](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#step-1-setup-your-templates)
        
    *   [Step 2: Customize the New Project Tool](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#step-2-customize-the-new-project-tool)
        
    *   [Step 3: Create New Projects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#step-3-create-new-projects)
        
    *   [Customization: Project Folder Input](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#customization-project-folder-input)
        
*   [Import Footage and Create Sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectSetup/#import_footage_and_create_sequence)

# Clip & Animation Tools | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#)

On this page

Clip & Animation Tools
======================

Automation Blocks lets you breeze through complex animations in no time, giving you more room to focus on your story and bring your vision to life!

Scale Clips to Fit or Fill Frame Size[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#scale-clips-to-fit-or-fill-frame-size "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

In this tutorial, we'll demystify the difference between Premiere Pro's built-in functions 'Set to Frame Size' and 'Scale to Frame Size', but more imporantly you learn why Automation Blocks provides a superior solution for scaling your footage to match your sequence. Discover how Automation Blocks allows you to scale your clips without clipping the content or alternatively to ensure the entire frame is covered accurately even when the aspect ratios differ.

Animate In and Out[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#animate-in-and-out "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/keyframingTools/inOutPreviews.gif)

Just select some clips in your sequence and run one of the tools in the section "Clips->Keyframes->In and Out" to animate the layers in and out in all kinds of ways.

Bouncing Ball[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#bouncing-ball "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/keyframingTools/bouncingBall.gif)

Creating a dynamic bouncing animation is a snap! Begin by positioning the image or clip you want to animate at the final bounce destination within your project. With the clip selected, simply execute the "Selected Clips - Ball Bounce In" tool.

This tool automates the process by conducting a physics simulation and placing keyframes on the clip's position, resulting in a captivating and natural bouncing animation.

Within the Inputs Panel, you have the flexibility to tailor your animation precisely to your liking by adjusting the following parameters:

*   **Elasticity**: This parameter allows you to control the intensity of the animation's bounce. For instance, setting it to 50% results in the second bounce being half as high as the first one. On the other hand, a value of 90% extends the bounce duration as the second bounce reaches 90% of the first one's height, creating a prolonged bouncing effect.
    
*   **Move to the Side**: This parameter enables you to introduce lateral movement to your animation. By entering a value of 100, the object will glide 100 pixels to the right during its bounce. Conversely, setting it to 0 ensures the object falls vertically without any horizontal displacement.
    

Create a Visual Focus with Scale Bump[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#create-a-visual-focus-with-scale-bump "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Scale Bump tool is a powerful way to direct your viewers' attention, making an object shout, "Hey, look at me!"

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-pr/keyframingTools/scaleBump.gif)

Using this tool is as simple as positioning the current time indicator at the desired scale bump moment, selecting the clip you want to scale, and running the tool.

In the Inputs Panel, you have control over:

*   **Scale Amount**: Define how much the layer should be scaled up (or down, for values less than 100%).
*   **Bump Duration**: Set the length of the animation.
*   **Overshooting**: Toggle this option to create a more elastic and natural feel to the animation, enhancing its visual impact.

Remote Interview Tools[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#remote-interview-tools "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Automation Blocks introduces a robust solution for effortlessly editing remote interviews in Premiere Pro.

When dealing with remote interviews, the desire is often to display two videos side by side, each featuring one speaker. Additionally, animating the clips to highlight the current speaker by enlarging their video adds a professional touch. Thanks to Automation Blocks, accomplishing these transitions becomes a seamless process. With just a few clicks, you can create dynamic and engaging side-by-side videos, automatically resizing and animating the speaker's clip to draw the audience's attention to the current speaker, enhancing the overall viewing experience.

Keyframe Removal[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#keyframe-removal "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

While the keyframe tools empower you to animate multiple clips efficiently, there are times when you need to remove these animations. Automation Blocks offers the following tools for effortless keyframe removal:

*   **Remove All Keys:** This tool erases all keyframes from the selected clips in your timeline. It covers not only keyframes on transform properties but also those on effect properties. Moreover, it provides an option to remove keyframes solely within the sequence's in-out range.
    
*   **Remove All Transform Keys:** Exclusively designed to target keyframes on transform properties, this tool clears all such keyframes from the selected clips. You can further refine the process by choosing specific transform properties to focus on, such as position or scale.
    

These tools make the task of removing keyframes swift and precise, allowing you to maintain full control over your project's animation.

Ken Burns Pan & Zoom[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#ken-burns-pan--zoom "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Elevate your slideshows with the Ken Burns Pan & Zoom tool, designed to infuse dynamic motion into your presentations. With just a single click, you can effortlessly apply Pan & Zoom effects to all selected clips, transforming static images into captivating visual narratives.

Add Playful Motion with Wiggle Animation[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#add-playful-motion-with-wiggle-animation "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The classic "wiggle" animation, widely popular in Adobe After Effects, is now at your fingertips in Premiere Pro, thanks to Automation Blocks! Our Wiggle tool simplifies the process by creating keyframes while taking into account any existing position keyframes on the layer.

In the Input Panel, you have control over the following parameters:

*   **Wiggles per Second:** Adjust this parameter to determine the animation's speed. Setting it to 10, for example, creates 10 keyframes per second, resulting in a quick wiggle effect. A higher value increases the speed.
*   **Amount:** Specify how much the layer wiggles, dictating the maximum distance the layer can move away from its original position. You can customize this for both the x and y axes independently.
*   **In to Out Only:** When enabled, this option restricts the wiggle animation to occur only within the current in-out range of the sequence.

Explore the Wiggle Animation tool to inject playful motion into your projects, adding a touch of creativity and vibrancy to your scenes.

Craft Your Own Keyframing Tools[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#craft-your-own-keyframing-tools "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

With Automation Blocks, you have the incredible ability to design your own keyframing tools for Premiere Pro. Although Automation Blocks already provides you with a wealth of pre-built keyframing tools, it goes a step further by empowering you to create personalized tools tailored to your frequently used animations. This customization feature not only streamlines your animation process but also significantly enhances your workflow efficiency, allowing you to achieve complex animations with just a single click.

*   [Scale Clips to Fit or Fill Frame Size](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#scale-clips-to-fit-or-fill-frame-size)
    
*   [Animate In and Out](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#animate-in-and-out)
    
*   [Bouncing Ball](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#bouncing-ball)
    
*   [Create a Visual Focus with Scale Bump](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#create-a-visual-focus-with-scale-bump)
    
*   [Remote Interview Tools](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#remote-interview-tools)
    
*   [Keyframe Removal](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#keyframe-removal)
    
*   [Ken Burns Pan & Zoom](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#ken-burns-pan--zoom)
    
*   [Add Playful Motion with Wiggle Animation](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#add-playful-motion-with-wiggle-animation)
    
*   [Craft Your Own Keyframing Tools](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/keyframeTools/#craft-your-own-keyframing-tools)
