# Write Your First Custom Script with Automation Blocks | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#)

On this page

Write Your First Custom Script with Automation Blocks
=====================================================

Wow! So you've decided to take your workflows in Premiere Pro to the next level - great to have you on board! As with any tool that gives you superhero powers, Automation Blocks comes with a learning curve. But don't worry, this quick tutorial will teach you all the basics. Along the way, we'll also create a useful script that imports all your footage from a certain folder and inserts it into a new sequence. Time to get your superhero cape out of the closet!

### The User Interface[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#the-user-interface "Direct link to heading")

First, open the Automation Blocks user interface with the menu command `Window->Extensions->Automation Blocks`.

The user interface consists of several panels, which you can rearrange freely:

![comment example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/ui_overview.png)

*   **Toolbar:** Here you can run the currently open script, and load and save scripts. On the far right side of the toolbar, you'll find the "Help" button, which opens the online help.
*   **Script Panel:** This is where you can see and change the currently open block script. By default, it just contains a _comment_ block and an _open web page_ block. These are special blocks which have no influence on the script at all - they are just used for documentation. If you click on the play button of the _open web page_ block, for example, it opens the Automation Blocks documentation. We'll take a look at some more exciting blocks in a second.
*   **Block Library:** Here you'll find all the blocks that are included in Automation Blocks. Since there are quite a few block types, they are grouped into categories. Just click on a category to see all the blocks included in it. To add a block to your script, just drag & drop it from the block library to the script panel.
*   **Script Library:** This is where you'll find all the example scripts that come with Automation Blocks. Once you've started creating your own scripts, they will show up here, too (in the _User Library_ section).
*   **Console:** After you execute a script, the console shows if it was executed successfully, or if it failed (in which case you'll find an error message with more details, too). Scripts can also produce status output to explain what they are currently doing. All this output will show up in the console, too.
*   **Inputs Panel:** This is where the user interface of the currenty open script is shown (if the script has a custom UI created with the [input block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#input_arg)
    ).

Your First Script[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#your-first-script "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Adding a Block[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#adding-a-block "Direct link to heading")

Let's first open the script panel and get rid of its default blocks by clicking the "New" button of the toolbar. Now, in the block library, we open the category _Pr Project_ and drag the _Sync Folder_ block into the script panel (click on the image to play the screen recording):

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/step_1.gif)

This block can import an entire folder on your hard-drive into the currently open Premiere Pro project. If the folder contains subfolders, it will create subbins into the project, too. Even better: before importing anything, the block checks if the project bin already contains any of the files and if so, it won't import those a second time. So this block is like an intern that you can ask "Hey, could you please take a look at this folder and check if all of it is imported properly into that project bin?"

Now I've told you what this block is doing, but in general, how do you figure out exactly what a block does? Just right-click on any block and choose "help" to get an in-depth documentation of what it does.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/step_2.gif)

### Customizinig Block Parameters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#customizinig-block-parameters "Direct link to heading")

Now let's adjust the block to our needs! First we choose the folder that you want to import. Just click on the folder icon at the top right of the block and then choose a folder in the "Open Folder" dialog that pops up. Make sure that the folder actually contains some footage, so we have something we can import. Next, we also need to choose target bin in Premiere Pro's project panel, where the imported footage should be inserted. For this, we just select the desired target bin inside the project panel and then click on the _refresh icon_ icon in the second line of the sync folder block. In our example, we select the bin "day 1" which is located inside the bin "footage". Automation Blocks describes this bin with the path `\footage\day1`. Instead of using the _refresh icon_ you can also enter these paths manually, if you like. This is useful, for example, if you want to use a bin which does not exist in the project, yet.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/step_3.gif)

##### Backslash vs Slash

For the path of a project item (i.e. a bin, sequence, footage etc. inside your project), Automation blocks always uses a backslash `\` whereas for file or folder paths (which represent a location on your hard-drive and not inside your project), you can use both `/` or `\` (the latter is usually used on Windows).

![slash vs backslash](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/slash_vs_backslash.png)

### Run the Script[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#run-the-script "Direct link to heading")

Now it's time to run your script! Just click the _Run Script_ button and see what happens! Automation Blocks has two buttons for this: The "Run Script" button at the top left of the toolbar and the "Run" button in the library panel. Both buttons do exactly the same, so it does not matter, which one you use.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/step_4.gif)

As you can see, the script imports all footage into the desired target bin successfully. Note the green "DONE" message next to the "Run" button, which indicates that the script execution was successful.

### The Console[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#the-console "Direct link to heading")

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/step_5.gif)

The console panel of Automation Blocks shows some additional information about the script execution, which is created by the blocks while they do their job. What exactly you see there depends on the block - our _sync folder_ block, for example, prints a `check file` message whenever it checks if a particular file has been imported before. In case it found new files which need to be imported, it also prints an `import file` message for each of those files. For files which could not be imported, it also shows a `skip file` message. Some other blocks don't write anything to the console, or only if they run into any issues.

These detailed outputs can be a lifesaver for troubleshooting, since they give you a good overview of what exactly a block is doing - it's like a logbook of the script execution. But as long as your script does what you want, you can ignore them.

### Errors[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#errors "Direct link to heading")

Now, let's provoke an error: we'll replace the folder path of the block with another text saying `whatever`. Assuming that you don't have a folder with that name in the root folder of your file system, the block should have trouble importing footage from that location. And as you can see, the status message now shows a red text "ERROR" with a helpful description:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/step_6.gif)

### Saving Your Script[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#saving-your-script "Direct link to heading")

Great work - you've just written your very first script! So let's save it by clicking on the _Save_ button of the toolbar. After you've saved the script, it also shows up in the script library panel:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/step_7.gif)

Note that scripts are just simple, self-contained xml files. If you want to restructure your library, or delete or rename scripts, just open the library folder with Windows Explorer or Mac OS Finder and move/delete/rename the files to your liking. After you did that, make sure to restart Automation Blocks, such that it notices your changes.

##### User Library Location

By default, you'll find your user library in a folder `Automation Blocks Pr Library` inside your documents folder. If you prefer to keep your scripts in some other location, you can change that in the Automation Blocks preferences. In the preferences, you'll also find a button to open the library folder in Windows Exploder or Mac OS Finder.

![preferences](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/open_preferences.png)

### Adding Clips to a New Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#adding-clips-to-a-new-sequence "Direct link to heading")

Ok, the sync block is amazing, but if a single block alone can be so useful, imagine what you can do if you combine two blocks! So far, our example script just imports some footage. Wouldn't it be great if it could also put those clips into a new sequence?

Since we want to create a new sequence, let's take a look at what the block category "Pr Sequence" has to offer (you'll find an [overview of all block categories](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/overview)
 in the block reference). If you browse through the blocks in that category, three of them seem to be related to creating new sequences and adding clips to them:

![blocks for creating sequences](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/sequence_creation_related_blocks.png)

The first two are both about creating a new sequence, so which one are we going to use? To understand exactly what these blocks do, drag each of them into your script panel, right click on them and choose "help" to see their documentation. There you'll find the info that you can simply give the "_new sequence from clips_" block a bin and it will take all the footage items in that bin and insert them into a new sequence. Cool - exactly what we want!

The other block, "_new sequence from preset file_", is useful if you need more control over the format of the sequence. Say you have HD footage, but want to insert it into a 4K comp. You could first create a 4K comp with the "_new sequence from preset file_" block and then insert your HD footage items using the "_add project item(s) to track of sequence_" block. Bottom line: sometimes you can achieve the same thing in different ways - and often one way is simpler, but the other way gives you more control or flexibility.

So let's go for the "_new sequence from clips_" block and add it to our script. We drag it into our awesome importer script and make sure it uses the same project bin as our sync folder block:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/step_8.gif)

### Adding a User Interface[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#adding-a-user-interface "Direct link to heading")

Now our script is already pretty useful - whenever you start a new project, it imports your footage and creates a sequence in no time! But the folder from which you want to import the footage is probably different for each project you create, right? So wouldn't it be cool if the script asked you for the folder you want to import?

In the block category "_User Interface & Comments_", you'll find the [Select Folder Dialog block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#block_extendscript_open_folder_dlg)
. Just drag it into your script and replace the hard-coded folder block with it. Now, instead of using the same folder each time you run the script, a dialog pops up and asks you for the folder you want to use.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/step_9.gif)

Thanks to the _Open Folder Dialog_, you don't need to modify the script itself anymore whenever you want to choose a new folder to import. This might seem like a minor difference - whether you enter a new file path right inside the block script, or use this kind of dialog instead - but imagine if you want to share your blocks with your colleagues. You won't need to explain to them anymore where exactly in your block code the folder path needs to be adjusted - the dialog just pops up whenever they run the script, so they won't have to touch your code at all.

### Inputs Panel[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#inputs-panel "Direct link to heading")

Instead of showing a popup dialog where the user can choose a folder, you can also add a user interface element for this to the inputs panel. If you want to add something to the inputs panel, you always need to use the [Input block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#input_arg)
 for this. Just replace the _Select Folder Dialog block_ by the _Input_ block and choose a type of the input in the block parameters (in our case the type is "choose folder").

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/step_10.gif)

Summary[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#summary "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Congratulations - you just finished your first lesson on your path to becoming a true automation hero! By now, you've developed a good intuition for where to find the right block for your task, and gained some hands-on experience by writing your first script.

*   [The User Interface](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#the-user-interface)
    
*   [Your First Script](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/firstSteps/#your-first-script)

# Template Tools | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/templateTools/#)

Template Tools
==============

Easy Text Replacement with a Spreadsheet[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/templateTools/#easy-text-replacement-with-a-spreadsheet "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Unlock the power of seamless text updates in your After Effects Motion Graphics Templates within Premiere Pro! Our streamlined workflow makes it a breeze. Simply craft a spreadsheet, noting down the old and their corresponding new texts, and watch as Automation Blocks effortlessly transforms them all with just a single click. You've got this!

Update Clips, Text, Colors and more with a Spreadsheet[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/templateTools/#update-clips-text-colors-and-more-with-a-spreadsheet "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Batch-updates of texts are fantastic, but what if you also need to swap out clips, adjust colors, reposition layers, or tweak other properties? Look no further – this workflow has got you covered!

### Batch-Create Projects[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/templateTools/#batch-create-projects "Direct link to heading")

In [this forum discussion](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro-discussions/automation-blocks-multi-import-and-render-duplicate-sequences-instead-of-send-to-ame/td-p/14543513)
 SAMCS95 developed with the community a variant of this tool which saves for each variant in the spreadsheet a new copy of the project instead of adding it directly to the render queue.

Custom Template Workflows[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/templateTools/#custom-template-workflows "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The ready-to-use template tools provided by Automation Blocks are not only user-friendly but also highly adaptable. However, there might be occasions when you require an even more tailored solution. For instance, if you possess a spreadsheet with a very specific format or if you wish to craft a dedicated custom tool for a particular template, we've got you covered. Allow us to guide you through the process of creating your very own custom template tool from scratch with our comprehensive tutorial:

[Watch Tutorial](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/mogrtFromSpreadsheet)

# Guides | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#)

On this page

Guides
======

Automation Blocks contains an entire collection of scripts to simplify working with guides.

Create[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#guidesCreate "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You can use this script to create guides in the active composition.

Just execute the script **Guides - Create** and the following dialog will appear:

![After Effects Script: Guides - Create - Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/guideCreateDialog.png)

Choose the orientation of the guides, and define where they should be created.

The values can be in pixels (px) and percentages (%). If no unit is defined, the default is pixels. To create multiple guides at once, you can enter multiple values separated by a comma.

### Examples of valid values[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#examples-of-valid-values "Direct link to heading")

*   100, 200 (create guides at 100px and 200px)
*   10px (create guides at 10px)
*   10%, 90% (create guides at 10% and 90% of the comp size)
*   10, 20px, 30%, -40, -50px, -60% (you can mix percentage and px values)

### Mirror Option[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#mirror-option "Direct link to heading")

If you enable the mirror option, the tool creates a symmetrical set of guides. If you enter a position of 100px, for example, it will create guides both 100px away from the left (and/or top) and the right (and/or bottom) edge of the comp.

Create Grid[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#guidesCreateGrid "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![After Effects Script: Guides - Create Grid](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/guidesCreateGrid.png)

This script creates a grid of guides in the active composition. You can specify the width and height of the cells, as well as an optional gutter (set gutter to 0 to disable it). If you enable the center option, the grid will be aligned to the center of the composition. Otherwise, the grid is aligned to the top left corner of the composition.

Note that the script does not delete existing guides (but you can do this with the Ae menu View->Clear Guides before running the script).

Create Around Selected Layers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#guidesAroundSelectedLayers "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![After Effects Script: Guides - Create Around Selected Layers](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/guidesCreateAroundSelectedLayers.png)

This script creates guides along the bounding box of the selected layers. If either creates guides around each of the selected layers individually, or calculates a single bounding box that includes all the selected layers and then creates guides for this one.

The script can create guides at the top/bottom and right/left edges of the bounding box, and optionally also at the center. With the margin option, you can specify that the box formed by the guides is a little bigger than the actual bounding box of the layer(s).

Save[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#guidesSave "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You can use this script to save the guides in the active composition as a preset.

Just execute the following script:

*   **Guides - Save**

When you execute the script, a dialog pops up where you can choose the preset name, the orientation of the guides, and define which unit they should be saved as.

![After Effects Script: Guides - Save - Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/guideSaveDialog.png)

##### Units

A guide saved at 100px will always be in the same position, independent of the composition dimensions, but a guide at 10% will be dynamically positioned in relation to the composition dimensions.

##### Customize Preset Folder

By default, the presets are stored in the folder `~/Documents/Automation Blocks/Guides Library/`. If you prefer a different location, you can customize it easily in the block code:

![choose custom folder for guides presets](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/guidesSaveCustomizeFolder.png)

If you do this, make sure to change the folder in both the scripts "Guides - Load" and "Guides - Save".

Load[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#guidesLoad "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You can use this script to load a guides preset into the active composition.

Just execute the following script:

*   **Guides - Load**

When you execute the script, a dialog pops up where you can select a preset and the orientation of the guides you want to load.

![After Effects Script: Guides - Load - Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/guideLoadDialog.png)

Mirror[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#guidesMirror "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use this script to mirror guides from the active composition. This is useful to create symmetrical guide setups. If you mirror a guide that is 100px from the left edge of the comp, for example, the result will be a guide that is 100px away from the right edge.

![After Effects Script: Mirror Guides Example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/guideMirrorExample.png)

Just execute the script **Guides - Mirror** to open this dialog:

![After Effects Script: Guides - Mirror - Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/guideMirrorDialog.png)

In the dialog you can select which guides you want to mirror. You can mirror

*   all horizontal guides,
*   all vertical guides,
*   all vertical and horizontal guides, or
*   choose one specific guide.

*   [Create](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#guidesCreate)
    *   [Examples of valid values](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#examples-of-valid-values)
        
    *   [Mirror Option](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#mirror-option)
        
*   [Create Grid](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#guidesCreateGrid)
    
*   [Create Around Selected Layers](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#guidesAroundSelectedLayers)
    
*   [Save](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#guidesSave)
    
*   [Load](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#guidesLoad)
    
*   [Mirror](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides/#guidesMirror)

# Project Item Tools | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#)

On this page

Project Item Tools
==================

Batch-Rename Project Items[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#batch-rename-project-items "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Explore the capabilities of Automation Blocks to efficiently batch-rename your project items with a range of powerful tools, including:

1.  **Search & Replace**: Effortlessly find and replace text within project item names, ensuring quick and precise adjustments.
    
2.  **Add Text**: Seamlessly append or prepend custom text to the beginning or end of project item names, streamlining your organization.
    

Our Search & Replace tool goes beyond the ordinary, offering support for regular expressions, allowing you to craft intricate replacement patterns. For added convenience, you can harness ChatGPT to assist you in creating these patterns effortlessly!

Label Colors[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#labelColors "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Organize Your Projects with Label Colors[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#organize-your-projects-with-label-colors "Direct link to heading")

In Premiere Pro, label colors are invaluable for maintaining organization, provided your project items are consistently labeled. Our tool, "Change Label Color by Item Type", simplifies this process by allowing you to label all project items according to your personal preferences with just a single click. ChangeLabelColorByType

### Identify Used Clips with Labels[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#identify-used-clips-with-labels "Direct link to heading")

Imagine having the ability to instantly identify which clips in your project panel have already been used in your timeline. With the "Change Label Colors of Used Clips" tool, you can effortlessly assign dedicated label colors to used and unused clips, streamlining your editing process.

### Custom Label Color Tools[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#custom-label-color-tools "Direct link to heading")

We also have a tutorial explaining how to create your own custom tools to set label colors exactly according to your personal preferences.

[Watch Tutorial - Custom Label Color Tools](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/customLabelColorScripts)

Moving Items[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#moving-items "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Move Used Clips in Sub-bin[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#move-used-clips-in-sub-bin "Direct link to heading")

Maintaining a well-organized project is paramount when working in Premiere Pro, and Automation Blocks provides a convenient solution. The tool "Move Used Clips to Sub-bin" is designed to automatically relocate all clips that have already been used in the timeline into a dedicated sub-bin. This intuitive feature grants you instant visibility into which clips have been utilized. Should your clip usage evolve over time, simply run the tool again, and it will update everything as needed.

### Organize Clips by Rating in Bins[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#organize-clips-by-rating-in-bins "Direct link to heading")

In Premiere Pro, you have the ability to assign ratings to clips on a scale of zero to five stars. To make the most of this feature, Automation Blocks offers a tool to automatically sort clips into sub-bins based on their ratings. This means that you can easily group all your five-star clips into a single bin, simplifying your project organization.

### Organize Project Items into Bins by Type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#organize-project-items-into-bins-by-type "Direct link to heading")

Project cleanliness is essential, and Automation Blocks offers a powerful tool for effortlessly categorizing all project items into bins based on their type. With just one click, you can establish a structured project layout, complete with separate bins for audio files, video files, sequences, nested sequences, and more.

What's more, you have the flexibility to customize this folder structure to match your precise project organization preferences, ensuring that your project structure aligns perfectly with your needs.

Deleting Items[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#deleting-items "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Remove Unused Footage[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#remove-unused-footage "Direct link to heading")

Deleting unused footage items from your Premiere Pro project is a breeze with the built-in menu command, "Edit->Remove Unused." However, Automation Blocks takes it a step further with the "Delete Unused Footage Items" tool, offering additional advantages:

*   **Selective Processing**: This tool allows you to target specific items, enabling you to remove only the unused clips from a particular bin, for instance.
    
*   **Detailed Console Output**: Gain complete visibility into the removal process with a comprehensive console output that precisely outlines which clips have been deleted.
    

As a bonus, our tutorial provides insights into customizing the tool's block code, so you can create a list of unused footage without actually removing it, tailoring the tool to your unique requirements.

### Remove Empty Bins[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools/#remove-empty-bins "Direct link to heading")

When you execute the menu command "Edit->Remove Unused" in Premiere Pro, the application diligently removes everything that isn't used in your timeline—almost everything. It conveniently leaves behind empty bins, which may clutter your project.

Fortunately, Automation Blocks introduces the "Delete Empty Project Bins" tool to address this oversight. This smart tool goes a step further by eliminating not only empty bins but also bins that solely contain other empty bins, ensuring your project remains clutter-free and well-organized.

# Synced Folders | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#)

On this page

Synced Folders
==============

Automation Blocks contains some tools to synchronize system directories with project bins, which you'll find in the section _Project->Footage->Synced Folders_ of the library. The basic workflow is:

1.  Import entire folders into the After Effects project with the tool **Synced Folders - Import**.
2.  After you've added new assets to a synced folder, just run the tool **Synced Folders - Update**. It will detect all changes in any of the synced folders, import all new assets, and also delete project items of assets that have been deleted.

Overview[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#overview "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Import[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#Import "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use this script to create an After Effects folder with the same name as the system folder you've selected and synchronize their contents.

##### Folder Location

If you selected an After Effects folder before running the script, the new folder will be created inside that folder. Otherwise, it is created in the root of your project.

![After Effects Script: Synced Folders - Import](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/syncedFoldersImportDialog.png)

### Import Options[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#import-options "Direct link to heading")

#### Select folder[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#select-folder "Direct link to heading")

Select a system folder to import and sync with.

#### Subfolders[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#subfolders "Direct link to heading")

Lets you choose what to do with the subfolders within the selected folder:

**include**: Includes the subfolders' contents, creating new subfolders in After Effects to match the system folder structure.

**exclude**: Excludes the subfolders' contents.

**flatten**: Includes the subfolders' contents but does not create any new subfolders in After Effects.

#### Import multi-layers files as[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#import-multi-layers-files-as "Direct link to heading")

Lets you choose how to import multi-layer files (as footage, composition or composition with cropped layers).

#### Import numbered still images as sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#import-numbered-still-images-as-sequence "Direct link to heading")

Imports images as image sequences or single items.

#### Delete other items[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#delete-other-items "Direct link to heading")

Deletes items from the After Effects bin if needed to match the system folder.

Update[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#Update "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Run this script after any of the synced folders are changed on disk. The script will detect all changes and update the contents of all synced folders in the project accordingly.

Show List[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#ShowList "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You can use this script to show a list of all synced folders in the project.

Unsync[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#Unsync "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You can use this script to stop syncing folders in the project. It lets you choose to unsync all folders, or only the selected After Effects folders.

![After Effects Script: Synced Folders - Unsync](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/syncedFoldersUnsyncDialog.png)

Change Selected Bin Settings[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#ChangeSelectedBinSettings "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

You can use this script to change the settings of an already synced bin.

![After Effects Script: Synced Folders - Change Selected Bin Settings](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/syncedFoldersChangeSelectedBinSettingsDialog.png)

The options of the dialog are identical to those of the [Import tool](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#Import)
, except for the additional _Sync now_ option:

*   [Overview](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#overview)
    
*   [Import](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#Import)
    *   [Import Options](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#import-options)
        
*   [Update](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#Update)
    
*   [Show List](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#ShowList)
    
*   [Unsync](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#Unsync)
    
*   [Change Selected Bin Settings](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders/#ChangeSelectedBinSettings)

# Rigs & Presets | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#)

On this page

Rigs & Presets
==============

Auto Resizing Text Box[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#AutoResizingTextBox "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you run the **Auto Resizing Text Box Rig** tool, it will create a box around each currently selected text layer:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/rigAutoResizingTextBox.gif)

The box is created with effects and expressions on the layer itself. It will update its size instantly when the text of the layer is changed. In case you want to delete the box later, just delete the effects on the text layer.

##### Pins & Boxes

If you want to create more complex dynamic layouts - like a box around multiple layers - take a look at our After Effects extension [Pins & Boxes](https://aescripts.com/pins-and-boxes/)

### Create Your Own Preset Rigs[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#create-your-own-preset-rigs "Direct link to heading")

The block code of the _Auto Resizing Text Box Rig_ tool consists of just this single line:

![Apply Preset with Automation Blocks](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/rigAutoResizingTextBoxCode.png)

All this code does is apply an After Effects preset named _"Text Box Rig.ffx"_ to all selected text layers. If you have other After Effects presets and want to create Automation Blocks tools to apply those, just choose a different preset file in the block code and then choose the layers that the preset should be applied to.

![Apply Preset with Automation Blocks](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/rigAutoResizingTextBoxCodeAnnotated.png)

For more details see the [Apply Preset](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeLayer#ae_layer_apply_preset)
 block.

Time-remapping properties[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#TimeRemapProperties "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Repeating or retiming an animation is usually very tedious, especially if the animation includes keyframes on many different properties. The **Time-Remap Selected Properties With a Slider** tool allows you to connect such an animation to a single slider.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/rigTimeRemapProperties.gif)

The slider is like a playback control for the animation: To play the animation, just keyframe the slider from 0 to 100%. The result is very similar to precomposing an animation and time-remapping the precomp, but the big advantage is that you don't need to create a precomp.

### Overview[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#overview "Direct link to heading")

### Clean & Simple[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#clean--simple "Direct link to heading")

The rig created is very lightweight: There are no extra layers, no precomposing etc. The only things that the tool creates are

*   the slider, which you use to control the animation and
*   expressions on the keyframed properties, which should react to the slider.

### Adding More Properties Later[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#adding-more-properties-later "Direct link to heading")

If you've already created a rig and want to add more keyframed properties later, just select those properties and run the tool again. In the dialog which asks for the slider name, make sure to enter the same name that you used before (unless those new properties should be controlled by a different slider).

### Time-Remapping Multiple Animations[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#time-remapping-multiple-animations "Direct link to heading")

The Automation Blocks tool **Time-Remap Selected Properties With Multiple Animations** is very similar to the basic variant of the tool, except that it uses two sliders in order to handle multiple animations. As in the basic variant, the first slider controls the playback of the animation. The second slider allows you to pick which of the animations should be played:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/rigTimeRemapPropertiesMulti.gif)

The workflow with this tool for multiple animations is identical, except that before running it you need to create some markers on the layer, to indicate which keyframes should belong to which animation. Just create one layer marker for each animation and then set the duration of the marker to the duration of the respective animation:

![use markers to indicate which keyframes belong to which animation](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/rigTimeRemapPropertiesMultiMarkers.png)

The following tutorial shows this workflow in detail:

Import File, Apply Preset & Blending Mode[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#importFileApplyPresetBlendingMode "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Do you have some favorite texture or other footage that you use all the time? Now you can do this in a single click:

*   import the footage file into your project if it has not been imported before
*   create a new layer with the file in the active comp
*   set a blending mode of your choice for the layer
*   apply any Ae preset to the layer (to apply effects, expressions and keyframes to it, for example)

Consider this tool as a template for creating your custom tool. The main part of the code consists of a single block, where you can easily choose the texture image, preset and blending mode.

![Create a custom texture tool](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/rigImportFileApplyPresetParameters.png)

To create a custom tool for your favorite texture, just change those values as desired and save the block script in your user library.

### Detailed Tutorial[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#detailed-tutorial "Direct link to heading")

Repeat Animation on Selected Layers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#RepeatAnimationSelectedLayers "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This tool allows you to repeatedly apply keyframes to many layers. By default, the tool applies a step by step move downwards, but you can also use your own custom keyframes. On the left is the result with the default keyframes, and on the right with custom keyframes:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/rigRepeatKeyframesExamples.gif)

If you want to use a custom sequence of keyframes, you need to first load those keyframes into the tool and then apply it to your target layers:

![repeat keyframes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/rigRepeatKeyframesTimeline.png)

The tool then repeatedly pastes the loaded keyframes on all selected layers. However, in contrast to a normal cut & paste operation, the tool adjusts the pasted animation as needed, so it continues seamlessly from the location where the layer currently is. Also, a _probability_ parameter gives the option of skipping the animation from time to time randomly, to create more variation.

### Loading Keyframes and Adjusting Parameters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#loading-keyframes-and-adjusting-parameters "Direct link to heading")

To load keyframes into the tool or adjust any of the parameters, you need to edit the block code:

![customize code of repeat animation tool](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/rigRepeatKeyframesBlockParams.png)

To load custom keyframes, just select the property with those keyframes and then click the refresh button on the two first parameters of the block. The first parameter stores the actual keyframe data, and the second one the property that the keyframes should be applied to (the tool not only supports position animations, but also keyframes on any properties).

### Expression[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#expression "Direct link to heading")

The last parameter of the block code is an expression that is applied on top of the pasted keyframes. By default, this expression ensures that if a layer moves outside the comp, it appears at the other side again. If you don't want this to happen, you can simply delete the expression parameter. If you want to use your own expression, you can right click on the expression parameter and choose _expand_ to edit the code of the expression.

### Detailed Tutorial[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#detailed-tutorial-1 "Direct link to heading")

This tutorial explains in detail how to use and customize the tool. It also includes some advanced tricks, like using keyframes on multiple properties of the layer.

##### Preserving Position of Layers

This tool adjusts the keyframes before pasting them to make sure the animations start at the current position of the layer. However, this is only the case if the properties have at least one keyframe at or before the start of the workarea. So, if your layers don't have any keyframes yet, add a keyframe at the beginning of the workarea before running the tool.

*   [Auto Resizing Text Box](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#AutoResizingTextBox)
    *   [Create Your Own Preset Rigs](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#create-your-own-preset-rigs)

    *   [Overview](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets/#overview)

# Randomize | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#)

On this page

Randomize
=========

Automation Blocks has a collection of tools to randomly distribute layers and quickly randomize the values of all kinds of properties, and even to create random shapes!

Randomize Property Values[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#RandomPropertyValues "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/RandomizeSelectedPropsExampleCrab.gif)

With the **Set Selected Props to Random Values** tools, you can quickly set the values of all selected properties to random values in a customizable range. There are three different variants of the tool - for 1D, 2D and 3D properties. The only difference is that for 1D you can only specify a single min and max value, whereas for 2D and 3D you have a min & max for x and y (or x,y and z) separately.

![Set selected properties to random values](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/RandomizeSelectedPropsUI.png)

If you run the 1D variant, for example, all selected properties which don't have 1D values are ignored.

##### Position and Scale are always 3D

Note that internally in Ae, layer position and scale values are always 3D values, so you have to use the 3D variant of the tool for these values. If the 3D switch for the layer is not enabled, the z component of the layer is simply ignored.

Alternatively, if you want to set the position, you can also use the tool [Distribute Layers Randomly](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#DistributeLayersRandomly)

### Add to Current Value[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#add-to-current-value "Direct link to heading")

If you enable the "Add to current value" option, the existing value of the property is not replaced. Instead, the random value is added to the existing value. This is useful, for example, if you want to keep the existing values and just add a little bit of randomness on top.

### Keyframes[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#keyframes "Direct link to heading")

Note that this tool does not support keyframes. Any keyframed selected properties are ignored.

Distribute Layers Randomly[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#DistributeLayersRandomly "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This tool is very similar to the tool [Randomize Property Values](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#RandomPropertyValues)
, the only difference being that you don't need to select the position property of the layers. The tool will randomize the position properties of all selected layers. Also, for 2D layers you can use the 2D variant of the tool, whereas for the _"Randomize Property Values"_ tool, you'll always need to use the 3D variant for position properties.

Replace Layer Source Randomly[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#ReplaceLayerSourceRandomly "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/RandomizeLayerSourceExample.gif)

In After Effects, you can replace the source of a layer by selecting it and then alt-dragging an item from the project panel onto the layer. But what if you want to replace the source of many layers at once?

The tool **Replace Layer Source Randomly** allows you to replace the source item of many layers at once with random project items. Just do the following:

1.  select the layers whose source should be replaced in the timeline.
2.  select the footage items you want to use as source in the project panel
3.  run the tool

Each of the selected layers now gets one of the selected project items as source.

Create Random Background Shapes[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#RandomBackgroundShapes "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The _Create Random Background Shapes_ tool creates a shape layer for you with random shapes. In the dialog you can customize the number of random shapes, as well as their position, scale, rotation and opacity:

![After Effects script: Create shape layer with random background shapes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/randomBackgroundShapesUI.png)

Instead of specifying a single value for each of position, scale, rotation and opacity, you specify min and max values, and for each shape a random value within that range will be chosen.

### Custom Shapes & Colors[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#custom-shapes--colors "Direct link to heading")

The colors and shapes used by the tool cannot be changed in the dialog, but can easily be customized in the block code of the tool. This allows you to create custom variants of the tool for specific shapes:

![Create Different Random Shapes with Ae Script](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/tools-ae/randomBackgroundShapesExamples.png)

By default, the _Random Background Shapes_ tool creates shapes as shown on the left of this image, and you find the two other variants with the wave shapes and stars in the [Community Library](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/#access-community-library)
.

The following tutorial shows how to customize the colors and shapes yourself:

*   [Randomize Property Values](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#RandomPropertyValues)
    *   [Add to Current Value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#add-to-current-value)
        
    *   [Keyframes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#keyframes)
        
*   [Distribute Layers Randomly](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#DistributeLayersRandomly)
    
*   [Replace Layer Source Randomly](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#ReplaceLayerSourceRandomly)
    
*   [Create Random Background Shapes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#RandomBackgroundShapes)
    *   [Custom Shapes & Colors](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize/#custom-shapes--colors)
