# Pr Project Items | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#)

On this page

Pr Project Items
================

The blocks in this section allow you to do all kinds of things with project items - all the footage, sequences, bins etc that are listed in the project panel (and its sub-bins).

### Accessing a Particular Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#accessProjectItem "Direct link to heading")

Automation Blocks represents project items with a text - like a path indicating where the item can be found. You can specify a particular project item as follows:

*   **Project Item block**: The [project item block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pr_item_path)
     and the [project bin block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pr_itembin_path)
     offer a convenient way to create a text description of any existing project item in one click.
*   **Name:** If the project item has a unique name, just use a text block with that name `my item name`
*   **Path:** You can also give a path to where the item can be found, like `bin\subbin\my item name`. Note that in contrast to file paths on your hard-drive, these paths inside your project structure always use backslashes! If you provide the full path, Automation Blocks does not need to search your entire project, which makes the script faster. Also. you'll avoid getting into trouble if multiple project items have the same name.
*   **Path with project:** If you want to access project items from a project that is not the active project (but is still open in Premiere), you can put the project name at the beginning of the path: `myProject.prproj\bin\subbin\my item name`.
*   **File:** If you want to access a project item that represents a footage file (and not a sequence or bin, for example), you can also provide the file name (or better the entire path) to that file: `myFootage.mov` or `/path/to/myFootage.mov`. When a project item is expected, Automation Blocks will convert the file name or path into a project item (by searching for a project item which represents that file). If the footage is not found in the project, the script stops with an error. Also note that in contrast to project item paths, the file paths may use `/` instead of `\`. And again: if you provide the entire file path instead of just the file name, you'll avoid trouble when you have multiple files with the same name.
*   **Sequence or clip:** if you have a particular sequence or sequence clip (i.e. the occurrence of some footage inside a sequence) and want to use the project item that corresponds to it, Automation Blocks will convert it for you automatically. So in every block that expects a project item as an argument, you can also provide a sequence clip instead and its corresponding project item will be used.

Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pr_item_path "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_item_path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/pr_item_path.png)

This block is used inside many other blocks - wherever you need to specify a particular project item, this block is the default way to do it. Just select an item in the project panel and click the refresh button to use it:

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_item_path_basic_usage.gif)

Since in Automation Blocks project items are represented as path strings, this block is just a convenient way to create such a string. These two blocks do exactly the same, for example:

![project item block and identical result using a text block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_item_path_vs_string.png)

By default, the block references project items in the currently active project. Since you can have multiple open projects in Premiere Pro, the block also offers an option to choose an item in a different open project. Here are two ways to access the project item "clip001.mp4" in the bin "footage" of the project "myProject.prproj":

![accessing project items in other projects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_item_path_vs_string_with_project.png)

Note that the project must be open in Pr, if you want to access its items.

Project Bin[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pr_itembin_path "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_itembin_path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/pr_itembin_path.png)

This block is identical to the [Project Item block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pr_item_path)
, except that it allows only to select project bins and no other project items. It is used inside of other blocks whereever you need to choose a project bin.

For Each Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#block_pr_for_all_items_in_bin "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_for_all_items_in_bin](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/block_pr_for_all_items_in_bin.png)

Iterates over all project items of a project bin (and optionally sub-bins). The blocks you nest inside this block are executed once for each of those project items. During the execution of these blocks, the respective project item itself is saved in the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `my item`.

It comes in two flavors:

*   **bin:** only iterates over the project items which are contained in the bin, but not project items contained in any of its sub-bins.
    
*   **bin and sub-bins:** iterates over all project items which are inside the bin or any of its sub-bins.
    

You can filter the types of project items that you want to iterate using the checkbox options.

By default (empty string or `\` as project bin) this block iterates over all project items in the root bin of the active project. For all the ways to specify a particular bin, see [Accessing a Particular Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
.

#### Selected Items[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#selected-items "Direct link to heading")

The block can either iterate over all project items (in the given bin) or only over the selected ones. Note that in a Premiere Pro project you can have several selections at the same time: In addition to the project panel, you can have open bin panels, each with their own, independent selection:

![project panel vs bin panel](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/project_panel_selection_example.png)

However, Automation Blocks always just considers the selection of the project panel and not the selection of other bin panels. In the example above, `song 1` and `song 2` are selected in the project panel (and hence considered by Automation Blocks), whereas `song 3` is selected in a bin panel (so is ignored by Automation Blocks).

List of Project Items in Bin[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#block_pr_get_items_in_bin "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_get_items_in_bin](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/block_pr_get_items_in_bin.png)

This block creates a list of all project items that are contained in the given project bin (and optionally all sub-bins). It comes in two flavors:

*   **bin:** only adds the project items to the list which are contained in the bin, but not project items contained in any of its sub-bins.
    
*   **bin and sub-bins:** adds all project items to the lists which are inside the bin or any of its sub-bins.
    

You can filter the types of project items that you want to iterate using the checkbox options.

By default (empty string or `\` as project bin) this block puts all project items in the root bin of the active project in the list. For all the ways to specify a particular bin, see [Accessing a Particular Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
.

#### Selected Items[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#selected-items-1 "Direct link to heading")

This block can either return a list of all project items (in the given bin) or only a list of the selected ones. Note that in a Premiere Pro project you can have several selections at the same time and that that Automation Blocks always just considers the selection of the Project panel, and not of other bin panels. For details see the [For Each Project Item block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#block_pr_for_all_items_in_bin)

##### Iterating

If you want to do something with all the items in a project bin, there is no need to create a list. You can use the [For Each Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#block_pr_for_all_items_in_bin)
 instead.

Get Attribute of Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pr_item_get_property "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_item_get_property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/pr_item_get_property.png)

Returns one of the following properties of the given project item. Depending on the type of the project item, it may not have all those values. Each project item has a name, but a project item of type bin has no in point, for example.

### in point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#in-point "Direct link to heading")

The _in point_ of the project item (in seconds). Note that while the [in point of a clip in a sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#in-point)
 describes at what time in the actual source file the clip in the sequence starts, the inPoint of the project item corresponds to the in point you can set in the source monitor. This in point is also used when rendering the item (when being rendered on its own and not as part of a sequence).

### out point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#out-point "Direct link to heading")

The _out point_ of the project item (in seconds). Note that while the [out point of a clip in a sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#out-point)
 describes at what time in the actual source file the clip in the sequence starts, the inPoint of the project item corresponds to the in point you can set in the source monitor. This in point is also used when rendering the item (when being rendered on its own and not as part of a sequence).

### zero point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#zero-point "Direct link to heading")

Usually a footage item starts with the time 0s and then the zero point is also 0. But the beginning of the file can be assigned any other time - and this time is called the zero point. Note that the zero point is only used for displaying the time code in the user interface. Internally, in Premiere Pro (and also in Automation Blocks), for all in points, out points etc. it is assumed that the clip time starts at 0 and the zero point has no influence on them.

### duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#duration "Direct link to heading")

The duration of the footage item in seconds (and the Still Image Default Duration for still images)

### name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#name "Direct link to heading")

The name of the project item as shown in the project panel

### node id[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#node-id "Direct link to heading")

A unique ID assigned to the project item, upon being added to the project. Several project items may have the same name and the same media path, but their node id is always unique.

### team project asset id[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#team-project-asset-id "Direct link to heading")

The Team Projects Asset ID of the project item.

### tree path[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#tree-path "Direct link to heading")

The current project location of the project item, like `\ProjectName.prproj\Media\MXF\filename.mxf`. As mentioned [here](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
, in Automation Blocks a project item is represented by this kind of path text. Therefore, a tree path is a valid project item in Automation Blocks, too.

### parent bin[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#parent-bin "Direct link to heading")

The bin in which the project item is contained. If you want to move the item to a different bin, use the [Move Project Item to Bin block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#block_pr_item_move_to_bin)
.

### (extended) type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#extended-type "Direct link to heading")

The type of the project item. The result is one of the following texts

*   `TYPE_FOOTAGE` : an item representing a video or image file
*   `TYPE_FILE` : an item representing a file that is not a video or image file - like a text file, for example
*   `TYPE_ROOT` : the root bin of the project that contains all other project items
*   `TYPE_BIN` : a bin that is not the root bin
*   `TYPE_MERGED_CLIP` : a merged clip
*   `TYPE_SEQUENCE` : a sequence
*   `TYPE_MULTICAM_SEQUENCE` : a [multi-camera source sequence](https://helpx.adobe.com/premiere-pro/using/create-multi-camera-source-sequence.html)
    

If you just want to check if your project item has one particular type, you can also use the [Project Item Has Type Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#block_pr_item_has_extended_type)
.

### can change media path[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#can-change-media-path "Direct link to heading")

This is `true` when the item is associated with a media file and that media file can be changed. If it is `true` you can change it with the [Change Media of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#block_pr_item_change_media)
.

### media path[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#media-path "Direct link to heading")

The file that belongs to this project item. You can change it with the [Change Media of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#block_pr_item_change_media)

### can proxy[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#can-proxy "Direct link to heading")

This value is `true` or `false` depending on whether this project item supports proxies or not.

### has proxy[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#has-proxy "Direct link to heading")

This value is `true` or `false` depending on whether this project item has a proxy or not.

### proxy path[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#proxy-path "Direct link to heading")

The proxy file for this project item (only if "_has proxy_" is `true`)

### is offline[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#is-offline "Direct link to heading")

This is `true` if the project item is currently offline and otherwise `false`.

### color label[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#color-label "Direct link to heading")

The label color of this project item represented as a text. One of the following values:

*   `Violet`
*   `Iris`
*   `Caribbean`
*   `Lavender`
*   `Cerulean`
*   `Forest`
*   `Rose`
*   `Mango`
*   `Purple`
*   `Blue`
*   `Teal`
*   `Magenta`
*   `Tan`
*   `Green`
*   `Brown`
*   `Yellow`

You can _set this property_ via both

*   [Set Attribute of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#pr_item_set_property)
     which accepts as a value either: any of the color name texts above, or a number where `Violet` corresponds to `0` and `Yellow` corresponds to `15`.
*   [Set Label Color of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#block_pr_item_metadata_color_label_set)
     which offers a convenient dropdown menu to choose the label color.

### alpha usage[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#alpha-usage "Direct link to heading")

The alpha channel usage as specified in the _interpret footage_ dialog. Must be one of the following texts

*   `ALPHACHANNEL_NONE`
*   `ALPHACHANNEL_STRAIGHT`
*   `ALPHACHANNEL_PREMULTIPLIED`
*   `ALPHACHANNEL_IGNORE`

### field type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#field-type "Direct link to heading")

The field type as specified in the _interpret footage_ dialog. Must be one of the following texts

*   `FIELDTYPE_DEFAULT`
*   `FIELDTYPE_PROGRESSIVE`
*   `FIELDTYPE_UPPERFIRST`
*   `FIELDTYPE_LOWERFIRST`

### ignore alpha[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#ignore-alpha "Direct link to heading")

as specified in the _interpret footage_ dialog (`true` or `false`)

### invert alpha[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#invert-alpha "Direct link to heading")

as specified in the _interpret footage_ dialog (`true` or `false`)

### framerate[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#framerate "Direct link to heading")

The framerate as specified in the _interpret footage_ dialog.

### pixel aspect ratio[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pixel-aspect-ratio "Direct link to heading")

The pixel aspect ratio as specified in the _interpret footage_ dialog.

### remove pulldown[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#remove-pulldown "Direct link to heading")

The remove pulldown option as specified in the _interpret footage_ dialog (`true`or `false`).

### vr conform projection type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#vr-conform-projection-type "Direct link to heading")

The projection type, as specified in the _VR properties_ dialog. One of the following texts

*   `VR_CONFORM_PROJECTION_NONE`
*   `VR_CONFORM_PROJECTION_EQUIRECTANGULAR`

### vr layout type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#vr-layout-type "Direct link to heading")

The VR layout type as specified in the _VR properties_ dialog. One of the following texts

*   `VR_LAYOUT_MONOSCOPIC`
*   `VR_LAYOUT_STEREO_OVER_UNDER`
*   `VR_LAYOUT_STEREO_SIDE_BY_SIDE`

### vr horizontal view[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#vr-horizontal-view "Direct link to heading")

The horizontal capture view, as specified in the _VR properties_ dialog.

### vr vertical view[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#vr-vertical-view "Direct link to heading")

The vertical capture view, as specified in the _VR properties_ dialog.

### video usage[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#video-usage "Direct link to heading")

A number, indicating how often the video of this item has been used in a sequence.

### audio usage[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#audio-usage "Direct link to heading")

A number, indicating how often the audio of this item has been used in a sequence.

### has video[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#has-video "Direct link to heading")

Is true `true` if the project item has video (i.e. is a video file or a sequence)

### has audio[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#has-audio "Direct link to heading")

Is true `true` if the project item has audio (i.e. is an audio file, or a video file with audio or a sequence)

### is still image[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#is-still-image "Direct link to heading")

Is `true` if the footage item is a still image (like a single png or jpg, but no image sequence) and `false` for everything else.

### rating[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#rating "Direct link to heading")

A number between 0 and 5 (for a rating of 0 to 5 stars). To see this in the project panel, enable the metadata display for _Basic->Rating_.

### good checkbox[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#good-checkbox "Direct link to heading")

Either `true` or `false` - this corresponds to the _good_ checkbox in the project panel. To see it in the project panel, enable the metadata display for _Premiere Pro Project Metadata->Good_

### hidden[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#hidden "Direct link to heading")

Either `true` or `false` - can be used to hide elements from the project panel. This corresponds to the _hide_ checkbox in the project panel. To see it in the project panel, enable the metadata display for _Premiere Pro Project Metadata->Hide_.

### width[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#width "Direct link to heading")

The width of the footage in pixels.

### height[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#height "Direct link to heading")

The height of the footage in pixels.

### aspect ratio[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#aspect-ratio "Direct link to heading")

The aspect ratio of the footage (assuming square pixels).

### pixel aspect ratio[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pixel-aspect-ratio-1 "Direct link to heading")

The pixel aspect ratio of the footage.

### frame aspect ratio[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#frame-aspect-ratio "Direct link to heading")

The aspect ratio of the footage (taking into account the pixel aspect ratio).

Set Attribute of Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pr_item_set_property "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_item_set_property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/pr_item_set_property.png)

This block sets any property for a project item. For a list of the available properties and their description, see the [Get Attribute of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#pr_item_get_property)
. Note that some of the properties described there are read only and don't occur in the Set Attribute of Project Item block.

Get Metadata of Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pr_item_get_metadata "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_item_get_metadata](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/pr_item_get_metadata.png)

Can retrieve any kind of metadata (both project metadata and xmp metadata) from a project item. The dropdown contains a list of metadata properties that most project items have. But this block is not limited to the properties listed there - it can retrieve **any kind of metadata**, as long as you have an example project item that has the kind of metadata you want to read:

### Reading metadata keys from a sample clip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#reading-metadata-keys-from-a-sample-clip "Direct link to heading")

1.  select a clip in the active sequence (not in the project panel)
2.  click the refresh icon (circular arrow) behind the dropdown in the _Get Metadata of Project Item_ block
3.  now the dropdown list is updated and contains all the metadata keys it could find in the selected clip

This way, you can access any kinds of metadata entries - even properties of a new custom schema that you created yourself.

##### Get Property Block

A few frequently used metadata properties like _video usage_, _audio usage_, _hidden_ or _good_ are also available in the [Get Attribute of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#pr_item_get_property)

Set Metadata of Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pr_item_set_metadata "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_item_set_metadata](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/pr_item_set_metadata.png)

Writes any kind of metadata to a project item. Exactly as in the _Get Metadata of Project Item_ block, in this block you can also use the refresh icon behind the dropdown field to load all available metadata keys from a clip in your sequence. See the description of the [Get Metadata of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#pr_item_get_metadata)
 for details.

Move Project Item to Bin[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#block_pr_item_move_to_bin "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_item_move_to_bin](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/block_pr_item_move_to_bin.png)

Moves the given project item to a project bin. If the bin does not exist, the script stops with an error. So make sure to [create the bin](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#pr_project_item_create_bin)
 before you execute this block, if you want to move the project item to a bin that does not yet exist.

For all the possibilities to specify a project item (or a bin - which is also a project item), see the section [Accessing a Particular Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#accessProjectItem)

Delete Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#block_pr_item_delete "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_item_delete](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/block_pr_item_delete.png)

Deletes the given project item. Note that for footage items, only the item - not the footage file on the hard-drive - is deleted. If you want to delete the footage file too, use the [Delete File Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_delete_file)
 for that.

For all possibilities to specify a project item, see the section [Accessing a Particular Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#accessProjectItem)

Project Item Exists[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pr_item_exists "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_item_has_extended_type](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/pr_item_exists.png)

This block returns a [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 which is `true` if the given project item exists.

Project Item Has Type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#block_pr_item_has_extended_type "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_item_has_extended_type](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/block_pr_item_has_extended_type.png)

Checks if the given project item has the chosen type and returns `true` or `false`. You can choose one of the following types:

*   `TYPE_FOOTAGE` : an item representing a video or image file
*   `TYPE_FILE` : an item representing a file that is not a video or image file - like a text file, for example
*   `TYPE_ROOT` : the root bin of the project that contains all other project items
*   `TYPE_BIN` : a bin that is not the root bin
*   `TYPE_MERGED_CLIP` : a merged clip
*   `TYPE_SEQUENCE` : a sequence
*   `TYPE_MULTICAM_SEQUENCE` : a [multi-camera source sequence](https://helpx.adobe.com/premiere-pro/using/create-multi-camera-source-sequence.html)
    

If you want to retrieve the type of a project item as a text instead of checking for a particular type, you can use the [Get Attribute of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#pr_item_get_property)
 to retrieve the (extended) type.

For all the possibilities to specify a project item, see the section [Accessing a Particular Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#accessProjectItem)

Set Label Color of Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#block_pr_item_metadata_color_label_set "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_item_metadata_color_label_set](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/block_pr_item_metadata_color_label_set.png)

##### Deprecated

This block is deprecated. Use the [Set Attribute of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#pr_item_set_property)
 instead (with the attribute 'Color Label').

Change Media of Project Item[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#block_pr_item_change_media "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_item_change_media](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/block_pr_item_change_media.png)

With this block you can replace the footage file of a footage item (like when you right-click on a footage item and choose _replace footage..._).

##### Does not work for all items

Some items (like sequences or bins) have no media path that can be changed. To check if a project item supports this operation, use the [Get Attribute of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#pr_item_get_property)
 to read the _can change media path_ property.

Create Subclip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#pr_project_item_create_subclip "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_project_item_create_subclip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/pr_project_item_create_subclip.png)

Creates a subclip of the given project item.

#### subclip name or path (optional)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#subclip-name-or-path-optional "Direct link to heading")

If you provide a text like `my item name` for this parameter, the subclip gets this name. If you provide a full path like `path\to\my item name`, the subclip is named `my item name` and moved into the bin `path\to`. If this bin does not exist yet, it will also be created.

#### start time **and** end time (optional)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#start-time-and-end-time-optional "Direct link to heading")

If you don't specify a start or end time, the current in point or out point of the clip is used. For details on time values, see [Pr Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime)
.

#### hard boundaries[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#hard-boundaries "Direct link to heading")

This checkbox corresponds to the option "_Restrict Times to Subclip Boundaries_" in Premiere Pro's dialogs to create or change subclips.

#### take video **and** take audio[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#take-video-and-take-audio "Direct link to heading")

determines if the video and/or audio of the project item should be used for the subclip.

Get New Unique Project Item Path[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#block_pr_item_unique_name "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_item_unique_name](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProjectItems/block_pr_item_unique_name.png)

To avoid getting into trouble, it's always a good idea not to have two project items with exactly the same name in exactly the same bin. Therefore, if you want to create a new item with the name `my sequence`, for example, and in the same project bin there is already an item with the name `my sequence`, then it's better to name the new one `my sequence 2`. That's exactly what this block is doing:

![check if project item exists](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_pr_item_unique_name_example_2.png)

The text you plug into this block must be the path to a project item (like `my sequence` for an item in the root folder of the project or `my bin\my sequence` if the item is located in another bin of the project). The block will then return

*   exactly the same path, if no item with the same path yet exists
*   the same path with a number appended, if the item exists already.

This way you can be sure the block always returns an item path that does not yet exist in the project (although there could be existing items with the same name in a different bin).

### Checking if Project Item Exists[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems/#checking-if-project-item-exists "Direct link to heading")

![check if project item exists](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_pr_item_unique_name_example.png)

You can also use this block to test if a project item exists. This example returns `true` if a project item `image1.png` exists in the project bin `footage` and `false` if it does not exist (since the _Get Unique Project Item Path_ block returns something different than the original path itself if and only if it already exists).

# Text | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#)

On this page

Text
====

Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/text.png)

This block creates a simple text. If you need a text with multiple lines, use the [Multiline Text block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#text_multiline)
 instead.

### Text for Paths[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text-for-paths "Direct link to heading")

In Automation Blocks, texts are also used to [access project items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
 or [clips](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips#accessSequenceClip)
 by their path. You can also use it for File and Folder path but for those, Automation Blocks has dedicated [File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_file)
 and [Folder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_folder)
 blocks, which allow you to choose this type of path more conveniently.

Multiline Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text_multiline "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block text_multiline](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/text_multiline.png)

This block is identical to the [Text block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#text)
, except that it supports multiple lines.

Join Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text_join "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block text_join](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/text_join.png)

This block combines several texts into one. By default, it offers inputs for two texts, but you can add more by clicking on the blue cogwheel in the top left corner of the block. If you need to combine many texts, the [Text Pattern](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#string_pattern)
 might be more convenient to use. The following blocks do exactly the same - on the left with the Join Text block, and on the right with the Text Pattern block:

![Block text_join](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/text_join_example.png)

Text Pattern[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#string_pattern "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block string_pattern](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/string_pattern.png)

This block takes a template text and replaces any occurrence of the placeholder symbols `$1`, `$2`,... with the respective texts. The example above creates the text `Mr. Miller is 41 years old`.

The placeholders can occur in the template text in any order, and if any of them occurs multiple times, all occurrences are replaced.

To add more (or remove) placeholders, click on the blue cogwheel icon in the top left corner of the block.

This block is also very useful to create file paths. This example creates the text `path/to/myFile_001.jpg`, for example:

![Block example: creating file paths with pattern](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/string_pattern_example_files.png)

##### Multi-line texts

Of course, you can also use a [multi-line text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text_multiline)
 as your pattern.

Replace in Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#string_replace "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block string_replace](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/string_replace.png)

This block does a basic search & replace on the given text. It can either replace all occurrences, or only the first occurrence of the search word. For an example, see the tutorial [Search & Replace in Project Items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/searchReplaceProjectItems#search--replace-in-texts)

If you just want to search without replacing, you can use the [Find In Text block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#text_indexOf)
.

For All RegExp Matches[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#string_for_all_regex_matches "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block string_for_all_regex_matches](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/string_for_all_regex_matches.png)

This is an advanced block to search in a text with the help of regular expressions. If you don't know what regular expressions are, I recommend [this article](https://www.codemag.com/article/0305041/Getting-Started-With-Regular-Expressions)
 to get started. In a nutshell, regular expressions can be used to search for complex patterns and identify or extract parts of a string which match that pattern.

Regular expressions can be very useful to read structured data from a text file. For a more advanced example, see the script "Import Marker from Srt File".

For a simpler search, you can also use the [Find in Text block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#text_indexOf)
.

### Let ChatGPT help you![​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#let-chatgpt-help-you "Direct link to heading")

See our [tutorial about Batch-Renaming Project Items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-pr/projectItemsTools#batch-rename-project-items)
 to learn how to use ChatGPT to create regular expressions for you.

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#example "Direct link to heading")

![RegEx example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/string_for_all_regex_matches_example.png)

This script searches in the text `this file has the following format: mp4` for the expression `format: (.*)`, which essentially says "search for something that starts with `format:` followed by arbitrary characters (this is the `.*`). Since we have put `.*` in parentheses, this part of the result is stored in a capturing group.

The block now searches in the text and for each occurrence it finds, it executes the blocks you nest inside of it. The search result is stored in the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `match`. Match is actually a list, whose first element is the full match and the second element is the match of the first capturing group (and if you have a second capturing group in your regular expression, it is stored in the next element of the list, etc). In our example, the block finds exactly one match, and hence executes the nested blocks only once. The first list entry of `match` is `format: mp4` (the full match) and the second list entry is just `mp4` (the first capturing group). So, the script prints the following text to the console:

    found match 'format: mp4' with first capturing group 'mp4'

Text Length[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text_length "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block text_length](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/text_length.png)

This block returns the length of the given text, i.e. the number of characters including spaces, line break characters etc. For `abc` it returns 3 and for `Hello World` it returns 11.

Is Text Empty[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text_isEmpty "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block text_isEmpty](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/text_isEmpty.png)

Returns the [logic value](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/logic)
 `True` when the given text has a length of 0 and otherwise `False`.

Change Case of Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text_changeCase "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block text_changeCase](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/text_changeCase.png)

This block can change the case of a text to:

*   UPPER CASE
*   lower case
*   Title Case

Find In Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text_indexOf "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block text_indexOf](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/text_indexOf.png)

This block searches for the occurrence of a text inside another text. If it finds the text, it returns the position where it was found. If the hit already starts at the very first letter, it returns `1`, for example. If it does not find the word, it returns `0`.

##### 0 vs -1

If you are a programmer, you might be familiar with functions like `indexOf` in JavaScript, which also return the position of the match they found. JavaScript returns -1 when it doesn't find anything, since 0 is the first letter of the text in JavaScript. However, in Automation Blocks we count the letters starting with 1, and 0 means that nothing was found.

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#example-1 "Direct link to heading")

This example checks if the name of the active sequence contains `final` and then prints in the console either `sequence name contains the word "final"` or `sequence name does not contain the word "final"`:

![Block text_indexOf](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/text_indexOf_example.png)

Search in Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#string_search_mm "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block string_search_mm](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/string_search_mm.png)

This is an advanced version of the block [Find In Text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text_indexOf)
. It has the option to do a case-sensitive search, or even using a regular expression. Regular expressions allow you to search not only for a single text, but for complex patterns. If you don't know what regular expressions are, I recommend [this article](https://www.codemag.com/article/0305041/Getting-Started-With-Regular-Expressions)
 to get started.

If it finds the text, it returns the position where it was found. If the hit already starts at the very first letter, it returns 1, for example. If it does not find the word, it returns 0.

Get Letter[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text_charAt "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block text_charAt](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/text_charAt.png)

This block returns a single letter of the given text.

Get Substring[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text_getSubstring "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block text_getSubstring](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/text_getSubstring.png)

This block returns a part of the given text.

Trim Spaces[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text_trim "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block text_trim](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Text/text_trim.png)

This block removes spaces (and other whitespace like tabs) from the beginning and/or end of the given text. This is useful if you ask the user to enter some text with a [prompt](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#block_extendscript_prompt)
, for example, but want to ignore any space he might accidentally add to his input.

*   [Text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text)
    *   [Text for Paths](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text/#text-for-paths)

# Pr Sequence | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#)

On this page

Pr Sequence
===========

### Accessing a Particular Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#accessSequence "Direct link to heading")

By default, all blocks use the [active sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#block_pr_active_seqence)
, but you can also specify other sequences easily:

*   **Sequence Name or Path** Each sequence has a corresponding project item and Automation Blocks converts this type of project item to the corresponding sequence object whenever needed. So, in every block that expects a sequence as argument, you can also [provide the project item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
     of that sequence instead. Since project items can simply be specified by their name or path, you can do the same for any sequence, too.
    
*   **Iterating over all sequences** Since each sequence is a project item, you can use the [For Each Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#block_pr_for_all_items_in_bin)
     to iterate over all sequences of your project or inside a particular bin. Just make sure to set the filter checkboxes to only allow items of type sequence.
    
*   **Sequence from Clip** If you have a particular [sequence clip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequenceClips)
     and need to access the sequence that this clip is located in, you can just use the clip itself, instead of the sequence; whenever a block expects a sequence as argument but gets a clip instead, it converts it to the sequence that the clip is contained in.
    

Active Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#block_pr_active_seqence "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_active_seqence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequence/block_pr_active_seqence.png)

The currently active sequence. If there is no active sequence, the script stops with an error message.

Activate Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#block_pr_seqence_activate "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_seqence_activate](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequence/block_pr_seqence_activate.png)

Makes the given sequence the active sequence. This will also open the sequence in the Timeline panel.

For all the possible ways to specify a sequence, see [Accessing a Particular Sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#accessSequence)
.

New Sequence from Clips[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#block_pr_new_sequence_from_clips "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_new_sequence_from_clips](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequence/block_pr_new_sequence_from_clips.png)

Creates a new sequence and inserts all clips into it, which are given in the _Clips_ argument (as if you were dragging those clips onto the _new item_ icon at the bottom right of the Project Panel of Premiere Pro).

#### clips[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#clips "Direct link to heading")

As clips, you can either provide a single clip or a list of clips. If any of the provided clips is a bin, all clips in that bin (but not of any subbins) are used. In the example above, the _clips_ value `footage` means that all clips in the bin footage are inserted into the new bin (assuming that your project has a bin named _footage_).

#### sequence name (or path)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#sequence-name-or-path "Direct link to heading")

In the second argument you specify the name of the new sequence. You can also specify a full path to the project item of the sequence like `path\to\name`. In that case, the sequence name will be `name` and it will be placed in the project bin `path\to`. If the bin does not exist, the block creates it for you.

New Sequence from Preset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#block_pr_new_sequence "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_new_sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequence/block_pr_new_sequence.png)

Creates a new sequence with the settings specified in the chosen preset.

#### sequence presets[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#sequence-presets "Direct link to heading")

The sequence presets you can choose in the dropdown are identical to the presets you find in Premiere Pro's _New Sequence_ dialog.

#### using custom presets[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#using-custom-presets "Direct link to heading")

Instead of using a preset from the dropdown, you can also drag a [File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_file)
 or [Embedded File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_embedded_file)
 over the dropdown to use a custom preset file:

![use custom preset](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_pr_new_sequence_custom_preset.png)

The preset file must have the format _.sqpreset_; it contains all the settings of the sequence. You create a preset file in Premiere Pro as follows

1.  Go to the menu _File->New->Sequence..._
2.  In the dialog that opens, switch to the tab "_Settings_" (if you want to base your preset file on an existing preset, you can first choose one in the tab "_Sequence Presets_")
3.  Choose the settings you want for your sequence and click the **Save Preset...** button

![Save Sequence Preset](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/block_pr_new_sequence_save_preset.png)

After saving the preset, you find the `.sqpreset` file in your Documents folder at:

`~/Documents/Adobe/Premiere Pro/VERSION/Profile-NAME/Settings/Custom/`

The part `VERSION` and `NAME` depend on your Premiere Pro version and user. If you've created a preset with the name `myPreset` then the quickest way to find it is often to search with Windows Explorer or Mac OS Finder in the folder `~\Documents\Adobe` for a file with name `myPreset.sqpreset`.

#### sequence name (or path)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#sequence-name-or-path-1 "Direct link to heading")

In the second argument you specify the name of the new sequence. You can also specify a full path to the project item of the sequence like `path\to\name`. In that case, the sequence name will be `name` and it will be placed in the project bin `path\to`. If the bin does not exist, the block creates it for you.

##### unique sequence name

You should avoid having multiple sequences with the same name in the same bin of your project. If you are not sure if a sequence with the name you chose already exists, you can use the [Get New Unique Project Item Path block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#block_pr_item_unique_name)
 to make sure your sequence has a unique path.

New Multicam Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#new-multicam-sequence "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

There is no way to create a new multi-camera source sequence with Pr's scripting API (and hence also with Automation Blocks). But you can create an empty multicam sequence with offline clips using the desired settings manually and then save that in a template project. Then, whenever you want to create such a multicam sequence in your active project, you can simply import the sequence from your template project (see [Import Sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#pr_import_sequence)
 below).

Import Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#pr_import_sequence "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This tutorial shows how to import sequences (or multi-camera source sequences) from another project into the active project.

Add Project Items to Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#pr_sequence_add_clips "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_sequence_add_clips](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequence/pr_sequence_add_clips.png)

This block adds the given project items as clips into a sequence. You can either provide the name (or path) of a single project item or [a list](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/lists)
 of them. These will be inserted in the same oder as in the list. If an item is a project bin, all footage items inside that bin (but not sub-bins) are inserted.

From each footage item, only the range from the in to the out point is inserted into the sequence.

Clone Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#block_pr_sequence_clone "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_sequence_clone](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequence/block_pr_sequence_clone.png)

This block creates a copy of the given sequence. The footage items it uses are not duplicated, but you can choose to also duplicate any nested sequences.

#### sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#sequence "Direct link to heading")

By default, this block clones the active sequence. For all the possible ways to specify a sequence, see [Accessing a Particular Sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#accessSequence)
.

#### new name (or path)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#new-name-or-path "Direct link to heading")

In the second argument, you specify the name of the new sequence. You can also specify a full path to the project item of the sequence like `path\to\name`. In this case, the sequence name will be `name` and it will be placed in the project bin `path\to`. If the bin does not exist, the block creates it for you.

#### clone nested sequences[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#clone-nested-sequences "Direct link to heading")

If you enable this and the sequence contains any nested sequences, then these nested sequences will be duplicated too (as well as any sequences nested into these nested sequences, etc). If you specified a path for the new sequence, the cloned nested sequences are placed in that same bin, too.

Get Time Value of Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#pr_sequence_get_time "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_sequence_get_time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequence/pr_sequence_get_time.png)

This block is deprecated. Please use the [Get Attribute of Sequence block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#pr_sequence_get_property)
, which now covers all time-related attributes as well.

Get Attribute of Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#pr_sequence_get_property "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_sequence_get_property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequence/pr_sequence_get_property.png)

This block returns one of the attributes of the given sequence listed below, including both time-related and technical properties. Time values in Automation Blocks are always given as point numbers in seconds. To convert them to other timecode formats, see the blocks in [Pr Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime)
. By default, this block uses the active sequence. For all the possible ways to specify a sequence, see [Accessing a Particular Sequence](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#accessSequence)
.

##### Legacy name

This block was named **Get Property of Sequence** in earlier versions of Automation Blocks.

### audio channel count[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#audio-channel-count "Direct link to heading")

The number of audio channels in the sequence.

### audio channel type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#audio-channel-type "Direct link to heading")

Audio channel type in use. One of the following texts

*   `AUDIOCHANNELTYPE_Mono`
*   `AUDIOCHANNELTYPE_Stereo`
*   `AUDIOCHANNELTYPE_51`
*   `AUDIOCHANNELTYPE_Multichannel`
*   `AUDIOCHANNELTYPE_4Channel`
*   `AUDIOCHANNELTYPE_8Channel`

### audio display format[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#audio-display-format "Direct link to heading")

Audio timecode display format. One of the following texts:

*   `TIMEDISPLAY_24Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_25Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_2997DropTimecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_2997NonDropTimecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_30Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_50Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_5994DropTimecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_5994NonDropTimecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_60Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_Frames`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_23976Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_16mmFeetFrames`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_35mmFeetFrames`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_48Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_AudioSamplesTimecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_AudioMsTimecode`

##### Convert time to timecode

You can convert between time values and any of these timecode formats using the blocks in the section [Pr Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime)

### audio sample rate[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#audio-sample-rate "Direct link to heading")

The audio sample rate of the sequence, as a number.

### frame duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#frame-duration "Direct link to heading")

The duration of a single frame in seconds (this is the multiplicative inverse of the framerate).

### frame height[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#frame-height "Direct link to heading")

the height of the frame in pixels (for full resolution, not for preview)

### frame rate[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#frame-rate "Direct link to heading")

The framerate of the clip (i.e. the number of frames per second).

### frame size[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#frame-size "Direct link to heading")

a text like `1920 x 1080` which represents the frame width and height

### frame width[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#frame-width "Direct link to heading")

the width of the frame in pixels (for full resolution, not for preview)

### in point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#in-point "Direct link to heading")

The in point of the sequence (in seconds).

### name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#name "Direct link to heading")

the name of the sequence

### out point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#out-point "Direct link to heading")

The out point of the sequence (in seconds).

### playhead position[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#playhead-position "Direct link to heading")

The position of the current time indicator (in seconds).

### preview codec[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#preview-codec "Direct link to heading")

Four character code of the preview codec in use.

### preview file format[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#preview-file-format "Direct link to heading")

path to the output preset (.epr file) being used for preview file rendering.

##### Rendering

You can render clips using that output preset file with the [Add to Media Encoder Queue Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prRendering#block_media_encoder_render)

### preview frame height[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#preview-frame-height "Direct link to heading")

the height of the frame in pixels (for preview, not full resolution)

### preview frame size[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#preview-frame-size "Direct link to heading")

a text like `1920 x 1080` which represents the frame width and height (for preview, not full resolution)

### preview frame width[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#preview-frame-width "Direct link to heading")

the width of the frame in pixels (for preview, not full resolution)

### sequence duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#sequence-duration "Direct link to heading")

The total duration of the sequence (in seconds).

### sequence ID[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#sequence-id "Direct link to heading")

The unique identifier assigned to this sequence, at the time of its creation, in the form `xxxxxxx-xxxx-xxxx-xxxx-xxxxxxxxxxxx`

### video display format[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#video-display-format "Direct link to heading")

Video timecode display format. One of the following texts:

*   `TIMEDISPLAY_24Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_25Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_2997DropTimecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_2997NonDropTimecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_30Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_50Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_5994DropTimecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_5994NonDropTimecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_60Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_Frames`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_23976Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_16mmFeetFrames`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_35mmFeetFrames`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_48Timecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_AudioSamplesTimecode`
*   `TIMEDISPLAY_AudioMsTimecode`

##### Convert time to timecode

You can convert between time values and any of these timecode formats using the blocks in the section [Pr Time](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prTime)

### video field type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#video-field-type "Direct link to heading")

Video field type in use in the sequence. One of these texts:

*   `FIELDTYPE_DEFAULT`
*   `FIELDTYPE_PROGRESSIVE`
*   `ALPHACHANNEL_UPPERFIRST`
*   `ALPHACHANNEL_LOWERFIRST`

### video pixel aspect ratio[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#video-pixel-aspect-ratio "Direct link to heading")

The pixel aspect ratio, as a ratio, as a text. Like `1:1`, for example.

##### Getting the pixel aspect ratio as a number

To get the pixel aspect ratio of the sequence as a single number instead of a text, use the [Get Attribute of Project Item Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#pr_item_get_property)
 to retrieve the _pixel aspect ratio_. The block for project items returns it as a single number, and since sequences are also project items, it works for those, too.

### work area (enabled / in point / out point)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#work-area-enabled--in-point--out-point "Direct link to heading")

These three properties show/hide the workarea and change its in and out point. Note that the workarea is not the same as the in out range of the sequence:

![in out of sequence vs work area in out point](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_sequence_set_property_in_out_workarea.png)

##### API Bug

Due to a bug in the Premiere Pro scripting API (#DVAPR-4239719), the functions to manipulate the workarea do not work reliably. Most notably, the in and out point can only be set via the API after they have been changed manually once. Apparently a sequence can have two states regarding the workarea: manual state (user or Extend script can set the workarea) or auto-state, where the workarea keeps adapting to the length of the last clip (default state). But we couldn't find a way to swich from auto-state to manual state by code, other then a user manually dragging before executing code.

We still keep the functions here in Automation Blocks, in case they are still useful, despite the current limitations.

See also [this forum discussion](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro-discussions/code-sample-and-video-on-how-to-setworkarea-in-a-sequence/m-p/14503238#M494863)
.

### zero point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#zero-point "Direct link to heading")

Usually a sequence starts with the time 0s and then the zero point is also 0. But the beginning of the sequence can be assigned any other time - and this time is called the zero point. Note that the zero point is only used for displaying the timecode in the user interface. Internally, in Premiere Pro (and also in Automation Blocks), all in points, out points, playhead positions etc. are treated as if the sequence time starts at 0 and the zero point has no influence on them.

Set Property of Sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#pr_sequence_set_property "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_sequence_set_property](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequence/pr_sequence_set_property.png)

This block sets one of the attributes of the sequence. For a detailed description of all available attributes (including the time-related ones), see the [Get Attribute of Sequence block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#pr_sequence_get_property)
.

Sequence Preset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence/#pr_sequence_preset_file "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_sequence_preset_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrSequence/pr_sequence_preset_file.png)

This block shows a list of the available sequence presets and returns the corresponding `.sqpreset` file. Usually, this block is only used as part of the [New Sequence from Preset block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prSequence#block_pr_new_sequence)
, but you can also use it as a standalone block. This is useful, for example, if you want to create multiple sequences with the same preset. In this example, we create two sequences and both use the preset you choose in the first line:

![multiple sequences with same preset](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_sequence_preset_file_example.png)
