# Pr Project | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#)

On this page

Pr Project
==========

These blocks allow you to work with Premiere Pro projects. Open, save and close projects, import footage into a project, iterate over project items, and more.

### Accessing a Particular Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#accessProject "Direct link to heading")

Usually, you do all your actions on the [active project](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#pr_project_active_project)
. However, Premiere Pro supports having multiple projects open at the same time, and Automation Blocks can also perform operations on the other currently open projects. Instead of `active project`, you can use one of the following methods to explicitly use another project (note that this only works with projects that are already open in Pr):

*   use a text block with the name of the project: ![myProject.prproj](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/project_text.png)
    
*   use a block that represents a project item, sequence, or clip within a sequence. If these are used in a context where a project is expected, they will be converted to the project that contains the project item, sequence or clip.
    
*   you can iterate over all open projects with the [For All Open Projects block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#block_pr_for_all_open_projects)
    , to do something with each of them.
    

Active Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#pr_project_active_project "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_project_active_project](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/pr_project_active_project.png)

Represents the currently active project (this is used in other blocks that do something with a particular project - like opening or saving it).

Create Project Bin[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#pr_project_item_create_bin "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_project_item_create_bin](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/pr_project_item_create_bin.png)

This block creates a new bin in the project. In the example above, the bin with name `subbin` is created inside the bin with name `bin`. If `bin` does not exist yet, it is also created.

![Block pr_project_item_create_bin](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_project_item_create_bin_example.png)

This example also creates the bin `subbin` inside the bin `bin`, but this time, if `bin` does not exist yet, the script stops with an error. Note that this time it will also search for a bin with the name `bin` inside all sub-bins of the project if it does not find it in the root bin (see [Accessing a Particular Project Item](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProjectItems#accessProjectItem)
 for details of all possibilities to specify a parent bin).

Import File(s)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#block_pr_project_import_file "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_project_import_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/block_pr_project_import_file.png)

Imports a single file or a list of files into the given project bin. If you import a lot of files, it is best to put them all into a list and then import them with a single call of this block. This is much faster than importing each file separately.

If you import images, you can

*   **import as normal footage**: if there is an image in your list of files to import, it just imports that single image
*   **import as image sequence**: if there is an image in your list of files to import, it assumes that it is the first image of an image sequence and imports the entire sequence as a single project item.

##### Syncing folders

If you want to import all the files in a particular folder, the [Sync Folder with Project Bin Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#block_pr_sync_project_bin_with_folder)
 is the most powerful and convenient choice.

Import Ae Comps[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#pr_project_import_ae_comps "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_project_import_ae_comps](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/pr_project_import_ae_comps.png)

This block imports the After Effects compositions from the given After Effects project file (.aep file) into the given project bin.

#### Import Comps By Name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#import-comps-by-name "Direct link to heading")

If you choose `comps by name` instead of `all comps`, you can provide a list of the names of the comps you want to import (or also just a single name). The following example imports comps with names `first comp name`, `second comp name`and `third comp name`:

![Import Comps Example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_project_import_ae_comps_example.png)

##### Import in a single call

If you want to import a lot of comps, it's better to put them in a list and import them all at once. This will be faster than calling the block several times with different comp names.

Sync Folder with Project Bin[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#block_pr_sync_project_bin_with_folder "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_sync_project_bin_with_folder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/block_pr_sync_project_bin_with_folder.png)

This block imports all files from the chosen folder into the given bin of your Premiere Pro Project. Even better: it checks if some of these files have already been imported into that bin before and if so, it does not import them a second time. So whenever you add some new files to the folder, you can run the block again to import only the new files.

If the bin does not exist in your project yet, it will be created.

#### subfolders[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#subfolders "Direct link to heading")

Here you have three options:

*   **include**: imports all footage from the folder and all sub-folders (and creates corresponding subbins in the project as needed)
*   **exclude**: imports only the footage in the folder, but no footage from sub-folders
*   **flatten**: imports all footage from the folder and all sub-folders but does not create subbins in the project. Instead, all project items are placed directly in the given bin. If you have footage files with identical names in different sub-folders, this may result in several project items with the exact same name (in the exact same bin).

#### image sequence[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#image-sequence "Direct link to heading")

If you enable this option, the block detects image sequences in the folder and imports them as a single video file. Other images that are not part of an image sequence are still imported as a single image file (and not as a sequence with just a single frame). If you disable this option, all images are imported as a separate project item.

#### delete other items[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#delete-other-items "Direct link to heading")

With this option, all project items that have no corresponding file in the folder anymore are deleted from the project. This is useful if you deleted files in the folder and want to get rid of the corresponding project items. But be careful: This also deletes sequences and other project items in the bin which have no file associated with them.

#### suppress warning dialogs[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#suppress-warning-dialogs "Direct link to heading")

Enable this only in fully automated scenarios where you want to avoid any user interaction.

##### Don't Sync Root Bin

It is good practice to always sync with a subbin of your project and not the root bin `\`: Since your project also contains other items like sequences (which have no corresponding file on your hard-drive), these would be lost if you sync with the _delete other items_ option enabled.

Project Name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#block_pr_project_name "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_project_name](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/block_pr_project_name.png)

This block returns the filename of the given project. It returns either the full name like `myProject.prproj` or, if you choose the option **without extension**, the name without the file suffix, i.e. just `myProject`.

By default, the block uses the active project. See [Accessing a Particular Project](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#accessProject)
 for how to use another currently open project instead.

Project File[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#block_pr_project_file "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_project_file](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/block_pr_project_file.png)

This block returns the project file of the given project. Since in Automation Blocks files are simply represented by the full file path on your hard-drive, the result is a text containing that path.

By default, the block uses the active project. See [Accessing a Particular Project](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#accessProject)
 for how to use another currently open project instead.

Project Folder[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#block_pr_project_folder "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_project_folder](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/block_pr_project_folder.png)

This block returns the full path of the folder on your hard-drive, where the given project file is located. This is the same as the [Project File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#block_pr_project_file)
 but without the [Project Name](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#block_pr_project_name)
 at the end.

By default, the block uses the active project. See [Accessing a Particular Project](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#accessProject)
 for how to use another currently open project instead.

New Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#pr_project_new "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_project_open](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/pr_project_new.png)

Creates a new Premiere Pro project file - please provide the path of the project file as argument. The block will give an error if the file path has a different file extension than ".prproj". If the path has no file extension at all, the extension ".prproj" will be added.

#### allow to overwrite if file exists[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#allow-to-overwrite-if-file-exists "Direct link to heading")

If this option is enabled, the block deletes the project file before creating a new one. If the option is disabled, the block gives an error if the project file already exists.

#### create folder if not existing[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#create-folder-if-not-existing "Direct link to heading")

If this option is enabled, the block creates the folder on your hard-drive where the project should be located (and its parent folders) if needed. If the option is disabled, the block gives an error if the folder does not exist yet.

See [Accessing a Particular File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
 for all the ways to specify a file.

Open Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#pr_project_open "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_project_open](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/pr_project_open.png)

Opens the given Premiere Pro project file (must be a .prproj) file.

See [Accessing a Particular File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
 for all the ways to specify a file.

Save Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#pr_project_save "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_project_save](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/pr_project_save.png)

Saves the given project.

By default, the block uses the active project. See [Accessing a Particular Project](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#accessProject)
 for how to use another currently open project instead.

Save Project As[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#pr_project_save_as "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_project_save_as](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/pr_project_save_as.png)

Saves the given project to a new file.

#### allow to overwrite[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#allow-to-overwrite "Direct link to heading")

If this option is **not** enabled, the script stops with an error if the project file already exists.

#### create folder if not existing[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#create-folder-if-not-existing-1 "Direct link to heading")

With this option, you can save the project to a new folder that does not exist yet. The block will create the folder where the project file is located (and any parent folder thereof) if needed, before actually saving the project.

See [Accessing a Particular File](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#accessFile)
 for all the ways to specify a project file.

By default, the block uses the active project. See [Accessing a Particular Project](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#accessProject)
 for how to use another currently open project instead.

Close Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#pr_project_close "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_project_close](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/pr_project_close.png)

This block closes the given project.

By default, the block uses the active project. See [Accessing a Particular Project](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject#accessProject)
 for how to use another currently open project instead.

For Each Open Project[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#block_pr_for_all_open_projects "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_pr_for_all_open_projects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/block_pr_for_all_open_projects.png)

This block iterates over all projects that are currently open in Premiere Pro. The blocks you nest inside this block are executed once for each open project. During the execution of these blocks, the project itself is saved in the [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
 `my project`.

For an example of how to use this block, see the script **save and close all projects except active one**.

Set Scratch Disk Path[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#pr_project_set_scratch_disk_path "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block pr_project_set_scratch_disk_path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/PrProject/pr_project_set_scratch_disk_path.png)

Allows you to set the scratch disk paths for the project, which you would manually change with the Premiere Pro menu command `File->Project Settings->Scratch Disks...` in this dialog:

![Scratch Disks Dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/pr_project_set_scratch_disk_path_example.png)

The available scratch disks you can set are

*   Captured video
*   Captured audio
*   Video Previews
*   Audio Previews
*   Project Auto Save
*   CC Libraries Downloads
*   Motion Graphics Template Media

*   [Accessing a Particular Project](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#accessProject)
    
*   [Active Project](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/prProject/#pr_project_active_project)

# Variables | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables/#)

On this page

Variables
=========

Overview[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables/#overview "Direct link to heading")

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Variables allow you to store values and reuse them in later blocks of your script. They are also an essential ingredient for loops.

Here is an example:

![Block variables_set](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/variables_introduction_example.png)

In the first line, the script gives the variable `name` the value `my awesome project`. In the next lines, it first creates a new project with project file `~/Desktop/my awesome project/my awesome project.prproj` and then creates in that project a sequence with the name `awesome project`.

The great benefit of using variables is that when you want to run the script with another project name, you just need to replace `my awesome project` in the very first line. Once you've done that, any block using the variable `name` will use the new value you assigned to the variable.

For a more detailed explanation of variables, see the tutorial [Search & Replace in Project Items](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tutorials-pr/searchReplaceProjectItems#variables)
.

Set Variable[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables/#variables_set "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block variables_set](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Variables/variables_set.png)

With this block, you can assign a value to a variable. All variables of your current script are listed in the dropdown; just choose the one you want and set its value. The value can be anything: a number, text, logic value, list and even any more complex data like a sequence, markers or clip parameter, for example.

Often, you set variable values at the very beginning of your script. But you can also set variable values at any later point, and multiple times (to overwrite the previous value). Loops set the variables they provide themselves, so for loop variables you only need the [Get Variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables/#variables_get)
 block and never the Set Variable block.

Change Variable[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables/#math_change "Direct link to heading")

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block math_change](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Variables/math_change.png)

This block is only useful for variables whose value is a number. It allows you to increase the value of a variable (or decrease it, if you give a negative number). The same can also be done with the [Set Variable block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables/#variables_set)
, but with the Change Variable block the code is a bit more compact.

Both these blocks increase the value of the variable `my number` by 2:

![Block variables_set](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/math_change_example_1.png)

Get Variable[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables/#variables_get "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block variables_get](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/Variables/variables_get.png)

The round purple block, which just consists of the variable name, returns the value of the variable. If you execute this block before you've set the value of the variable, it will return `undefined` (unless it is a loop variable, in which case the loop itself takes care of setting the variable value before each loop iteration).

Also note that in the dropdown, you cannot just pick any variable of your script, but you also have the option to delete or rename a variable. These operations affect not just this block, but all occurrences of the variable in your script. So, if you rename the variable, for example, the name is updated throughout your entire script.

*   [Overview](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables/#overview)
    
*   [Set Variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables/#variables_set)
    
*   [Change Variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables/#math_change)
    
*   [Get Variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables/#variables_get)

---

# Overview | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/overviewToolsAe/#)

Overview
========

To give you an idea of what Automation Blocks is capable of, here are some examples of tools that are included in the library.

[![Comp Duplicates From Spreadsheet](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolTeaserCompSpreadsheet.png)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/compDuplicatesFromSpreadsheet)
[### Comp Duplicates From Spreadsheet](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/compDuplicatesFromSpreadsheet)

Batch replace text, footage, colors and other properties based on a spreadsheet.

[![Color Palette Workflow](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolTeaserColorPalette.png)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes)
[### Color Palette Workflow](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/colorPalettes)

Manage the colors of your project to a central color palette and dynamically link all color properties to it.

[![Comp Time Tools](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolTeaserInsertFrames.png)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/compTimeTools)
[### Comp Time Tools](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/compTimeTools)

Insert frames - in the middle of a comp, for example. This tool will shift layers, keyframes and markers as needed and change the duration of a comp & precomps.

[![Expressions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolTeaserSearchReplaceExpressions.png)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions)
[### Expressions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/expressions)

Tools to search & replace in Expressions, globally enable or disable expressions, and more.

[![Guides](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolTeaserGuides.png)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides)
[### Guides](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/guides)

Create guides around selected layers, save your favourite guide setups as presets, and more.

[![Keyframes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolTeaserKeyframeLibrary.png)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/keyframes)
[### Keyframes](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/keyframes)

Save frequently used keyframe combinations in a library and create keyframe presets that automatically adjust to existing keys.

[![Precompose On Steroids](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolTeaserPrecompose.png)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/precomposeScripts)
[### Precompose On Steroids](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/precomposeScripts)

Precompose & crop to content size, move layers into and out of precomps, and more.

[![Project Cleanup](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolTeaserProjectCleanup.png)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/projectCleanup)
[### Project Cleanup](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/projectCleanup)

Move project items into bins based on their file type, search & replace in project items, and more.

[![Randomize](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolTeaserRandomness.png)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize)
[### Randomize](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/randomize)

Distribute layers randomly or randomize any layer properties in no time.

[![Rigs & Presets](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolTeaserRigs.png)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets)
[### Rigs & Presets](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/rigsAndPresets)

Auto resizing text boxes which live on the text layers themselves (no extra layers needed), a rig to time-remap keyframes, and more.

[![Text From/To Spreadsheet](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolTeaserTextSpreadsheet.png)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/textFromToSpreadsheet)
[### Text From/To Spreadsheet](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/textFromToSpreadsheet)

Exports or replaces text based on a spreadsheet

[![Sync Folders](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/overview/toolTeaserSyncFolders.png)](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders)
[### Sync Folders](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/tools-ae/syncedFolders)

Sync system directories with bins of your After Effects project easily

# User Interface & Comments | Automation Blocks

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#)

On this page

User Interface & Comments
=========================

These blocks allow you to create user interfaces, like showing a message or asking the user to enter a value or confirm an action. There are also some blocks that help you to document your code and link to external resources.

Input[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#input_arg "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block input_arg](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/UserInterfaceAndComments/input_arg.png)

With this block you can create all kinds of user interface elements in the inputs panel of Automation Blocks. Whenever you add an input block to your script, a corresponding input shows up in the inputs panel. When you run a tool, the values are retrieved from the inputs panel and inserted in the block code.

These two blocks behave exactly identical, for example, except that the actual text of the green [Text block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text#text)
 can only be changed in the block editor, whereas the text of the red _Input_ block can be changed in the inputs panel:

![Text Input vs Text Block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/input_arg_example_string_constant.png)

When you export a tool as jsx script, the values of the panel at the time of the export are used. If you want a jsx script to show a user interface when it is launched, you need to use a [modal input dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#modal_input_dialog)
 instead.

This example shows all available UI elements and the panel elements, which they create:

![List of all inputs which can be created](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/input_arg_example_all.png)

### File & Folder Inputs[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#file--folder-inputs "Direct link to heading")

The input types _open file_, _save file_ and _choose folder_ are very similar, since they all offer an auto-complete text field to enter a file or folder path. However, there are subtle differences:

*   **open file** allows the user to choose an existing file
*   **save file** allows the user to choose a file (either an existing one or one that does not exist, yet)
*   **choose folder** allows the user to choose a folder instead of a file

### Binding Inputs to Variables[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#binding-inputs-to-variables "Direct link to heading")

If you need to use the value of an input multiple times in your script, you cannot duplicate the input block, since this would also duplicate the user interface in the inputs panel. Instead, we recommend to bind inputs to [variables](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
. Here we create a file input and store the file path which the user entered in the variable `my file path`:

![binding inputs to variables](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/input_and_variables_example.png)

Modal Input Dialog[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#modal_input_dialog "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block modal_input_dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/UserInterfaceAndComments/modal_input_dialog.png)

Yes, this block might looks scary at first sight, but it is very easy to use and allows you to create a custom user interface for your block script! The user interface elements are very similar to the ones of the [Input](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#input_arg)
 block, but they are shown in a popup dialog instead of the inputs panel.

By default, the block shows a user interface which demonstrates all the available elements:

![Block modal_input_dialog](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/modal_input_dialog_ui.png)

### Choosing UI Elements[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#choosing-ui-elements "Direct link to heading")

As you can see, each row of the block creates one input of the final dialog. To remove, add or reorder the inputs, click on the blue cogwheel icon in the top left corner of the block.

Here I removed all except the first three inputs, and have set their type to single line text, checkbox and dropdown:

![Ae Scripting Creating a User Interface](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/modal_input_dialog_example_1.png)

### Initialize Values[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#initialize-values "Direct link to heading")

Note that the text input and the dropdown show the value `undefined` when the dialog opens. To change this, we need to understand that each input is connected to a [variable](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables)
, namely `user input 1`, `user input 2`, etc. The block shows as default value the value that the respective variable had before the block is executed, and after you entered the values and closed the dialog, it will set the variables to the values you entered.

Hence, to change the default values we need to [set the variables](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/variables#variables_set)
 to the values we want, before the block that creates the user interface:

![Ae Scripting Creating a User Interface](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/modal_input_dialog_example_2.png)

##### Persistant Values

Often it is convenient to remember the values that a user entered and use those as the default the next time the dialog is opened. To achieve this, you can [save the values in the settings](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/aeProject#ae_settings_save)

### Dropdown Lists[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#dropdown-lists "Direct link to heading")

Note that we set `user input 3` to `first options|second one|and third`. That's how you specify the options of a dropdown: Just use a single [text](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/text)
 where you separate the different options with a | symbol

If you want to access the values the user entered, you'll find them again in the variables `user input 1` etc. If the user chose "second one" in the dropdown, for example, the variable `user input 3` will have the value `second one` after the Modal Input Dialog block was executed.

### Dropdown Lists with Color[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#dropdown-lists-with-color "Direct link to heading")

If your dropdown choices are hex codes like `#ffffff` or label color names like `Sea Foam`, Automation Blocks detects this automatically and shows the color next to the text. To create the dialog below, we initialized the variable of the dropdown with `#23341e|#485e38|#f3971b|#6c6c6c`:

![Ae Scripting Dropdown List with Color](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/modal_input_dialog_example_color.png)

##### Rename variables

In the dropdowns to the right end of each row of the block (the ones showing "user input 1" etc.), you can choose the variable that should be used by this input; you'll also find an option to rename the variable. Giving your variables meaningful names can make your scripts much easier to read.

Write to Console[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#block_extendscript_console_log "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_console_log](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/UserInterfaceAndComments/block_extendscript_console_log.png)

This block prints the given message in Automation Block's console panel. This is useful

*   to inform the user about progress
*   to debug the output
*   to output data (you can cut & paste text from the console to use it somewhere else), but for this you can also [write text files](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/filesText)
    

Alert Dialog[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#block_extendscript_alert "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_alert](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/UserInterfaceAndComments/block_extendscript_alert.png)

This block shows the given message in a popup dialog.

![Block block_extendscript_alert](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/alert_example.png)

**Message vs Error**

You have the option to alert using a message or an error. The only difference is that in the latter case the script stops with an error after the message has been shown.

##### Alternative: Write to Console Block

If you want to show a lot of messages, it's better to use [Log to Console](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments#block_extendscript_console_log)
, which does not require any user interaction to close the message popup.

Confirm Dialog[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#block_type_extendscript_confirm "Direct link to heading")

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_type_extendscript_confirm](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/UserInterfaceAndComments/block_type_extendscript_confirm.png)

This block shows a popup window with a confirmation message, which you can answer with 'yes' or 'no'.

![Block block_extendscript_alert](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/confirm_example.png)

The block returns a logic value `true` or `false` (depending on if you answer 'yes' or 'no'), which you can use in [if statements](https://github.com/google/blockly/wiki/IfElse)
 and other logic operations.

Save File Dialog[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#block_extendscript_save_file_dlg "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_save_file_dlg](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/UserInterfaceAndComments/block_extendscript_save_file_dlg.png)

Shows a "Save File" dialog and returns the chosen file.

If you put a [File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_file)
 into the `preselected file` parameter, the dialog will choose this file by default, but still allow the user to choose another.

##### Hard-coded files

If you want to hard-code a particular file instead of allowing the user to choose one, you can use the [File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_file)
 instead.

Open File Dialog[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#block_extendscript_open_file_dlg "Direct link to heading")

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_open_file_dlg](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/UserInterfaceAndComments/block_extendscript_open_file_dlg.png)

Shows an "Open File" dialog and returns the chosen file.

In contrast to the Save File dialog, in the Open File dialog the user can only choose existing files.

If you put a [File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_file)
 into the `preselected file` parameter, the dialog will choose this file by default, but still allow the user to choose another one.

##### Hard-coded files

If you want to hard-code a particular file instead of allowing the user to choose one, you can use the [File block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_file)
 instead.

Open Folder Dialog[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#block_extendscript_open_folder_dlg "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_open_folder_dlg](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/UserInterfaceAndComments/block_extendscript_open_folder_dlg.png)

Shows an "Open Folder" dialog and returns the chosen folder.

If you put a [Folder block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_folder)
 into the `preselected folder` parameter, the dialog will choose this folder by default, but still allow the user to choose another one.

##### Hard-coded folder

If you want to hard-code a particular folder instead of allowing the user to choose one, you can use the [Folder block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_folder)
 instead.

#### Bug in Premiere Pro 24.2[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#bug-in-premiere-pro-242 "Direct link to heading")

##### Bug in PR2024

In Premiere Pro 2024 versions 24.2 and probably also 24.3 there is a [bug which causes the select dialog to fail](https://community.adobe.com/t5/premiere-pro-discussions/open-folder-dialog-doesn-t-works-in-pr-2024/m-p/14503742#M494898)
. If your Pr version still has this bug, this block will not show a dialog and returns `null` immediately.

Workarounds:

1.  use a newer Pr version (the current beta or any release newer than Pr 24.3 already contains the fix) or
2.  hard-code a folder (using a [folder block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/files#block_extendscript_folder)
    )

Prompt Dialog[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#block_extendscript_prompt "Direct link to heading")

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block block_extendscript_prompt](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/UserInterfaceAndComments/block_extendscript_prompt.png)

Shows a prompt dialog box where the user can enter some text.

![Block block_extendscript_alert](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/prompt_example.png)

You can use this block instead of a simple text block whenever you want the user to enter a custom text instead of using a hard-coded one.

Comment - Open Web Page[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#es_tutorial "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block es_tutorial](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/UserInterfaceAndComments/es_tutorial.png)

Allows you to embed a link to a website (like a documentation or tutorial). If you click the play button next to the _open web page_ text, the given url opens in the default web browser. This block has no effect on the Automation Blocks script at all and is just for documentation purposes.

### Help Button[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#help-button "Direct link to heading")

If you click on the Help button of the Automation Blocks library panel, it will always open the link of the first Open Web Page block of the currently selected block script. If the script contains no Open Web Page block, the Help button is disabled.

![Adding Help Link to Automation Blocks tools](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockOther/es_tutorial_block_help_button.png)

Comment - Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/block-reference/userInterfaceAndComments/#mm_multiline_comment_block "Direct link to heading")

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![Block mm_multiline_comment_block](https://docs.mamoworld.com/automation-blocks/img/blockScreenshots/UserInterfaceAndComments/mm_multiline_comment_block.png)

Use this block to add comments to your script. This block has no effect on the Automation Blocks script at all and is just for documentation purposes.
